Mesa — starters and five more

Read-only evaluation. The first five alternatives add a Type absent from the starter set first, then sort alphabetically; this is a preview, not a stored rank. WSRS-TSO will use actual curated Type coverage. No resource is marked Curated here. Items needing resolution stay in the administrator list.

Housing

Ten complementary usable starters spanning distinct pathways: statewide navigation (211), the single-adult and family coordinated-entry front doors, Mesa's shelter for families/women/seniors, Mesa's men's shelter, Mesa's rent/utility crisis aid, the rental-subsidy rehousing route, the East Valley DV shelter, the 12-17 youth crisis line, and the veterans' walk-in access point. The set deliberately mixes service types - navigation, entry/assessment, shelter, financial aid, rehousing, safety, youth, veterans - rather than several variants of one service, and each limitation is stated so missionaries can set expectations before calling. All needs-resolution records (Helaman House, SAFEDVS, the tracker, UMOM youth crisis/ILP, NREC, Dismas, the Resource Village, Mesa I-HELP) remain outside the starter set with administrator-focused reasons, and reserve usable resources (deposit help, the Mesa Resource Line, second DV shelters, Vista Colina, HUD-VASH, AHP/H2O, DDD lanes, additional shelter capacity) carry considerations written against this actual set. No tiers or reserve ranks are introduced.

Starter set

211 Arizona Helpline (2-1-1)

Statewide one-number front door: live specialists 9 a.m.-7 p.m. daily, a Housing Crisis Hotline, and shelter/housing/rent/utility referrals with Spanish-language support.

Referral-only - it provides no beds or funds and may not know same-day shelter availability.

Brian Garcia Welcome Center – single adult coordinated entry (Keys to Change)

The single-adult coordinated entry front door - 24/7 walk-in assessment, diversion and shelter/housing referrals for adults across Maricopa County.

Central Phoenix walk-in; no placement guarantee; phone variants in older listings (use 602-883-4509 / 602-229-5155); Spanish-language service not confirmed.

Family Housing Hub — family coordinated entry and shelter referrals (UMOM New Day Centers)

The countywide family coordinated entry and shelter waitlist - the front door for families with children seeking shelter and housing programs.

Long waits (200+ families, about three months reported) and inconsistent posted hours - call before visiting.

City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Off the Streets emergency shelter (Windermere Hotel site)

Mesa's main emergency shelter for families, women, seniors and DV survivors - hotel-based, 65 rooms/111 beds, with meals, 24/7 staffing, case management and housing plans.

Referral-only (no walk-ups) via 480-644-HOPE; about a six-week wait reported and the operator transition to Mercy House - confirm current status.

A New Leaf · East Valley Men's Center (EVMC)

Mesa's 104-bed men's emergency shelter with a defined intake path (online pre-screen plus the (877) 211-8661 placement hotline).

No phone intakes; beds via waitlist and availability can change daily; children under 18 are not allowed on site.

A New Leaf · MesaCAN Rent and Utility Assistance

Mesa's rent/utility crisis window with a Spanish application path, documented criteria and a clear document list.

Mesa residents only; first-come funding with narrow appointment windows (first-Thursday rent line, limited slots).

A New Leaf · Rapid ReHousing (rental subsidy and case management)

The rental-subsidy rehousing route (up to 12 months plus move-in help and case management) that shows clients what coordinated entry can lead to beyond shelter.

No direct applications - entry only through coordinated entry (211); funding and timing vary.

A New Leaf · Autumn House Domestic Violence Shelter (East Valley/Mesa)

The East Valley's local DV shelter - 22 beds, any gender, pet-friendly, screened through A New Leaf's 24-hour hotline.

Confidential location; placement not guaranteed; the May 2026 countywide DV hotline transition means confirming the current intake route.

Safe Place — Youth Crisis Entry (Ages 12–17), Maricopa County

The 12-17 crisis entry - call, text or walk into Safe Place sites (QuikTrip, Valley Metro, libraries, fire stations) for immediate youth help countywide.

Overnight shelter not guaranteed; use the current 44357 text instructions (older guides show 69866).

VA Community Resource & Referral Center (CRRC) – Phoenix (veteran housing and coordinated entry)

The veterans' walk-in front door - housing assessments and referrals (transitional, permanent, eviction prevention), the HUD-VASH pathway, benefits and health care, free of charge.

Weekdays 7:30 a.m.-4 p.m.; central Phoenix; referrals depend on capacity - arrive early for same-day referrals.

Gaps: No confirmed emergency shelter capacity for young adults 18-24 in Mesa/East Valley: the UMOM youth records are unconfirmed or historical (operator transferred to Child Crisis Arizona), Covenant House has no Arizona site, and only Safe Place (12-17) plus outreach/coordinated-entry points (NAC; UMOM youth outreach) are verified.; No general hotel/motel voucher program and no safe-parking pathway: Red Cross assistance is disaster-triggered, UMOM heat relief is seasonal and referral-based, and hotel-based shelters (Off the Streets, Sue's Espacio, Rio Fresco) are referral-only.; Reentry housing: no confirmed community-referral reentry beds in Phoenix (Dismas' Phoenix facility unconfirmed); H2O and HUD-VASH touch reentry but require health-plan or VA routes.; No open voucher waitlists captured: Mesa HCV, HAMC, Tempe and Phoenix lists are closed or referral-only - no immediate Section 8 application pathway.; Mesa I-HELP rotating shelter unresolved - no confirmed rotating-shelter intake for Mesa adults (operator largely resolved to LSS-SW serving adult women, current operation unconfirmed).; Eviction/tenant legal defense is not represented as a resource (AZEvictionHelp.org appears only as a referral mention).; East Valley single-women capacity: no confirmed Mesa-area beds for women without children; Halle Women's Center (Phoenix) and DV shelters are the closest options.; No confirmed women-veterans-specific beds in Maricopa County (MANA House described for male veterans; Ashley's Place within U.S.VETS noted but not separately verified).

First five more to consider

A New Leaf · La Mesita Apartments (affordable housing and project-based vouchers, Mesa)

Adds a permanent-affordable Mesa property with PBV units for households leaving homelessness - a longer-term destination the starter crisis/rental-aid resources do not cover.

AHCCCS Housing and Health Opportunities (H2O) Demonstration — Solari Program Administrator

Adds H2O's housing and health supports for SMI members, including people leaving jail - a reentry-linked pathway absent from the starter set.

AHCCCS Housing Program (AHP) — Statewide Permanent Supportive Housing (administrator: Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation with HOM, Inc.)

Adds the state-funded permanent supportive housing lane for AHCCCS members with behavioral health needs - no supportive-housing route exists in the starter set.

AllThrive 365 (formerly Foundation for Senior Living / FSL) — Affordable Housing Communities

Adds a portfolio of senior- and disability-designated affordable communities with direct manager contacts - another property-level route beyond the starter set.

Area Agency on Aging, Region One - HOPWA Housing Information Services (HIV Care Directions)

Adds an HIV-specific housing navigation access point - a medically linked pathway not covered by the starter set.

More to consider (6–10)

Arizona Department of Housing (ADOH) — Section 811 PRA (DDD referral) + statewide affordable-housing/LIHTC information

Adds the statewide affordable-housing search and 811 PRA administration - useful for staff hunting income-restricted units beyond the starter set's specific options.

Arizona Recovery Housing Association (AzRHA) — certified recovery residence search

Adds a certified recovery-residence search for clients needing sober housing - the starter set includes no recovery-housing pathway.

ARM of Save the Family — Affordable Rental Housing (Mesa and Maricopa County)

Adds a large scattered-site affordable portfolio with a Mesa office - another landlord channel; confirm waitlists and process by phone.

Chicanos Por La Causa (CPLC) - Housing & Community Development (income-restricted affordable housing communities)

Adds a nonprofit landlord pipeline (including a planned Mesa property) - worth watching for units, though waitlists are unverified.

Circle the City · Medical respite centers and care for adults experiencing homelessness

Adds medical respite - the right placement when a client is too ill for shelter; no health-based lodging pathway exists in the starter set.

More to consider (11–15)

City of Mesa Housing Authority · Section 8 HCV, project-based, Mainstream, VASH, FUP, EHV and TBRA/deposit assistance

Adds Mesa's voucher inventory (lists closed; project-based/Mainstream/VASH referrals) - the long-term subsidy the starter rent/utility aid and rehousing resources do not provide.

City of Phoenix Housing Department · Public Housing & Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher programs

Adds the county's largest housing authority for clients with a Phoenix connection - Section 8 is closed and Phoenix preferences apply; call to confirm public-housing status.

City of Tempe CARE & HOPE Line / HOPE Street Outreach Team

Adds Tempe's 24/7 outreach-and-referral line for clients physically in Tempe - complements the starter 211 and Phoenix-based entry points.

Coffelt-Lamoreaux Apartment Homes — 30 units reserved for DDD members

Adds a named property with DDD-reserved units - a concrete address for the DDD referral lane; ask about vacancies and unit sizes.

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) - Coordinated Entry, PATH Outreach & Hotel Bridge Housing

Adds a behavioral-health-linked coordinated entry and outreach route plus hotel bridge housing - useful when clients need a mental-health-capable path.

More to consider (16–20)

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) · Supportive Housing for Adults with Serious Mental Illness

Adds a behavioral-health provider's supportive-housing line for SMI clients, with a Mesa administration office - capacity and costs unpublished; call first.

DDD Affordable Housing Program — Section 811 PRA, Coffelt-Lamoreaux and housing voucher referrals (DES Division of Developmental Disabilities)

Adds the DDD referral lane into subsidized units and vouchers for adults with developmental disabilities - no disability-specific housing route exists in the starter set.

DOVES (Area Agency on Aging, Region One) - domestic violence services for older adults (50+)

Adds a DV pathway for survivors 50+ with a 24/7 line and transitional beds - fills the older-adult gap in the starter DV option.

Escobedo at Verde Vista Phase II (LIHTC and project-based voucher community)

Adds a second Mesa PBV property (referral-only) for households leaving homelessness - complementary to the starter rent/utility and rehousing aids.

First Place AZ – First Place–Phoenix Apartments (supportive apartments; residential Transition Academy with SARRC)

Adds rare supported housing for adults with autism/IDD with onsite life-skills and employment programs - costs and availability require a call.

More to consider (21–25)

Florence Crittenton Services of Arizona – UPLIFT permanent supportive housing

Adds a small supportive-housing program for young women leaving foster care - verify current operations and referral process first.

Foster360 – housing and wraparound services for youth aging out of foster care

Adds East Valley housing for foster youth aging out - a population gap; capacity is small (about 11-12) and ages need confirming.

Hand in Hand Street Outreach – Maricopa County Human Services Department

Adds a request-an-outreach-visit channel for covered county areas (not Mesa) - for clients outside Mesa city limits.

Homeward Bound — Thunderbird Apartments (A New Leaf)

Adds Thunderbird Apartments (the former Homeward Bound campus) - an affordable family property with a published leasing line and rental criteria.

HOPWA (Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS) — STRMU, Permanent Housing Placement, TBRA and supportive housing (City of Phoenix)

Adds the countywide HOPWA front door with rent, move-in and TBRA help for households affected by HIV - a distinct medical-housing funding stream not in the starter set.

More to consider (26–30)

House of Refuge · Transitional housing program for families experiencing homelessness (Mesa, AZ)

Adds a structured year-long transitional program in Mesa for families - a step-up after shelter; openings are periodic and work/income requirements apply.

Housing Authority of Maricopa County (HAMC) · Housing Choice Vouchers & special voucher programs (Emergency Housing Vouchers winding down)

Adds the county housing authority's referral-based special vouchers (Mainstream, FUP, VASH) - check the jurisdiction map, since most of Mesa is excluded.

HUD-VASH (HUD-VA Supportive Housing) — VA Phoenix Health Care System

Adds the voucher-plus-case-management program behind the starter CRRC referral - explains what veterans can be referred into; capacity is limited.

Lost Our Home Pet Rescue – Temporary Care Program

Adds pet temporary care that removes the pet barrier blocking shelter or housing access - a unique service with no equivalent in the starter set.

MANA House · Veteran transitional housing

Adds a concrete VA-referred transitional destination behind the starter CRRC route - confirm current beds and women-veteran eligibility via the VA.

More to consider (31–35)

Mercy Housing – Mesa Senior Meadows (Mesa, AZ)

Adds a currently-accepting senior community in Mesa with a direct leasing line - a concrete alternative to waitlisted voucher routes.

Oxford House, Inc. · Oxford House sober-living residences (self-run recovery housing)

Adds a national sober-living network with a live vacancy search - practical recovery housing not in the starter set.

Phoenix Dream Center · Life Recovery Program (residential recovery for men and women)

Adds a large free residential recovery program with onsite medical care - an option when treatment-linked housing is needed; callbacks can take days.

Phoenix Rescue Mission · Changing Lives Center (women and children residential recovery)

Adds a long-term recovery program where mothers keep children with them - family-capable recovery housing absent from the starter set.

Phoenix Rescue Mission · Street Outreach Hotline (Hope Coach) and Get Help line

Adds a request-a-visit outreach hotline and housing-assistance intake for unsheltered clients countywide - useful when someone will not go to a center.

More to consider (36–40)

Prisma Community Care (formerly Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS) – HIV housing assistance (HOPWA)

Adds a second HOPWA-affiliated agency to call (new name) - verify which housing help it currently delivers; citywide intake remains the reliable route.

Sanctuary Recovery Centers / Sanctuary Sober Living — Residential Treatment and Recovery Housing, North Phoenix

Adds treatment-linked housing in North Phoenix - coverage is unclear (Medicaid directory vs. private-pay signals), so verify cost before referring.

Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Ozanam Manor (Interim/Transitional Housing)

Adds transitional beds for older adults and adults with disabilities with a clear screening line - a step after shelter for those populations.

Southwest Behavioral & Health Services (SB&H) · HOPWA Housing Programs (HIV/AIDS)

Adds a direct HOPWA provider with transitional and permanent units - a placement route behind the countywide HIV housing intake.

Step Into Housing — City of Tempe / Tempe Community Action Agency shelter-to-housing pathway

Adds a structured shelter-to-housing pathway within Tempe's system - worth asking about; confirm it is still operating before promising entry.

More to consider (41–45)

Sue's Espacio bridge shelter — City of Tempe (Mercy House case management)

Adds a pet-friendly, private-room bridge shelter in Tempe with a 24/7 referral line - non-congregate capacity for Tempe-side clients.

Tempe Housing Services / Tempe Housing Authority — Section 8 HCV (waitlist closed), HOME TBRA and special-purpose vouchers

Adds referral-based TBRA and special vouchers with Tempe preferences - mainly for clients with a Tempe connection; lists are closed or referral-based.

U.S.VETS Phoenix — veteran housing and supportive services

Adds a nonprofit veteran provider with transitional, permanent and SSVF resources - a second door besides the starter VA route, including women veterans.

UMOM New Day Centers / Helping Hands Housing Services — Permanent Affordable Housing Communities

Adds current Valley affordable communities with property-manager applications - a landlord route beyond the starter set (UMOM clients receive no preference).

UMOM Youth Outreach (ages 18-24)

Adds a young-adult (18-24) coordinated entry and outreach route - distinct from the starter 12-17 line and the adult entries; confirm the day-center address and hours by phone before sending anyone.

More to consider (46–50)

Ability360 (formerly Arizona Bridge to Independent Living) - housing information & referral, home modifications, Mainstream voucher partnership

Adds disability-specific housing navigation and free home-modification help - distinct from the starter 211 navigation and financial aids.

American Red Cross - Disaster Services, Phoenix (emergency lodging/shelter for people displaced by home fires & other disasters)

Adds disaster-triggered emergency lodging - a narrow pathway the starter set does not cover, with a caution that it is not a general hotel-voucher route.

Arizona Complete Health Avondale Resource Center (city resource center & coordinated entry access point)

Adds a walk-in West Valley coordinated entry point and IHELP intake site - useful when a client is physically in the Southwest Valley, though it is a long trip from Mesa.

AZCEND — East Valley shelter (I-HELP), rent & utility assistance, senior home sharing

Adds an East Valley shelter-plus-financial-aid nonprofit whose Senior Home Sharing program includes Mesa - a shared-housing model not in the starter set.

Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) — Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention (housing programs)

Adds a second rapid-rehousing provider with eviction prevention and landlord mediation - extra capacity beyond the starter RRH route.

More to consider (51–55)

Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) — Single Adult Shelter (main campus)

Adds the largest single-adult shelter itself - the main overflow capacity for Mesa singles when local options are full; the starter Welcome Center routes here.

Child Crisis Arizona · Emergency children's shelter — Mesa (Whiteman's Safe Haven, ages 2–10)

Adds the county's only licensed emergency shelter for ages 2-10 - a specialized children's pathway the starter youth line (12-17) does not cover.

Child Crisis Arizona · Group home for teen boys in foster care (ages 11–17, Mesa)

Adds a rare residential option for teen boys in foster care - placement-based, reachable only through child-welfare caseworkers.

City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Mesa Homeless Resource Line (480-644-HOPE)

Adds the city-staffed Mesa Homeless Resource Line as its own callable resource - the weekday referral route into the starter Off the Streets shelter and local outreach.

City of Mesa Housing Authority · Security and Utility Deposit Program

Adds one-time security/utility deposit help for Mesa movers - a move-in-cost instrument distinct from the starter rent/utility and rehousing resources; confirm the current application window (a temporary suspension was reported).

More to consider (56–60)

Adds a behavioral-health provider's shelter site reachable only via its access line - confirm population and availability before sending anyone.

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) — Mesa Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA)

Adds a Mesa-focused rental-assistance provider with a 24/7 access line - extra capacity beyond the starter RRH route (funding is year-to-year).

DDD Housing Specialists — referral route into the AHCCCS Housing Program and H2O

Adds the internal DDD referral route into AHP and H2O - clarifies how DDD-connected clients access those programs, distinct from the programs themselves.

De Colores (Chicanos Por La Causa) - domestic and sexual violence shelter and services

Adds a bilingual, culturally specific DV shelter with a 24-hour hotline plus non-shelter legal and housing services - an alternative to the starter East Valley DV route.

Family Promise of Greater Phoenix – Mesa House family shelter (plus congregation-network shelter)

Adds a Mesa family shelter with a direct screening line and pets welcome - useful when the starter family routes are waitlisted.

Adds a small navigation nonprofit for clients needing extra help finding transitional/supportive housing - confirm whether it directly leases or only refers.

Keys to Change (Human Services Campus) · Key Campus emergency shelter & nightly overflow - Brian Garcia Welcome Center (single adults, Phoenix)

Adds the official countywide access-point list (including two Mesa sites) - a staff reference for matching clients to the right entry; verify hours by phone.

Adds the county's largest DV shelter capacity (about 30% of DV beds) with children's services and a 24/7 direct hotline - a central alternative when East Valley capacity is full.

Adds an East Valley rotating shelter with a walk-in morning intake - capacity context; Mesa's own I-HELP chapter remains a separate unresolved lead.

Adds a 55+ shelter with private rooms for older clients with health or mobility needs - a population-specific capacity not in the starter set.

Adds the largest family shelter's own record - what the starter family waitlist can lead to; stays and waits vary.

UMOM Halle Women's Center

Adds the dedicated single-women's shelter - the main option for unaccompanied women; there is always a waiting list.

More to consider (81–82)

UMOM Summer Family Heat Relief — heat respite and hotel stays for families (City of Phoenix partnership)

Adds seasonal heat-relief and hotel overflow for families - summer-specific, access by referral, and hotel capacity has been inconsistent.

Vista Colina Family Shelter (Central Arizona Shelter Services)

Adds a private-room family shelter reached through the starter Family Housing Hub - useful context when families ask what the waitlist leads to.

Administrator resolution needed (8)

A New Leaf · Helaman House (bridge/transitional housing, Mesa)

Needs resolution: confirming whether this Mesa bridge-housing step still operates would clarify a transitional option in the city's own housing path.

Current existence, eligibility, service scope and intake are unresolved: Mesa's pages still name Helaman House in the housing path while A New Leaf's live service list omits it; the city funding agreement ran through December 31, 2024; no public contact route is published; and an unrelated Mesa recovery home uses the same name at a different address. Targeted checks (city pages, municipal agreement, program metrics, provider site) could not establish a usable intake route, so it is kept for administrator verification rather than client referral.

A New Leaf · SAFEDVS 24-hour domestic violence hotline (former countywide screening line)

Needs resolution: confirming this line's current scope would restore or rule out a direct DV-shelter routing number that A New Leaf still publishes.

Consequential access conflict unresolved: the countywide centralized DV screening line reportedly closed May 15, 2026 (12News; Sojourner Center), while A New Leaf's current site still promotes (480) 890-3039 / 1-844-SAFEDVS for placement into its DV services; public sources cannot confirm whether the line is currently staffed or how far its reach extends. Retained for administrator verification, with confirmed fallback routes (direct shelter calls; state and national hotlines) noted.

AffordableHousingHub.org (unofficial third-party) - waitlist & affordable-housing tracker for Mesa and Maricopa County

Needs resolution: an unofficial tracker whose waitlist claims cannot be trusted - worth resolving only as a staff monitoring pointer, never as a client referral.

Retained as a needs-resolution monitoring lead for administrators, not a client referral. Re-verified 2026-09-28: the site exists and covers Mesa/Maricopa County, but its core value - waitlist status - is unofficial (data attributed to section8waitlist.org) and internally inconsistent (the Mesa authority page displays "Information verified September 28, 2026" and "Waitlist data synced 3 hr ago" alongside last-verified dates of February 19 and May 21, 2026), and the site offers no intake, navigation or application channel of its own (it is a private third-party publisher carrying advertising). Remaining obstacle: its waitlist claims cannot be verified from primary sources and must not be passed to clients as fact; any use requires confirming status with the City of Mesa Housing Authority ((480) 644-3536) or the specific property before relaying it.

Central Arizona Shelter Services · Norton & Ramsey Social Justice Empowerment Center (Glendale coordinated entry access point)

Needs resolution: confirming whether the Glendale CE site still operates would restore a West Valley walk-in point; otherwise use the starter Welcome Center.

Needs resolution: the site's current walk-in status remains conflicted after live re-checks on 2026-09-28. NAC's access-point page (which lists the Glendale site as 'Central Arizona Shelter Services'), Justa Center and the City of Tolleson still list NREC at 7031 N 56th Ave with phone 623-207-8902 and weekday hours, but CASS's own current website lists only its Phoenix sites and tells single adults to call the Welcome Center (602-229-5155) for intake, and the City of Glendale's homeless-services page routes individual assessments to the Brian Garcia Welcome Center. Direct fetches of the Glendale page and the MAG access-points PDF failed during review (fetch failures are not evidence of closure; Glendale routing content was seen via a search snapshot). A responsible referral requires confirming the center is still operating; the phone lead (623-207-8902) and the central fallback route (602-229-5155 / 211) are preserved for administrators.

Dismas Charities, Inc. · Phoenix Residential Reentry Center

Needs resolution: verifying whether a Phoenix reentry center exists would open a reentry housing lane; until then route via corrections case managers.

Submitted as Dismas' 'Phoenix Residential Reentry Center,' but targeted checks found no current Dismas-operated Phoenix facility: public records show Dismas' Arizona residential reentry services and BOP contract in Tucson, and 211 Arizona lists only the Tucson center. Re-checked 2026-09-28: no Phoenix-area Dismas facility or contract surfaced, and current federal delivery orders continue Tucson-area services. Combined with the access rule that reentry-center placement runs through the Bureau of Prisons or corrections agencies rather than community self-referral, the Phoenix identity and geography cannot be responsibly confirmed. Retained as a needs-resolution lead with a supported contact path (dismas.com; BOP/probation/corrections; 211 Arizona).

I-HELP Mesa – Interfaith Homeless Emergency Lodging Program

Needs resolution: clearing up the Mesa I-HELP operation and intake number would restore a local rotating-shelter option; use 211 meanwhile.

Retained as needs-resolution after targeted checks on 2026-09-28: the earlier operator conflict is largely resolved toward Lutheran Social Services of the Southwest serving adult women (Tempe Community Action Agency's current Find Help Now page and a 2-1-1 record agree; the AZCEND contacts in one directory entry belong to AZCEND's separate Chandler I-HELP). However, LSS-SW's own current website no longer lists an I-HELP or shelter program, and the most recent program evidence is February–March 2024, so current operation and the intake line cannot be confirmed. The record preserves the supported numbers and details and routes confirmation through 2-1-1; kept for administrator confirmation rather than client referral.

Tempe River Bottom – Resource Village (City of Tempe with nonprofit/agency partners)

Needs resolution: a historical village whose pet-inclusive model is worth remembering, but current operation is unconfirmed - use the CARE & HOPE Line for present-day needs.

Targeted checks of Tempe's Salt River Bottom response page, the May 2023 River Bottom Update, the Sept. 2022 FAQ and the Oct. 2022 council presentation show the village as part of the concluded 2022–2023 river-bottom operation (site near the south bank; the river bed is a no-trespass area), with no current listing, hours or contact. Whether the village or its pet services still operate could not be confirmed, so the record is retained with a clear warning and the current CARE & HOPE Line as the only supported route; the documented amenity list was restored to include showers.

UMOM/Tumbleweed Youth Crisis Shelter and Independent Living Programs (ages 12-17; 16-21)

Needs resolution: confirming the current operator and location prevents a misdirected referral of a minor; Safe Place (602-841-5799) remains the supported live route for ages 12-17.

Consequential unresolved attribution: UMOM's current program materials list shelter for families and single women; child-welfare and youth-service announcements document that the Independent Living Program was adopted by Child Crisis Arizona effective July 1, 2021 (up to about 50 youth ages 16-21 at two Phoenix locations, some beds reserved for Safe Place youth) and that Child Crisis Arizona operates the county's Safe Place youth crisis response - while older UMOM profiles and directories still attribute a 12-17 youth crisis shelter (Open Hands) and the 16-21 program to UMOM, with conflicting bed counts. No current UMOM-operated youth shelter or independent living program could be confirmed, so a referral to a 'UMOM youth shelter' cannot be responsibly made; the Safe Place line (602-841-5799; text SAFE + location to 44357) is the supported live route for a youth 12-17 in immediate crisis, and the historical lead is retained for administrator verification and reconciliation of older listings.

All resource details

DeepSeek-reviewed Housing proposals. Office approval and agency verification remain separate.

211 Arizona Helpline (2-1-1) — usable

Statewide information-and-referral helpline for Arizona (a program of Solari) that connects people in Mesa and Maricopa County with shelter and housing options, rent and utility assistance, food, and other basic-needs programs. Live specialists help callers plan their next steps, and 211 operates a Housing Crisis Hotline for people who are experiencing homelessness or are at risk of it.

Kinds of help: Housing navigation; Groups: Spanish

phone: 2-1-1 in Arizona; 877-211-8661 from anywhere; Relay users: 7-1-1 or 800-367-8939 and ask for 877-211-8661

website: https://211arizona.org/

hours: Live specialists 9:00 a.m. to 7:00 p.m., 7 days a week; online directory available anytime.

Services Offered

Live help finding and connecting to shelter and housing options, rent and utility assistance, food programs, employment and education resources, veterans services, health coverage application help (COVER Arizona), and other basic-needs programs. A Housing Crisis Hotline supports people who are experiencing homelessness or at risk of becoming homeless, and call specialists route callers to shelter intake processes (including family shelter intake through the Family Housing Hub).

Eligibility Requirements

Open to anyone in Arizona who needs help finding services - no income test or referral is needed to call. The individual programs 211 refers to set their own eligibility rules (for example, shelter intake screenings).

Population Served

Statewide, including Mesa and Maricopa County; individuals and families. Live help is available in English and Spanish, with real-time interpreter services for other languages.

How to Best Connect

Dial 2-1-1 from any phone in Arizona, or 877-211-8661 from anywhere. For a Spanish-speaking representative, choose option 2 after dialing. Live operators are available 9:00 a.m. to 7:00 p.m., seven days a week. Search the online directory any time at search.211arizona.org, or email through 211arizona.org. Relay callers can dial 7-1-1 and ask for 877-211-8661.

Important Information to Know

211 is a free referral and navigation line, not a housing provider - it does not itself offer beds or pay rent, and it may not know same-day shelter bed status. Ask specifically about the Housing Crisis Hotline and about the programs you are trying to reach.

Sources: 211 Arizona - statewide information and referral service (Solari)

A New Leaf · Autumn House Domestic Violence Shelter (East Valley/Mesa) — usable

A New Leaf's domestic-violence shelter and transitional housing in a confidential Mesa-area location - the East Valley's first DV shelter, operating since 1983. It has 22 beds for survivors fleeing abuse and serves survivors of any gender; the facility is pet-friendly. Placement is arranged by phone screening through A New Leaf's 24-hour DV hotline.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Domestic violence survivors, Women, Families

phone: 24-hour DV hotline: (480) 890-3039; 1-844-SAFEDVS (844-723-3387)

address: Mesa area (confidential location; address not disclosed to protect survivors)

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/sexual-and-domestic-violence-services/autumn-house/

email: DVSTOP@turnanewleaf.org

hours: Hotline 24/7; staff on site around the clock; survivors accepted throughout the year.

Services Offered

Safe shelter and transitional housing for people fleeing domestic abuse, with staff support from arrival; help stabilizing daily life through a workforce center (job and employment help), transportation assistance, and counseling; court advocacy for orders of protection; help planning the move into independent housing; and optional services for up to six months after leaving. The facility is pet-friendly.

Eligibility Requirements

For victims of domestic violence. All clients are screened prior to placement - call the hotline to be assessed. Bed availability varies and placement is not guaranteed. No fee for shelter is published; ask about costs during screening.

Population Served

Survivors of domestic violence fleeing abuse in the East Valley/Mesa area. A New Leaf states Autumn House serves anyone who is a victim of domestic violence, regardless of gender; program materials describe shelter for women, children, and men leaving abusive situations.

How to Best Connect

Call A New Leaf's 24-hour domestic-violence hotline at (480) 890-3039 or 1-844-SAFEDVS (844-723-3387) - the hotline handles screening and placement, and program staff should not be contacted directly. A New Leaf also lists the email DVSTOP@turnanewleaf.org for domestic-violence services, and its 2026 domestic-violence services fact sheet lists AutumnHouse@turnanewleaf.org for availability questions; the hotline remains the screening and placement route. The location is confidential, and intake is by phone screening rather than walk-in.

Important Information to Know

Placement is not guaranteed: clients are screened before admission, and the shelter has 22 beds. Countywide domestic-violence shelter access changed in May 2026 when the centralized screening line closed and shelters shifted to direct calls; A New Leaf's hotline remains the line it lists for placement into its own programs. Other routes: National Domestic Violence Hotline (800-799-7233) and the Arizona Sexual & Domestic Violence Hotline (602-279-2980; text 520-720-3383). In an emergency, call 911.

Sources: A New Leaf - Autumn House Domestic Violence Shelter · A New Leaf - Sexual & Domestic Violence Services (24-hour hotline) · A New Leaf - Domestic & Sexual Violence Services Department fact sheet (2026; Autumn House availability email) · 12News - Arizona domestic violence hotline discontinued (May 2026)

A New Leaf · East Valley Men's Center (EVMC) — usable

Emergency shelter in Mesa for single men experiencing homelessness - 104 beds, an immediate bed and storage, case management toward a permanent housing plan, and onsite workforce help. Intake begins with an online pre-screen; A New Leaf does not take intakes over the phone.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Men

phone: Placement hotline (individuals): (877) 211-8661; program office: (480) 610-6722 (staff ask that services not be requested by direct call)

address: 2345 N Country Club Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/housing-and-shelter/east-valley-mens-center/

hours: Intake hours not published; enter through the online pre-screen and call (877) 211-8661 for current intake information.

Services Offered

Emergency shelter for single men: a bed and storage for personal belongings for the length of stay; help from staff to build a permanent housing plan; an onsite workforce center for resumes, references, and job interviews; employment and transportation assistance; and referrals to substance abuse support groups.

Eligibility Requirements

Single men experiencing homelessness. Children under 18 are not allowed on site. Entry requires completing A New Leaf's online pre-screen - no phone intakes are taken - and admission depends on bed availability.

Population Served

Single men facing homelessness, especially in Mesa and the East Valley; A New Leaf describes single men as the largest homeless population in Arizona and the group that struggles most to find help.

How to Best Connect

First step: complete the online pre-screen linked on the EVMC page (https://forms.office.com/r/E1CyUV7sR4). For shelter placement, call the individuals' hotline (877) 211-8661 (also reachable through 2-1-1). Do not call program staff for services. Intake hours are not published - confirm current details when you call.

Important Information to Know

Beds are filled through intake and a waitlist, so availability can change day to day, and a pre-screen does not guarantee a bed. No children can stay on site. Staff ask that clients not contact them directly - use the hotline and the pre-screen. Older regional lists have also described EVMC as a Mesa coordinated-entry access point with weekday hours; confirm current assessment hours by phone if you need that service.

Sources: A New Leaf - East Valley Men's Center · A New Leaf - Housing & Shelter services and placement hotlines

Transitional 'Bridge to Success' housing step in Mesa's housing path for people moving on from the Off the Streets emergency shelter - named in City of Mesa materials as A New Leaf's Helaman House, with wraparound case management, help building work and credit history and savings, and connections to rental assistance and housing vouchers. Whether it currently operates, who can enter, and how referrals work are not confirmed.

Kinds of help: Transitional housing; Groups: No group assigned

address: 1311 E University Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201 (per the City of Mesa 2022 agreement; current site not confirmed)

resolutionReason: Current existence, eligibility, service scope and intake are unresolved: Mesa's pages still name Helaman House in the housing path while A New Leaf's live service list omits it; the city funding agreement ran through December 31, 2024; no public contact route is published; and an unrelated Mesa recovery home uses the same name at a different address. Targeted checks (city pages, municipal agreement, program metrics, provider site) could not establish a usable intake route, so it is kept for administrator verification rather than client referral.

Services Offered

Transitional (bridge) housing with built-in structure and continued case management for people moving on from Mesa's emergency shelter stabilization stage - per City of Mesa descriptions: building work and credit history, saving toward a home, practicing life skills, and connecting to rental assistance, housing vouchers, or other long-term affordable housing. A 2022 city agreement also described bridge-to-housing with emergency temporary housing and support services at this location.

Eligibility Requirements

Do not send anyone to the address without confirming the program is active there.

Sources: City of Mesa - Off the Streets (Bridge to Success / Helaman House) · City of Mesa - Addressing Homelessness in Mesa · City of Mesa - 2022 subrecipient agreement for bridge housing at Helaman House, 1311 E University Dr

A New Leaf · La Mesita Apartments (affordable housing and project-based vouchers, Mesa) — usable

A 110-unit affordable apartment community in Mesa (studios through four-bedroom homes) run by A New Leaf, with onsite services including a Head Start program for ages 0-5. Some units are income-restricted (at or below 60% or 30% of area median income), and units are available for people transitioning from homelessness through the City of Mesa Project-Based Voucher (PBV) program, where tenants pay about 30% of income.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities, Housing vouchers & subsidized rent programs; Groups: Families, Seniors, People with disabilities

phone: Leasing: (480) 969-5233; Mesa Housing & Community Development: (480) 644-3536

address: 2254 W Main St, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/housing-and-shelter/la-mesita-apartments/

email: assistant@lamesita123apartments.com

hours: Leasing office hours not published; Mesa Housing PBV office hours: Monday to Thursday, 7:30 a.m. to 5:00 p.m. (confirm).

Services Offered

Long-term affordable rental housing with studio through four-bedroom units and onsite support: a Head Start program for children ages 0-5, a computer lab, financial-wellness and parenting classes, and workforce assistance. Some units carry project-based rental assistance so that people transitioning from homelessness who need support services can pay about 30% of their income for rent.

Eligibility Requirements

General units are for low-income households within income limits (some units serve households at or below 60% of area median income; some at or below 30%). PBV units follow City of Mesa criteria: applicants must meet the local chronically homeless definition (at least two episodes of shelter living plus a substantiated need for long-term case management and supportive services) and be an elderly individual or family, a disabled individual or family, or a family needing supportive services; income must be at or below published limits (PBV materials list 30% AMI caps, for example $13,200 for one person); a background check and LIHTC tenancy approval are required. The City describes PBV as a referral-based program.

Population Served

Low-income Mesa households - families with children, individuals, and (for PBV units) elderly or disabled households and families needing supportive services, including people transitioning from homelessness.

How to Best Connect

For unit availability, call the leasing office at (480) 969-5233 or email assistant@lamesita123apartments.com (Kay Kay Realty handles leasing, and A New Leaf directs housing inquiries there). For PBV-subsidized units, current City of Mesa guidance routes adults without children through a coordinated-entry access point (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 602-229-5155) and families with minor children through the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700). For PBV questions, contact City of Mesa Housing & Community Development at (480) 644-3536 (20 E. Main St, Suite 250; Spanish-language information at the same number), or email housing@turnanewleaf.org for general housing support.

Important Information to Know

Status check: older City of Mesa PBV packets list the La Mesita waiting list as open, but the City's current PBV page describes La Mesita as 'Referral Only' - confirm the current process and any wait before promising anything. PBV rent is about 30% of income, and LIHTC income limits are very low. Some A New Leaf materials note a veteran preference for units serving people transitioning from homelessness - confirm current preference rules with Mesa Housing. A New Leaf also operates a separate La Mesita Family Shelter on the same campus for families in crisis - ask which program fits your situation.

Sources: A New Leaf - La Mesita Apartments · City of Mesa - Project-Based Voucher Program (La Mesita referral-only status) · City of Mesa - Project Based Voucher eligibility requirements and applications (PDF)

A New Leaf · MesaCAN Rent and Utility Assistance — usable

MesaCAN (Mesa Community Action Network), part of A New Leaf, provides emergency rent and utility assistance to low-income Mesa residents facing eviction or utility shut-off, plus access to broader services (financial coaching, workforce, tax help) at its Mesa office. Funding is limited and help is first-come, first-served.

Kinds of help: Rent, utility & deposit crisis assistance; Groups: Spanish

phone: (480) 833-9200 (office; utility orientations); (480) 750-9228 (rent-assistance appointment line)

address: 635 E Broadway Rd, Mesa, AZ 85204

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/financial-empowerment/mesacan/mesacan-rent-and-utility-assistance/

email: mesacanclient@turnanewleaf.org

hours: Office: Monday to Friday, 8:00 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. Rent appointments: first Thursday of the month, 2:00 to 3:00 p.m. (until slots fill). Utility orientations: Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday mornings from 8:00 a.m.

Services Offered

Financial help with past-due rent or mortgage payments and utility bills to help Mesa households avoid eviction or utility shut-off; help applying for LIHEAP (state utility assistance); information on City of Mesa security- and utility-deposit programs; and access to other MesaCAN services at the same office, including financial coaching, workforce services, free tax preparation (VITA), savings-match programs, and economic development help.

Eligibility Requirements

Must be a Mesa resident (city residents only). Rent help is for households facing eviction or a rent crisis; utility help is for households with a disconnection notice, and documented criteria include a household member who is 60+, a household member with a disability, or a child 6 or younger (confirm current criteria). Required documents include proof of income for the last 30 days for all household members, IDs for adults, birth certificates and Social Security cards for household members, and the current lease or mortgage statement plus the utility bill or disconnection notice. Assistance is first-come, first-served and funding can run out; applicants 60+ are exempt from the utility orientation.

Population Served

Low-income Mesa households facing a rent or utility crisis; application forms are available in English and Spanish.

How to Best Connect

Rent help: call the appointment line at (480) 750-9228 on the first Thursday of the month between 2:00 and 3:00 p.m., or until appointments are gone - you must reach a live person to get instructions. Utility help: schedule an orientation by calling (480) 833-9200 or emailing mesacanclient@turnanewleaf.org; orientations are Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday mornings at 8:00 a.m., and a completed application with all documents is required at the orientation. In person: 635 E Broadway Rd, Mesa, weekdays 8:00 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. Applications (English and Spanish) are on the program page or available at the office.

Important Information to Know

Assistance is not guaranteed: only a limited number of appointments are available, the phone system tells callers when funding is exhausted, and an appointment does not guarantee aid. Older referral lists show different call-in times (some said Thursdays 2:00-2:30 p.m.); the current program page says the first-Thursday 2:00-3:00 p.m. window - confirm when you call. Bring every required document; incomplete packets cannot be processed at orientation.

Sources: A New Leaf - MesaCAN Rent and Utility Assistance · City of Mesa - Mesa Community Action Network (MesaCAN) fact sheet

A New Leaf · Rapid ReHousing (rental subsidy and case management) — usable

A New Leaf's Rapid ReHousing program provides short-term rental subsidy (up to 12 months), move-in cost help, and case management for individuals and families experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including Mesa. Entry is through the region's coordinated entry system - call 211 - not a direct application.

Kinds of help: Rent and deposit help; Groups: Families, Single adults

phone: 2-1-1 / (877) 211-8661 (coordinated entry); A New Leaf main line: (480) 969-4024

address: No public office; staff meet participants across the community

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/housing-and-shelter/rapid-rehousing/

email: housing@turnanewleaf.org

hours: No public office hours; inquiries go through coordinated entry (call 211) or email.

Services Offered

Rental subsidy for up to 12 months depending on assessment, help with move-in costs including application fees, rent and utility payments based on standards tailored to the household, temporary case management, workforce assistance, and referrals to other services so participants can maintain housing long term.

Eligibility Requirements

Households currently experiencing homelessness or living in emergency shelter - both individuals and families. Must complete coordinated entry: single adults are assessed with the Vi-SPDAT through the single coordinated entry system, and families are assessed with the Vi-FSPDAT through the Family Housing Hub. The amount of assistance depends on case-management assessment and verification of household income and debt; funding availability varies.

Population Served

Individuals and families across the Valley (Maricopa County), including Mesa; the program has no single geographic focus and staff meet participants where they are.

How to Best Connect

There is no walk-in office. Call 2-1-1 (877-211-8661) to start coordinated entry; families may be routed through the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700). Questions about A New Leaf's housing programs can go to housing@turnanewleaf.org, and the main line is (480) 969-4024.

Important Information to Know

A New Leaf cannot take direct applications - entry is only through coordinated entry, and neither timing nor funding is guaranteed. Assistance is evaluated month to month and typically steps down over time; it is meant to stabilize housing quickly rather than provide a permanent subsidy. If a longer-term subsidy is needed, ask about vouchers or long-term affordable housing options at the same time.

Sources: A New Leaf - Rapid ReHousing · A New Leaf - Housing & Shelter services and placement hotlines
