Ability360 (formerly Arizona Bridge to Independent Living) - housing information & referral, home modifications, Mainstream voucher partnership — usable

Ability360 is the Phoenix area's nonprofit Center for Independent Living (formerly Arizona Bridge to Independent Living). It answers housing questions for people with disabilities, helps with accessible and affordable housing searches, and runs a home-modification program - ramps, grab bars, widened doorways and similar accessibility work - that is free for qualifying low-to-moderate-income Maricopa County residents. It has partnered with area housing authorities on HUD Mainstream vouchers for people with disabilities; ask about current voucher availability. Call (602) 256-2245 or the information line (602) 296-0536.

Kinds of help: Housing navigation; Groups: People with disabilities, Seniors

phone: (602) 256-2245 (main office); (602) 296-0536 (information & referral)

address: 5025 E. Washington St., Ste. 200, Phoenix, AZ 85034

website: https://ability360.org/

email: info@ability360.org

hours: Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m.; closed weekends (main office and Mesa office)

Services Offered

- Housing information and referral for people with disabilities and their families - staff answer questions about housing options and connect people to accessible/affordable housing and other community resources.
- Home Modification program that improves accessibility and safety at home: ramps, widened door openings, grab bars, sink and toilet modifications, relocated electrical switches, and similar work. "Adapt your home, not yourself."
- Broader independent living supports: independent living skills training, peer mentoring, transition services (including moving out of nursing homes or assisted living), home care services, and advocacy.
- A 2020 public report describes Ability360 working with five area housing authorities on HUD Mainstream Voucher applications for non-elderly people with disabilities, with designated vouchers reported under its control; the current status of any such vouchers is not confirmed - ask when you call.

Eligibility Requirements

- Information and referral: no eligibility rules are published; it serves people with disabilities and their families.
- Home modifications: for people with disabilities and/or seniors who are low-to-moderate-income Maricopa County residents. Mercy Care ALTCS members need a referral from their case manager. Required documents vary - call to confirm.
- Housing vouchers: eligibility is determined by the housing authority under HUD rules; nothing is guaranteed.

Population Served

- People with disabilities of all ages in the Greater Phoenix metropolitan area, including Mesa (Ability360 is described as Arizona's largest Center for Independent Living).
- Seniors are also served - the home-modification program is for "people with disabilities and/or seniors," and home-care services serve elderly clients.
- Families and support people of the above through information and referral.

How to Best Connect

- Call the main office at (602) 256-2245 or the Information & Referral line at (602) 296-0536; toll-free (800) 280-2245; AZ Relay Service 7-1-1; email info@ability360.org.
- Main office: 5025 E. Washington St., Ste. 200, Phoenix, AZ 85034 - open Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m., closed weekends.
- Mesa/Gilbert office (Home Care Services): 205 E. Southern Ave., Mesa, AZ 85210, (480) 655-9750, Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m. - ask which location handles your request.
- Home modifications: use the "Get Started" form on the Ability360 Home Modifications page or call.
- Remote access: phone, email and online forms; you do not need to visit in person to start.

Important Information to Know

- Ability360 is not a shelter and does not provide direct rent subsidies; its housing role is information/referral, home modifications, and the Mainstream voucher partnership described above.
- Home modifications are described as currently free to people who qualify; a separate community listing says "fees vary," so confirm cost and funding during intake (funded mainly by Community Development Block Grants, with ALTCS or other sources sometimes possible).
- Funding and capacity are limited; ask about current timing and availability.
- If you were told about a Mainstream voucher here, confirm current voucher availability directly - it is not published.

Sources: Ability360 - Home Modifications program page · Ability360 - Contact & Locations (main office, Mesa/Gilbert office, hours) · Ability360 - Information and Referral (housing/resource questions) · 211 Arizona - Ability360 Home Modifications (hours, eligibility, application) · ILRU - Choose, Get, Keep Integrated Housing (2020): Ability360 Mainstream voucher partnership

AffordableHousingHub.org (unofficial third-party) - waitlist & affordable-housing tracker for Mesa and Maricopa County — needs-resolution

Affordable Housing Hub is an independent, third-party website that compiles affordable-housing listings (Section 8, public housing, low-income tax-credit properties) and waitlist-status information nationwide, including Mesa and Maricopa County. It is not a housing provider and does not process applications, and its waitlist statuses are unofficial - data is attributed to another third-party site (section8waitlist.org) and should never be treated as authoritative. Treat it only as a rough monitoring pointer and confirm every opening with the housing authority or the property; its Mesa pages currently list both Mesa public housing and Section 8 waitlists as closed.

Kinds of help: Housing navigation; Groups: No group assigned

website: https://affordablehousinghub.org/

hours: Online resource - no in-person hours

resolutionReason: Retained as a needs-resolution monitoring lead for administrators, not a client referral. Re-verified 2026-09-28: the site exists and covers Mesa/Maricopa County, but its core value - waitlist status - is unofficial (data attributed to section8waitlist.org) and internally inconsistent (the Mesa authority page displays "Information verified September 28, 2026" and "Waitlist data synced 3 hr ago" alongside last-verified dates of February 19 and May 21, 2026), and the site offers no intake, navigation or application channel of its own (it is a private third-party publisher carrying advertising). Remaining obstacle: its waitlist claims cannot be verified from primary sources and must not be passed to clients as fact; any use requires confirming status with the City of Mesa Housing Authority ((480) 644-3536) or the specific property before relaying it.

Services Offered

- A free, searchable national directory of affordable-housing properties and waitlist statuses. Its Mesa pages include a City of Mesa Housing Authority profile (unit counts, waitlist summaries, HUD income limits and fair-market rents, and authority contact details) plus links to Mesa LIHTC properties such as Mesa Artspace Lofts and Residences at Center Street Station.
- Browsing and pointers only: no case management, no housing navigation, no intake, no applications processed, no vouchers issued.

Eligibility Requirements

- None to browse the website (it is public).
- Each listed housing program has its own rules (income limits, preferences, lotteries) set by the housing authority or property - not by Affordable Housing Hub.

Population Served

- Anyone researching affordable housing; no specific population is targeted. Mesa/Maricopa pages are relevant to local renters, including voucher seekers and people on or seeking waitlists.

How to Best Connect

- Website: https://affordablehousinghub.org/ - use the Mesa/Maricopa County pages to see what properties and programs exist.
- For anything actionable - status, applications, waitlist openings - contact the housing authority or the property directly, for example City of Mesa Housing Authority, (480) 644-3536, mesaaz.gov/housing. Nothing on this tracker should be passed to a client as fact without that confirmation.

Important Information to Know

- Unofficial and non-authoritative: waitlist data is supplied by a separate third-party site (section8waitlist.org); pages mix "re-check daily / synced" claims with last-verified dates months old, and the site is not a government or nonprofit service (it carries advertising).
- Do not rely on its status claims: an opening can be wrong or already closed. Confirm with the housing authority or property before advising anyone.
- Because its status information cannot be relied on and it offers no intake or application channel, it works at most as a monitoring pointer for staff watching for openings - not as a client referral.

Sources: Affordable Housing Hub - nationwide affordable housing search · Affordable Housing Hub - City of Mesa Housing Authority (Mesa, AZ) profile · Affordable Housing Hub - About Us

AHCCCS Housing and Health Opportunities (H2O) Demonstration — Solari Program Administrator — usable

Medicaid-funded housing program (H2O demonstration) for AHCCCS members who are homeless, have a Serious Mental Illness designation, and have a chronic health condition or a correctional release within 90 days. Solari is the statewide program administrator; enrollment runs through a data-driven referral process rather than a public application.

Kinds of help: Rent and deposit help, Health-linked housing (respite & HIV/AIDS); Groups: People with behavioral health needs, Re-entry, Medically vulnerable

phone: 1-855-814-4673 (local 480-546-7135)

website: https://community.solari-inc.org/h2o/

email: H2Omembersupport@solari-inc.org

hours: H2O Support Line: Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:00 p.m. MST (voicemail after hours; closed federal/AHCCCS holidays)

Services Offered

Outreach and education; transitional housing for up to 6 months (enhanced shelter settings and apartment/rental units with rental assistance); pre-tenancy and tenancy services (housing care plan, help finding and keeping housing); one-time transition and move-in costs; short-term rental assistance (up to 6 months, capped at 110% of fair market rent); and medically necessary home accessibility modifications. Program materials describe financial caps of up to $7,000 per federal fiscal year across utility arrears (up to 2 months, max $1,000), move-in assistance (max $3,000) and eviction prevention (up to 2 months of rent, max $3,000), with accessibility modifications up to $5,000 over the life of the demonstration. Rental assistance and move-in/eviction-prevention payments are administered by the program's Statewide Housing Administrator — the Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation (ABC), with HOM, Inc. handling housing functions and payments.

Eligibility Requirements

Must be an AHCCCS (Medicaid) member; must be experiencing homelessness or housing instability verified against the HUD definition (24 CFR 91.5); must have a Serious Mental Illness (SMI) designation; and must either have a chronic health condition or be in a correctional facility with release scheduled within 90 days, or released from a correctional facility within the last 90 days. Adults 18+.

Population Served

AHCCCS members with an SMI designation who are homeless, including those with chronic health conditions and people leaving incarceration; statewide, including Mesa and Maricopa County.

How to Best Connect

Solari H2O Support Line: toll-free 1-855-814-4673; local 480-546-7135; email H2Omembersupport@solari-inc.org. Hours: Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:00 p.m. MST (voicemail after hours). Callers can ask about eligibility and referrals; Solari also publishes an H2O provider contact list (organizations' service areas and services) from its Member Services page. Clients should also tell their AHCCCS health plan, behavioral-health home/provider or case manager; AHCCCS asks health homes to keep charts current with housing-related Z codes, SMI designation and chronic-condition diagnosis so members appear in the referral file, and members can ask their plan for a Housing Liaison or Housing Specialist. Note: AHCCCS states external referrals are not being accepted at this time; enrollment starts from AHCCCS's data-driven referral list plus Solari's eligibility verification, so a client cannot self-enroll by applying.

Important Information to Know

No member copayments or fees are described — H2O is Medicaid-funded. Services are time-limited (transitional housing/rental assistance up to 6 months) and capacity is limited ("as resources are available"), so call Solari for current status. Grievances or appeals about H2O services can go to the Solari support line (same numbers), email H2Oappeals@solari-inc.org, or use the online appeal portal (h2oappealsubmission.solari-inc.org).

Sources: AHCCCS — Housing and Health Opportunities (H2O) Demonstration · Solari — H2O Frequently Asked Questions (current, Jan. 2026) · Solari — H2O Operation Manual V2 (rev. Sept. 1, 2025; eligibility, caps, SHA roles) · Solari — H2O Community Support Network (Program Administrator site) · Solari — H2O Member Services (support line, provider contact list, appeals) · AHCCCS — H2O services (FFS provider manual ch. 29; utility arrears $1,000 / move-in $3,000 / modifications $5,000) · AHCCCS — H2O services fee schedule / rate methodology (rental assistance up to 110% FMR)

AHCCCS Housing Program (AHP) — Statewide Permanent Supportive Housing (administrator: Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation with HOM, Inc.) — usable

State-funded permanent supportive housing for AHCCCS members with behavioral health needs. The statewide Housing Administrator (ABC with HOM, Inc.) manages a single statewide waitlist and pays rent subsidies to landlords; members pay up to 30% of income, and referrals come from behavioral health providers.

Kinds of help: Supportive housing; Groups: People with behavioral health needs

phone: 602-712-9200 (ABC); 602-265-4640 (HOM)

website: https://www.azahcccs.gov/AHCCCS/Initiatives/AHP/

email: AHPinfo@azabc.org; Housing@azahcccs.gov

Services Offered

- State-funded permanent supportive housing (PSH) for AHCCCS members with behavioral health needs: long-term rent subsidies in scattered-site units, dedicated site-based units (Community Living Program models), and project-based voucher units.
- Members pay up to 30% of their income toward rent; the subsidy is paid directly to the landlord by the statewide Housing Administrator (Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation with HOM, Inc.).
- As budget allows, limited extra help: housing deposits, move-in assistance, eviction prevention, and payments for member-caused damages.
- The Housing Administrator receives provider referrals, manages the single statewide waitlist, verifies eligibility, hosts housing briefings, verifies household income and rent share, conducts Housing Quality Standards inspections, pays rental subsidies, and handles lease renewals and recertifications.
- Supportive services (case management, housing search help, life-skills and budgeting support) come through the member's AHCCCS behavioral health provider network, not from the administrator.

Eligibility Requirements

- Must be an adult AHCCCS member (18+).
- Most funding is reserved for members with a Serious Mental Illness (SMI) designation; a limited number of units serve high-cost/high-need members with general mental health and/or substance use (GMHSU) needs, and some units are tied to specific services such as Assertive Community Treatment (ACT) teams.
- A qualified behavioral health provider must document the behavioral health need and the housing need (examples: homelessness, an institutional transition, or a documented medical necessity).
- Only approved behavioral health providers can submit the AHP application on a member's behalf — members cannot apply directly.

Population Served

- Adults enrolled in AHCCCS with a serious mental illness designation, plus a smaller number of high-need members with general mental health/substance use conditions. Statewide, including Mesa and Maricopa County.

How to Best Connect

- Members: contact your behavioral health provider, health home, or health plan care team and ask for a housing assessment and referral (in Maricopa County the Regional Behavioral Health Authority is Mercy Care). The provider submits the AHP application.
- Program, application, or waitlist questions: ABC at 602-712-9200 or AHPinfo@azabc.org (administrative office: 501 E. Thomas Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85012).
- Already housed through AHP: HOM at 602-265-4640 or find your Housing Specialist at hominc.com/find (HOM Central AZ: 5326 E Washington St, Phoenix, AZ 85034).
- General AHCCCS housing questions: Housing@azahcccs.gov.
- Referral-based only; there is no walk-in application site.

Important Information to Know

- Housing resources are limited — the state allocation covers roughly 2,700 members — and a waitlist is in use; a referral does not guarantee housing or a timeline.
- This is the state-funded AHCCCS Housing Program (AHP), separate from the AHCCCS H2O demonstration and from HUD Section 8 and Continuum of Care programs.
- ABC/HOM's contract as statewide Housing Administrator was renewed effective July 1, 2026.
- Licensed behavioral health residential facilities, group homes, and other licensed clinical residential settings are not eligible for AHP.
- Current waitlist length and unit availability are not published — ask the member's provider or ABC for status.

Sources: AHCCCS Housing Programs (AHP) — AHCCCS program page · AHCCCS Housing Programs (AHP) — Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation/HOM administrator page and FAQ · ABC News — AHCCCS Housing Administrator contract renewed (effective July 1, 2026) · AHCCCS member flyer — housing supports and referral instructions · HOM, Inc. — housing assistance programs and HOM Central AZ contact

AllThrive 365 (formerly Foundation for Senior Living / FSL) — Affordable Housing Communities — usable

AllThrive 365 — the nonprofit formerly known as Foundation for Senior Living (FSL) — owns and manages affordable apartment communities across Arizona, including six in the Phoenix area, for older adults, adults with disabilities, and families with limited incomes. Each community has its own age and income rules and a local manager who handles applications.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities; Groups: Seniors, People with disabilities

phone: 602-285-1800

address: 1201 E. Thomas Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85014

website: https://allthrive365.org/housing/affordable-housing/

email: info@allthrive365.org

hours: Not published — call 602-285-1800 or the individual community manager

Services Offered

AllThrive 365 (the organization formerly called Foundation for Senior Living / FSL) owns and manages affordable apartment communities across Arizona, including six in the Greater Phoenix area:
- Acacia Heights — 78 units, ages 62+; 615 W. Pierson St., Phoenix 85013; manager Jeff Weist, 602-767-5812, JeffW@allthrive365.org
- Spectrum Courtyard — 25 units, family housing and adults with developmental disabilities; 5330 N. 21st Ave., Phoenix 85015; Jeff Weist, 602-767-5814, JeffW@allthrive365.org
- Vianney Villas Apartments — 50 units, ages 62+; 750 S. 4th St., Avondale 85323; Sylvia Ontiveros, 623-932-2036, SylviaO@allthrive365.org
- Sweetwater Gardens Apartments — 24 units, adults 18+ with mobility impairments; 2035 E. Sweetwater Ave., Phoenix 85022; Scott Van Soest, 602-867-4549, ScottV@allthrive365.org
- Village on Roeser — 112 units, ages 55+ and households 18+; 454 E. Roeser Rd., Phoenix 85040; Elvira Lopez, 602-268-5100, ElviraL@allthrive365.org
- 29 Palms Apartments — 21 units, ages 55+ and adults with developmental disabilities; 4901 E. Holly St., Phoenix 85008; Elvira Lopez, 602-595-7901, ElviraL@allthrive365.org
The organization also runs communities outside Maricopa County (Cottonwood, Flagstaff, Holbrook, Mayer, Payson, Prescott, Prescott Valley, Show Low, Williams, Coolidge, Yuma, Tucson, Lake Havasu City, Kingman and Wickenburg — full list at allthrive365.org/housing/affordable-housing), plus a Resource Navigator program that helps older adults and caregivers find services, and home energy-efficiency help.

Eligibility Requirements

Eligibility varies by property and is based on income and household size; each community is limited to a specific population — ages 62+, ages 55+, adults with disabilities (developmental disabilities or mobility impairments), or family housing. Exact income limits, rents and screening requirements are not published online; the manager for the specific community can explain the criteria and current limits.

Population Served

Older adults (55+ and 62+ communities), adults with developmental disabilities or mobility impairments, and families and individuals with limited incomes. Six communities are in the Phoenix metro area (Maricopa County); the rest are elsewhere in Arizona.

How to Best Connect

Call the central office at 602-285-1800 (TTY 602-297-1844) or email info@allthrive365.org; main office: 1201 E. Thomas Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85014. For a specific community, contact its manager directly using the names, phone numbers and emails listed above — managers handle applications and can explain availability, rents and paperwork. Note that "Foundation for Senior Living" and "FSL" now operate as AllThrive 365 (fsl.org redirects to allthrive365.org), so search both names when looking this up.

Important Information to Know

- The rebrand from Foundation for Senior Living (FSL) to AllThrive 365 took effect in April 2025 — it is the same housing program under a new name.
- These are permanent, income-restricted apartments, not emergency shelter. Waitlists, vacancy, rents and income limits can change and are not published — confirm directly with the community manager.
- No online application is listed; apply by phone or email with the manager. The website offers English and Spanish.
- Key links: allthrive365.org/housing/affordable-housing (community list); allthrive365.org (main site); fsl.org (redirects to the new site).

Sources: Affordable Housing in Arizona — AllThrive 365 (community list, managers, eligibility notes) · AllThrive 365 (formerly FSL) — homepage and contact information · Foundation for Senior Living web address (now redirects to AllThrive 365) · Foundation for Senior Living rebrands as AllThrive 365 (rebrand announcement)

American Red Cross - Disaster Services, Phoenix (emergency lodging/shelter for people displaced by home fires & other disasters) — usable

American Red Cross disaster assistance can help people displaced from their homes by disasters - including house and apartment fires and floods - with emergency needs that can include a place to stay (shelter/emergency lodging), food and clothing, provided case-by-case around the clock. It is not a general shelter or hotel-voucher program for homelessness. After a home fire or other disaster, call (800) 733-2767; for financial assistance you will typically need an incident number from the responding fire department. Local office: 4747 N. 22nd St., Ste. 100, Phoenix, (602) 336-6660.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: No group assigned

phone: (800) 733-2767 (24-hour emergency line); (602) 336-6660 (local office)

address: 4747 N. 22nd St., Ste. 100, Phoenix, AZ 85016

website: https://www.redcross.org/

hours: Emergency assistance 24 hours; office administration Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-4:30 p.m.

Services Offered

- Emergency assistance for people affected by local disasters (home/apartment fires, floods and similar events): can include shelter/emergency lodging, food, clothing, family reunification, and mental-health and health services; delivered 24 hours a day on a case-by-case basis.
- Recovery planning and referrals in the days and weeks after a disaster, plus preparedness information.
- Not offered: routine hotel/motel vouchers for people who are homeless, rent or deposit assistance, or ongoing housing programs.

Eligibility Requirements

- People impacted by a disaster (for example, displaced by a home fire).
- For financial assistance, the caller will typically need an incident number from the responding fire department; decisions are made case-by-case and are not guaranteed.

Population Served

- Residents affected by disasters in Maricopa, Gila and Pinal counties (the American Red Cross Central Arizona Chapter's service area), including Mesa - individuals and families of any age; no other population requirements.

How to Best Connect

- 24-hour emergency line: (800) 733-2767 (toll-free emergency voice/TDD). If a fire just happened, ask the responding fire department for the incident number before you call.
- Local office: 4747 N. 22nd St., Ste. 100, Phoenix, AZ 85016; (602) 336-6660; walk-in, phone, website or mail accepted. Office hours Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-4:30 p.m.; emergency services 24 hours.
- Online: redcross.org (see local Arizona pages).

Important Information to Know

- This is disaster relief, not a homelessness program: do not expect hotel vouchers for housing crises that are not disaster-related - for those needs, call 2-1-1 Arizona (dial 2-1-1) or local shelter services.
- Help is case-by-case and depends on verification (incident number); assistance can be limited in amount and scope and does not replace ongoing housing programs.
- Some older community lists show outdated Red Cross phone numbers (for example, (800) 842-7349); use the numbers above.

Sources: 211 Arizona - American Red Cross Disaster Services, Phoenix (eligibility, hours, phones, service area) · American Red Cross - Central Arizona Chapter (Greater Phoenix office, 24-hour assistance line, service counties) · City of Phoenix - "After the Fire" brochure: Red Cross emergency assistance for displaced families · American Red Cross - Home Fire Safety: help after a home fire

Area Agency on Aging, Region One - HOPWA Housing Information Services (HIV Care Directions) — usable

The Area Agency on Aging, Region One runs the HOPWA "Housing Information Services" program for people living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal Counties. A housing information specialist provides housing referrals, places clients on affordable-housing waitlists, and connects them to emergency shelter resources; the agency's HIV Care Directions program also provides HIV medical case management and help finding and maintaining subsidized and affordable housing. Call (602) 264-2273.

Kinds of help: Health-linked housing (respite & HIV/AIDS), Housing navigation; Groups: People living with HIV/AIDS, Medically vulnerable

phone: (602) 264-2273

address: 1366 E. Thomas Road, Suite 108, Phoenix, AZ 85014

website: https://www.aaaphx.org/area-agency-on-aging-programs/hiv-care-directions/

email: info@aaaphx.org

hours: Not published online - call (602) 264-2273 to confirm

Services Offered

- HOPWA Housing Information Services: housing referrals; placement on affordable-housing waitlists; emergency shelter resources; the Housing Information Specialist matches housing needs with appropriate housing providers in Maricopa and Pinal Counties.
- HIV Care Directions (same agency): comprehensive medical case management for people 18 and older with HIV; help enrolling in and maintaining Ryan White Part A services; bus tickets and ride services for medical-care access; help with health insurance premiums and HIV-related medical costs; and assistance finding and maintaining subsidized and affordable housing.
- This is navigation/intake help - not an emergency shelter bed line, and not a direct rent-subsidy program of its own.

Eligibility Requirements

- HIV or AIDS diagnosis - HOPWA requires at least one household member diagnosed with HIV/AIDS.
- Household income at or below 80% of Area Median Income.
- Residency in Maricopa or Pinal County; HIV Care Directions serves people 18 and older. Confirm details at intake.

Population Served

- Adults (18+) living with HIV/AIDS and their households, in Maricopa County (and Pinal County); Mesa residents are within the service area. A key housing-navigation route for medically vulnerable clients who need referrals rather than cash aid.

How to Best Connect

- Call (602) 264-2273 - the Housing Information Services line at Care Directions; this is the intake route for HOPWA housing help.
- Office: 1366 E. Thomas Road, Suite 108, Phoenix, AZ 85014. Agency main line: (602) 264-2255; email info@aaaphx.org; website aaaphx.org (see the HIV Care Directions program page). General 24-hour Senior HELP LINE: (602) 264-4357.
- Hours for this program are not published online - call to confirm before visiting; no walk-in intake process is published.

Important Information to Know

- The City of Phoenix is the local HOPWA funder and administers HOPWA activities for Maricopa and Pinal County residents; the Area Agency is the named access point for housing information, referrals and waitlist placement - not the funder or a shelter provider.
- Placement and timing depend on housing-provider availability and waitlists; no unit or voucher is guaranteed.
- Eligibility (HIV/AIDS diagnosis, income) is verified at intake; confirm current program scope by phone before sending someone.
- No fees are published for the information/referral services; housing obtained through HOPWA programs follows those programs' own rules.

Arizona Complete Health Avondale Resource Center (city resource center & coordinated entry access point) — usable

A city-run family and community resource center in Avondale (Southwest Valley) that is also a Maricopa County coordinated entry location for adults and families who are homeless or at risk, and the intake site for the Southwest Valley IHELP shelter program. Staff assess situations, connect people to shelter and housing options, and provide application and referral help; services are not limited to Avondale residents.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points; Groups: Families, Spanish

phone: 623-333-2703

address: 995 E. Riley Dr., Avondale, AZ 85323

website: https://www.avondaleaz.gov/

email: resourcecenter@avondaleaz.gov

hours: Mon, Wed, Thu 8 a.m.–6 p.m.; Tue 8 a.m.–7 p.m.; Fri 9 a.m.–5 p.m.; Saturday hours are limited — call first.

Services Offered

Housing help at this center is through coordinated entry and referrals rather than its own shelter. It is listed as a Maricopa County coordinated entry location for adults and families experiencing homelessness: staff interview people about their situation, connect them to appropriate services, and discuss shelter options and a housing plan. It is also the intake site for the Southwest Valley IHELP (Interfaith Homeless Emergency Lodging Program) — call to schedule intake — and the Maricopa County Community Action Program office for Avondale, Goodyear, Litchfield Park and Cashion, giving local access to county financial-support programs. The center also offers family and community services such as application assistance and information and referral to housing programs.

Eligibility Requirements

Anyone can ask for information or assistance; services are not limited to Avondale residents. Each program screens separately — coordinated entry, IHELP and county financial-assistance programs each have their own rules. Call 623-333-2703 to confirm what fits a particular situation.

Population Served

Families and individuals in Avondale and the Southwest Valley, including households that are homeless or at risk of homelessness; center programs also serve parents with young children. Services are available in English and Spanish.

How to Best Connect

Call 623-333-2703 or email resourcecenter@avondaleaz.gov, or walk in at 995 E. Riley Dr., Avondale, AZ 85323. Coordinated entry walk-in hours: Mon, Wed, Thu 8 a.m.–6 p.m.; Tue 8 a.m.–7 p.m.; Fri 9 a.m.–5 p.m.; Saturday hours are limited — call first. For Southwest Valley IHELP intake, call 623-333-2703; intake windows are listed as Mon–Tue 8 a.m.–4 p.m., Wed 8 a.m.–noon, Thu–Fri 9 a.m.–noon. For Avondale street outreach/homeless services, call 623-333-2713 or email SONAR@AvondaleAZ.gov.

Important Information to Know

This is an access and assessment point, not a shelter or landlord — a visit does not guarantee a bed, rent money or a unit, and availability and waitlists change. The center is now the Arizona Complete Health Avondale Resource Center at 995 E. Riley Dr.; older listings show the Care1st name, a 328 W. Western Ave. address and the number 623-333-2700 — use 623-333-2703. There is no fee to ask for help; individual programs set their own rules. The center is in the Southwest Valley, a long trip from Mesa — ask whether a phone assessment or a nearer entry point can start the process before traveling.

Arizona Department of Housing (ADOH) — Section 811 PRA (DDD referral) + statewide affordable-housing/LIHTC information — usable

Arizona's state housing agency. It administers the Section 811 Project Rental Assistance (811 PRA) rent subsidies for adults with developmental disabilities who are referred through DES/DDD, and it publishes statewide affordable-housing and Low-Income Housing Tax Credit (LIHTC) information. It is not an open waiting list for the general public.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities, Housing vouchers & subsidized rent programs; Groups: People with disabilities

phone: 602-771-1000

address: 1110 W. Washington St., Phoenix, AZ 85007

website: https://housing.az.gov/

hours: Public questions line: Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m.; affordable-housing search call center (877-428-8844): Monday–Friday, 7:00 a.m.–6:00 p.m. MST

Services Offered

Section 811 Project Rental Assistance (811 PRA): federal rent subsidies attached to units in participating affordable (LIHTC and other federally funded) properties, administered by ADOH as HUD's grantee. Tenants pay about 30% of household income toward rent, and the properties are expected to consider reasonable modifications for disability-related needs. ADOH also publishes LIHTC program materials, lists the online "Search for Affordable Housing in Arizona" route — HousingSearch.AZ.gov (free; market-rate, affordable, accessible and specialized-housing listings, with a call center for people without internet access) — and staffs public customer liaisons to answer housing questions; its site also lists Arizona's 24 housing authorities.

Eligibility Requirements

811 PRA is referral-only, not an open waiting list. For the DDD pathway, a member must be eligible for DDD and ALTCS, be 18–61 years old (some DES documents list 62 as the upper limit; confirm with DDD), have a current Person-Centered Service Plan, meet financial eligibility requirements, meet HUD requirements, and be referred by a DES/DDD Support Coordinator; the member must be willing to live where 811 units are available. The general affordable-housing information on ADOH's website is open to anyone; applying to a specific LIHTC property goes through that property's own tenant-selection process.

Population Served

811 PRA serves adults with developmental disabilities (DES/DDD members) ages 18–61 and their households. Statewide affordable-housing information is available to the general public, including Mesa and Maricopa County residents.

How to Best Connect

ADOH main line: 602-771-1000 (TTY: 7-1-1; older ADOH materials list 602-771-1001); customer liaisons answer public housing questions Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m. Address: 1110 W. Washington St., Phoenix, AZ 85007. Website: housing.az.gov. Affordable-housing search: housingsearch.az.gov or call 877-428-8844, Monday–Friday, 7:00 a.m.–6:00 p.m. MST (TTY 7-1-1), for a listing of subsidized and low-income properties. For 811 PRA, DDD members should contact their DES/DDD Support Coordinator, or the DDD Customer Service Center at 1-844-770-9500 (option 1); ADOH does not take public applications for 811 units. If ADOH's website does not load, call the main line for the current affordable-housing search link.

Important Information to Know

Wait times and unit availability vary. 811 units have been located in Phoenix and other Arizona cities (Phoenix, Tucson, Camp Verde, Bullhead City, Page, Globe), and DDD has reported Maricopa County units of its own; one DDD presentation reported an average of about 7 months from referral to move-in (2023 data — current times may differ). DES materials state that a deposit and first month's rent are the household's responsibility for Section 811 properties, with monthly rent at about 30% of combined household income. The program was documented as active in a November 2025 tenant-selection plan; confirm current details with ADOH or DDD.

Sources: Arizona Department of Housing (ADOH) · ADOH — Search for Affordable Housing in Arizona (HousingSearch.AZ.gov; call center 877-428-8844) · ADOH — Locating Affordable Housing in Arizona (handout: search links; state housing authority list) · Member Housing Opportunities — Arizona DES/DDD · Quick Reference Guide: DDD Affordable Housing (DDD-2313A, 06/25) · HUD Exchange — Section 811 PRA Program Grantees

Arizona Recovery Housing Association (AzRHA) — certified recovery residence search — usable

Statewide association of certified sober-living and halfway-house providers. Its website offers a search for certified recovery residences across Arizona, including the Phoenix metro area. It does not operate homes or take applications; each residence sets its own fees and rules.

Kinds of help: Recovery housing; Groups: People in substance-use recovery

address: 1940 W Chandler Blvd, Suite 2-215, Chandler, AZ 85224 (corporate mailing address)

website: https://www.myazrha.org/

email: membership@myazrha.org

Services Offered

Statewide certification and annual inspections of sober-living and halfway houses; a public "Search Homes" tool on its website for finding certified recovery residences; posted quality standards, code of ethics and a grievance process; and education, training and advocacy for recovery housing. The association works with the Arizona Department of Health Services, the Department of Corrections and local jurisdictions.

Eligibility Requirements

AzRHA has no client eligibility rules — it is an association, not a housing provider. Each residence screens its own applicants (for example, sobriety expectations, house rules, gender-specific homes) and sets its own fees, and homes are required by AzRHA standards to disclose all fees and rules in writing before move-in.

Population Served

Adults in recovery from substance use who are looking for structured sober-living or halfway-house housing. Certified homes are statewide, from Yuma to Flagstaff, with the largest concentrations in the Phoenix and Tucson metro areas.

How to Best Connect

Use the "Search Homes" page at https://www.myazrha.org/home-search. Contact AzRHA by email at membership@myazrha.org; corporate mailing address: 1940 W Chandler Blvd, Suite 2-215, Chandler, AZ 85224. No public phone number is listed on the current site. If the search page does not display results, email AzRHA to ask about currently certified homes; each home must then be contacted directly about openings and cost.

Important Information to Know

AzRHA certification is a private, voluntary credential separate from the Arizona Department of Health Services license that sober living homes must hold; a home's current certification can be checked through the site's search tool. Costs (weekly/monthly fees), bed availability, insurance or payment options, and admission rules are set by each residence and are not published by AzRHA — ask each home directly. Certification does not guarantee a bed or eligibility. AzRHA operates a grievance statement for concerns about certified homes.

Sources: Arizona Recovery Housing Association (AzRHA) — About · AzRHA — Find Certified Recovery Housing / Search Homes · AzRHA — Quality & Operational Standards (certification, grievance, fee disclosure) · NARR — Arizona Recovery Housing Association affiliate listing · Arizona DHS — Sober Living Homes licensing fact sheet (AzRHA certification is a recognized certifying organization)

ARM of Save the Family — Affordable Rental Housing (Mesa and Maricopa County) — usable

ARM (Affordable Rental Movement) is Save the Family's affiliated affordable-housing nonprofit: more than 170 scattered single-family rental homes across Mesa, Tempe, Scottsdale, Gilbert and Chandler, plus co-developed affordable communities — including Escobedo at Verde Vista in Mesa (families and individuals, with a focus on people facing behavioral and mental-health challenges), Valor on 8th in Tempe (veteran women and families) and the upcoming Phoenix Scholar House. Supportive services are available to ARM residents. The ARM waitlist is temporarily closed to new applications — call for status.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities; Groups: Families, Veterans

phone: (480) 898-0228

address: 125 E. University Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://savethefamily.org/

hours: Office: Monday–Friday 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m.; closed Saturday and Sunday.

Services Offered

- Permanent affordable rental housing through more than 170 scattered single-family homes across Mesa, Tempe, Scottsdale, Gilbert, and Chandler (ARM page; 2025 annual report).
- Co-developed affordable communities: Escobedo at Verde Vista in Mesa (families and individuals, with a focus on people experiencing behavioral and mental-health challenges); Valor on 8th (created with veteran women and families in mind); and the upcoming Phoenix Scholar House (planned to provide affordable housing along with academic and family support services for low-income single parents with children).
- Each complex includes a Save the Family Resource Center where a case manager provides workforce-development workshops and access to community resources; Save the Family's supportive services — career help, budgeting, parenting and life-skills classes, children's programming, and mental-health support — are available to ARM residents.
- Rental assistance is provided through HUD HOME funds, so rent is based on household income.

Eligibility Requirements

- Income at or below 60% of the HOME income limits (2025 limits effective June 1, 2025).
- Applicants must be employed and/or have stable, verifiable income; when a unit is available, at least six months of verifiable income and financial documentation is required for all household members over 18.
- No pets allowed; service and therapy/emotional support animals require supporting documentation.
- Reasonable accommodations can be requested, including help reading/completing the application and Spanish-to-English translation onsite.
- The waitlist is currently closed to new applications (temporarily, due to high volume) — ask about reopening. Existing applicants can only confirm whether they are on the list, and address/contact changes must be submitted in writing.

Population Served

Low-income working families and individuals in the East Valley. Specific communities focus on particular populations: Escobedo at Verde Vista (families and individuals, including people with behavioral and mental-health challenges), Valor on 8th (veteran women and families), and Phoenix Scholar House (single parents with children, in development).

How to Best Connect

- Call (480) 898-0228 Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–5 p.m., and ask about 'ARM of Save the Family,' the waitlist status, and how to apply when it reopens.
- Online: savethefamily.org/arm for ARM details, the income chart, and waitlist notices; the office is at 125 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85201.
- For existing applications or address/contact updates, use the email or mail instructions on the ARM page — phone changes are not accepted.
- If your family is currently experiencing homelessness and needs Save the Family's supportive housing programs, the county's family entry point is the Family Housing Hub, 3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, 602-595-8700. ARM is the organization's affordable-housing side, separate from its temporary and rapid-rehousing programs.

Important Information to Know

- ARM is affordable rental housing, not emergency shelter or short-term rental assistance; rent is income-based (amounts are not published — ask).
- The waitlist is temporarily closed to new applications at the time of this research; the ARM page says it will reopen at a future date.
- Vacancies are unpredictable and ARM cannot anticipate where they will occur; applicants who decline two offered units are removed from the waitlist.
- ARM is funded through HUD; housing is also supported by the Maricopa County HOME Consortium, Arizona Department of Housing, and City of Mesa HOME funds.
- No pets (with service/emotional-support animal documentation exceptions).

Sources: ARM of Save the Family — program page (Affordable Rental Movement; eligibility; waitlist closed; scattered-site cities; contact and hours) · Save the Family — How We Help (three housing programs; supportive services; Mesa office, hours, phone) · Save the Family 2025 Impact Report — ARM (170+ scattered homes; co-developed communities; Resource Centers; 6–12 month support) · Save the Family — Our Programs (Valor on Eighth; Escobedo children's programming; ARM supportive services; eviction prevention) · SRP community packet (Aug 2025) — Save the Family permanent affordable housing communities and supportive services

AZCEND — East Valley shelter (I-HELP), rent & utility assistance, senior home sharing — usable

East Valley nonprofit (formerly the Chandler Christian Community Center, renamed AZCEND in 2017) that runs nightly emergency shelter (I-HELP) for adults, rent/utility/mortgage and eviction-prevention assistance, and a Senior Home Sharing program. Rent/utility help covers Chandler, Sun Lakes, Queen Creek and Gilbert; Senior Home Sharing includes Mesa, Tempe, Chandler and Gilbert.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter, Rent, utility & deposit crisis assistance; Groups: Seniors, Homeless

phone: (480) 963-1423

address: 345 S California St, Chandler, AZ 85225 (main office; Gilbert CAP office: 132 W Bruce Ave, Gilbert, AZ 85233)

website: https://azcend.org/

email: info@azcend.org

hours: Admin/CAP (Chandler): Mon–Fri 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m., Tue until 6 p.m. CAP (Gilbert): Mon 7:30 a.m.–6 p.m., Tue–Thu 7:30 a.m.–5 p.m., Fri 8:30 a.m.–noon

Services Offered

I-HELP shelter: safe shelter, a hot meal and a shower every night for adults experiencing homelessness, with one-on-one case management that includes job placement, medical referrals, bus passes, SNAP and AHCCCS benefits help, and housing assistance. Community Action Program (CAP): rent, water/gas/electric, mortgage and eviction-prevention financial assistance, including the Maricopa County "HSRUA3" program (up to one month of past-due rent plus late fees). Gilbert Utility Discount Program: a $30/month water-bill discount for income-eligible Gilbert residents. East Valley Senior Home Sharing (a partnership between Aster Aging, AZCEND and Tempe Community Action Agency): matches adults 50+ who have a home with those looking for one so they can share living costs, serving Chandler, Gilbert, Mesa and Tempe. AZCEND also runs a food bank, heat relief, showers/laundry, a Family Resource Center and senior centers.

Eligibility Requirements

I-HELP: adults experiencing homelessness; an interview is required before entering, so call for bed availability. CAP rent/utility aid: residents of Chandler, Sun Lakes and Queen Creek (Chandler office) or Gilbert (Gilbert office) only; HSRUA3 prioritizes households at or below 200% of the federal poverty level, and applicants who already received Emergency Rental Assistance rent payments are not eligible (utility-only ERA recipients are eligible). Documents required include photo ID, current lease, proof of residence, 30 days of income records, and evidence of hardship or housing instability; the landlord must provide a Maricopa County vendor code on the Landlord Verification Form or complete a W-9. Senior Home Sharing: age 50+, able to care for oneself, able to cover basic living expenses, pass a background check, and provide proof of income. Gilbert utility discount: Gilbert water account holder with household income at or below 120% of area median income.

Population Served

Adults experiencing homelessness (I-HELP); households at risk of eviction or utility shut-off in Chandler, Sun Lakes, Queen Creek and Gilbert; and seniors 50+ across Chandler, Gilbert, Mesa and Tempe (home sharing). Mesa residents are not eligible for the CAP rent/utility assistance but are included in the Senior Home Sharing program; call to confirm whether a Mesa adult can be served by I-HELP.

How to Best Connect

I-HELP shelter: call Valentina Garcia at 480-963-1423 x112 to schedule the required interview and check bed availability. Rent/utilities: Chandler/Sun Lakes/Queen Creek — 480-963-1423 x126 or cap@azcend.org; Gilbert — 480-892-5331 x200 or cap@azcend.org. Senior Home Sharing: (480) 389-1284, info@evseniorhomesharing.org or evseniorhomesharing.org. Main office: 345 S California St, Chandler, AZ 85225; (480) 963-1423; info@azcend.org; azcend.org. Admin hours Monday–Friday 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m.

Important Information to Know

Mesa residents are not eligible for AZCEND's CAP rent/utility assistance (Chandler, Sun Lakes, Queen Creek and Gilbert only), but the Senior Home Sharing program specifically includes Mesa. HSRUA3 pays up to one month of past-due rent — it is not an ongoing subsidy. No fees are mentioned for the shelter or assistance programs. Capacity, waitlists and current funding are not published, so call before sending a client. AZCEND's current program list does not include an open rapid-rehousing program; its housing help is the shelter/case-management and rent/utility programs above.

Sources: AZCEND — Shelter (I-HELP) · AZCEND — Rent & Utilities: Chandler, Sun Lakes & Queen Creek · AZCEND — Rent & Utilities: Gilbert · AZCEND — East Valley Senior Home Sharing · AZCEND — Locations & Hours · AZCEND — Our Transformation (formerly Chandler Christian Community Center; renamed AZCEND in 2017) · AZCEND — Home

Brian Garcia Welcome Center – single adult coordinated entry (Keys to Change) — usable

Walk-up entry point on Key Campus in downtown Phoenix for single adults experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Staff provide diversion, vulnerability screening and assessment, shelter referrals, and Coordinated Entry into housing, plus connections to on-campus health, ID, benefits, employment, and other services. Free, no documents needed to start, and open around the clock.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points; Groups: Single adults

phone: 602-883-4509 (call ahead); 602-229-5155 (Key Campus main line); older listings also show 602-299-5155

address: 206 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007

website: https://keystochangeaz.org/get-help/

hours: Open 24 hours a day, 7 days a week; current guidance is to visit 7am–11pm to begin an assessment and plan

Services Offered

In-person help for single adults experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness in Maricopa County: diversion (short- and long-term housing planning), vulnerability screening and assessment, shelter referrals, and Coordinated Entry into housing. The Welcome Center is the lead access point for single-adult Coordinated Entry, and the Keys to Change Housing Match team manages the county's By-Name List used to prioritize people for housing and vouchers. Staff also connect clients to Key Campus services, including replacement ID and birth certificates, mail, storage, showers, medical and dental care, mental health and substance use services, DES state benefits, Social Security (SOAR) help, employment services, legal help and Homeless Court, meals, and pet services.

Eligibility Requirements

Single adults 18 and older who are experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County; Keys to Change describes its services as open to single adults at risk of homelessness. The Welcome Center does not serve adults with minor children. No documents are required to begin, there is no fee, and services are provided in person (not over the phone). Staff complete diversion and screening to determine next steps.

Population Served

Single adults 18 and older from across Maricopa County, including Mesa residents; people with medical, mental-health, and other high needs are among those connected to services on the Key Campus. The Welcome Center does not serve adults with minor children. Families with children, youth under 18, veterans, and domestic-violence survivors have separate official routes (see Important Information to Know).

How to Best Connect

Walk in to the Brian Garcia Welcome Center at 206 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007, on Key Campus — open 24 hours a day, 7 days a week, with current guidance to visit any time from 7am–11pm to begin an assessment and create a plan. Call ahead: 602-883-4509; Key Campus main line: 602-229-5155. No appointment is needed, and no documents are required to begin. Help is delivered in person — the phone line is for calling ahead. If the phone line does not connect, you can visit in person; the Welcome Center is open around the clock. From Mesa or elsewhere in the county, call 2-1-1 (1-877-211-8661) to find the nearest Maricopa County Coordinated Entry access point.

Important Information to Know

Free; no documents required to start. A shelter bed or housing placement is not guaranteed — resources are limited and demand is high, so a referral starts a process that may include waiting. Phone numbers in circulation differ: the official call-ahead number is 602-883-4509, the campus main line is 602-229-5155, and some older listings show 602-299-5155 — use the official numbers. Hours nuance: the Welcome Center is open 24/7, while current guidance says assessment visits run 7am–11pm. Other official routes: families with children — Family Housing Hub, 602-595-8700 (waiting list; TDD 602-759-1896); youth 12–17 — 24/7 Youth Hotline, 602-841-5799; veterans — VA Community Resource and Referral Center, 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, 602-248-6040, Mon–Fri 7:30am–4:30pm (national line: 1-877-424-3838); domestic violence — call shelters directly, because the countywide 24/7 screening line (480-890-3039) stopped operating as a centralized intake on May 15, 2026 and now plays a recorded message (statewide help line 602-279-2980 or text 520-720-3383, business hours; National Domestic Violence Hotline 1-800-799-7233; provider list at ArizonaSurvivors.org). The Welcome Center also hosts part-time veteran services through the Traumatic Brain Injury Alliance.

Sources: Keys to Change — Get Help (Welcome Center call-ahead 602-883-4509; 206 S. 12th Ave.) · Keys to Change - Key Campus services (shelter, Welcome Center, housing match) · Keys to Change – 2-1-1 Arizona record (last assured 03/01/2026; eligibility, fees, in-person service, no documents) · City of Tolleson — Housing Resources & Coordinated Entry Locations · Keys to Change / Human Services Campus – CauseIQ profile · 12News - Arizona domestic violence hotline discontinued (May 2026) · KOLD – Arizona domestic violence hotline to shut down (ACESDV statement; recorded message and ArizonaSurvivors.org transition)

Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) — Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention (housing programs) — usable

CASS's housing programs (Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) help individuals and families who are homeless or at risk of homelessness secure or keep housing, with short-term rent and utility help, deposits, case management, landlord mediation and eviction prevention. Enrollment goes through Maricopa County's coordinated entry system — call 2-1-1.

Kinds of help: Rent and deposit help; Groups: Families, Single adults

phone: 2-1-1 / 877-211-8661 (coordinated entry); 602-256-6945 (CASS main)

address: 230 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (CASS campus; housing intake by phone/coordinated entry)

website: https://www.cassaz.org/services

hours: No posted hours — access by phone/assessment; call 2-1-1.

Services Offered

CASS's housing programs (Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) provide short-term rental and utility assistance, security-deposit support, case management and tenancy-skills training, landlord mediation and eviction prevention, benefits enrollment and budgeting support, and housing navigation. They are designed to help individuals and families secure housing quickly or avoid returning to homelessness, and CASS runs them with Maricopa County's Coordinated Entry System.

Eligibility Requirements

Households experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness (including people facing eviction). Enrollment is through a coordinated entry assessment, and help depends on program eligibility, available funding and capacity — call 2-1-1 to start. No fee is charged to clients; the assistance itself is time-limited and funding-dependent.

Population Served

Single adults and families, including households with children, across Maricopa County.

How to Best Connect

Call 2-1-1 or 877-211-8661, or visit 211Arizona.org, to ask for housing and rental assistance and a coordinated entry assessment. If you are already in a CASS shelter or working with a CASS case manager, ask about rapid re-housing directly. General CASS line: 602-256-6945; if facing eviction, see AZEvictionHelp.org. CASS asks that its website contact form not be used for housing or rental requests.

Important Information to Know

An assessment does not guarantee rent help or housing — funds and openings change, so 2-1-1 can say what is currently available. Earlier materials (a 2021 state presentation) used the name "Flexible Housing Assistance"; the current public name is "Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention" — use that when asking. The program serves both individuals and families; shelter clients should loop in their case manager early.

Sources: CASS — Homeless Services (Single Adult Shelter; Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) · CASS — Contact (Welcome Center 602-229-5155; 2-1-1 for housing)

Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) — Single Adult Shelter (main campus) — usable

Arizona's largest single-adult emergency shelter, run by Central Arizona Shelter Services on the Human Services Campus in Phoenix. Open 24/7/365 with same-day placement subject to availability; single adults 18+ access it through the Brian Garcia Welcome Center (602-229-5155), and current bed availability can be checked by calling 211.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Homeless

phone: 602-229-5155 (Welcome Center intake) / 602-256-6945 (main)

address: 230 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (intake campus: 204 S. 12th Ave.; some listings show 206 S. 12th Ave.)

website: https://www.cassaz.org/get-help

hours: Shelter open 24 hours a day, 365 days a year; call for current intake windows.

Services Offered

Emergency shelter for single adults: a low-barrier bed, showers, restrooms and hygiene supplies, meals, case management and housing navigation, and connections to healthcare, public benefits and employment services. On-campus help includes mail service, help obtaining IDs and enrolling in benefits, behavioral-health and recovery referrals, and veterans case management. The shelter houses up to about 600 men and women at a time and is open around the clock.

Eligibility Requirements

Single adults (men and women) age 18 and older who are experiencing homelessness. Intake is described as low-barrier. Policies to confirm with the Welcome Center: guests must be able to manage their daily activities; pets are allowed with a county license and vaccination record; registered sex offenders are not accepted. There is no fee.

Population Served

Single adults 18 and older of all backgrounds, including young adults, seniors and veterans; services available in English and Spanish.

How to Best Connect

Call the Brian Garcia Welcome Center at 602-229-5155 (the number CASS and Maricopa County list) — CASS says intakes and information are obtained only by calling this number — or go in person to the Welcome Center on Key Campus, 204 S. 12th Ave. (some listings show 206 S. 12th Ave.), Phoenix, AZ 85007, to complete intake and ask to be directed to the CASS shelter at 230 S. 12th Ave. Keys to Change, which operates the Welcome Center, also lists a call-ahead line at 602-883-4509. Placement is same-day and subject to availability; to check coordinated shelter bed availability, call 211. Bring a photo ID and medications if possible; help obtaining replacements is available. Families with children should call the Family Housing Hub through 211; adults 55+ can ask about The Haven. Main CASS line: 602-256-6945.

Important Information to Know

Shelter is free, and beds cannot be reserved by phone — demand is high, so call 211 or the Welcome Center to check availability before traveling from Mesa. Welcome Center phone listings vary slightly (CASS and Maricopa County: 602-229-5155; Keys to Change call-ahead: 602-883-4509; some older guides: 602-299-5155) — if one number doesn't connect, try the other. Some community guides describe a 90-day shelter stay; CASS's current pages do not state a fixed length of stay — ask at intake. One CASS web page shows a mistyped "604" phone number; the correct main line is 602-256-6945. The shelter is on a Central Phoenix campus; plan transportation.

Sources: CASS — Homeless Services (Single Adult Shelter; Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) · CASS — Get Help (single adult intake; same-day placement subject to availability) · CASS — Contact (Welcome Center 602-229-5155; 2-1-1 for housing) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · Keys to Change — Get Help (Welcome Center call-ahead 602-883-4509; 206 S. 12th Ave.) · 211 Arizona — CASS Single Adult Shelter listing (eligibility, fees, hours; last assured February 2026) · City of Tolleson — Shelters (Southwest Valley IHELP intake at Avondale Resource Center)

Central Arizona Shelter Services · Norton & Ramsey Social Justice Empowerment Center (Glendale coordinated entry access point) — needs-resolution

The Norton & Ramsey Social Justice Empowerment Center (NREC) at 7031 N 56th Ave, Glendale is Central Arizona Shelter Services' West Valley location, listed by several current resources (NAC's access-point page, Justa Center and the City of Tolleson) as a Maricopa County coordinated entry access point for single adults — a walk-in housing assessment and referral into shelter and housing programs.
