Chicanos Por La Causa (CPLC) - Housing & Community Development (income-restricted affordable housing communities) — usable

Chicanos Por La Causa (CPLC) develops and manages income-restricted affordable apartment communities in metro Phoenix and Mesa - including Ascent on Broadway in South Phoenix (161 apartments at 40-60% AMI, opened November 2025) and the planned Mesa Royale on W. Main St. in Mesa (89 apartments planned for families at or below 60% AMI, still in development). Applications and waitlists are handled per community; the contact currently listed for CPLC's affordable-housing/real-estate division is Tiempo, Inc. (602-252-0482).

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities; Groups: No group assigned

phone: 602-252-0482 (Tiempo, Inc. - CPLC real-estate/affordable-housing contact); 602-257-0700 (CPLC main)

address: Tiempo, Inc. (CPLC real-estate contact): 1008 E. Buckeye Road, Suite #100, Phoenix, AZ 85034; Mesa Royale site: West Main Street between N. Country Club Dr. and N. Alma School Rd. (630-668 W. Main St.), Mesa, AZ

website: https://cplc.org/programs/affordable-housing

hours: No public leasing hours published - call Tiempo/CPLC to confirm; Mesa Royale's 2024 City of Mesa plan-of-operation filing described planned weekday business hours (Monday-Friday, 9 a.m.-5 p.m.).

Services Offered

- CPLC's real estate/Housing & Community Development division develops, owns, and manages income-restricted affordable rental communities in metro Phoenix and Mesa, including Low-Income Housing Tax Credit (LIHTC) properties; CPLC's affordable-housing page reports its multifamily properties are home to 3,471 residents, plus 35 families in single-family homes, and seven senior complexes.
- Mesa example: Mesa Royale on West Main Street in Mesa (630-668 W. Main St., between N. Country Club Dr. and N. Alma School Rd.) - a redevelopment that plans 89 affordable apartment units for families at or below 60% of Area Median Income, plus for-sale townhomes (Nuevas Vistas: 56 townhomes for first-time buyers at or below 80% AMI; 10 completed as of March 2025). CPLC planned to seek 2026 LIHTC funding; construction timing had not been announced as of March 2025.
- Phoenix example: Ascent on Broadway in South Phoenix - 161 affordable apartments (10 studios, 26 one-bedroom, 86 two-bedroom, 39 three-bedroom) near the light rail at Central Avenue, reserved for households at or below 40%, 50%, or 60% AMI; 140 units were already occupied when it opened in November 2025.
- CPLC also offers related housing programs (housing counseling, home lending, homelessness services); ask about the right program when contacting CPLC.

Eligibility Requirements

- Households must meet each community's income limits and screening rules; rent bands are set by program rules (for example, LIHTC rents; the 40/50/60% AMI bands at Ascent on Broadway; the planned at-or-below-60% AMI apartments at Mesa Royale).
- Income limits vary by property, unit size, and household size, and each community has its own application and waitlist; no single CPLC-wide waitlist is described publicly.

Population Served

- Low- and moderate-income individuals and families in metro Phoenix and Mesa who need affordable apartments; specific communities may target particular income bands (Ascent on Broadway includes family-sized two- and three-bedroom units; Mesa Royale is planned as family affordable housing; CPLC also reports operating seven senior complexes).
- CPLC states its services are offered to all people regardless of ethnic background while maintaining special competence serving the Latino community.

How to Best Connect

- Start with Tiempo, Inc., the contact listed for CPLC's affordable-housing/real-estate division: call 602-252-0482; office at 1008 E. Buckeye Road, Suite #100, Phoenix, AZ 85034; tiempoinc.com. CPLC's main line is 602-257-0700 if you need routing.
- For Ascent on Broadway, ask CPLC/Tiempo about current leasing and applications; for Mesa Royale, ask about the development timeline and future leasing - it is not yet a confirmed open community.
- CPLC's 2024 Mesa project filings listed a project contact (Evelyn.Guerrero@cplc.org, 602-257-6727) for the Mesa development; that filing is from 2024 and may be out of date - the Tiempo contact is the current route listed on CPLC's site.
- Ask specifically about which communities have open waitlists, current rents and deposits, income limits, and application steps.

Important Information to Know

- Rents are income-restricted and set by program rules - below market but not uniform; exact rents, deposits, and utility allowances must be confirmed with each property.
- Waitlists and current availability were not verified for any CPLC community, and new communities open periodically; ask about both existing communities and upcoming openings.
- Mesa Royale is still in development: as of March 2025, CPLC planned to apply for 2026 LIHTC funding and construction timing had not been announced; a 2024 City of Mesa plan-of-operation filing described planned weekday business hours and management by Tiempo, but there is no confirmed leasing office yet.
- Name caution: a separate, unrelated 'Mesa Royale Apartments' at 1825 N. Stapley Dr. in Mesa is an existing 55+ community - CPLC's Mesa Royale is the West Main Street redevelopment.
- The general phone line may route through a switchboard; the Tiempo contact above is the division contact listed on CPLC's current affordable-housing page.
- Eligibility and unit availability are determined by the property and are not guaranteed.

Sources: CPLC - Affordable Housing program (Tiempo, Inc. contact; 3,471 multifamily residents; seven senior complexes) · Maricopa County - Celebrating the opening of Ascent on Broadway (Nov. 10, 2025: 161 units; 140 occupied; 40-60% AMI) · Arizona Republic/azcentral - Mesa Royale plans near Main and Date (Mar. 31, 2025: 89 units at or below 60% AMI; 2026 LIHTC; timing unclear) · Mesa Royale - Plan of Operation (City of Mesa records; planned weekday hours; Tiempo management) · LISC - Mesa Royale Mobile Home Park (CPLC acquisition and redevelopment)

Child Crisis Arizona · Emergency children's shelter — Mesa (Whiteman's Safe Haven, ages 2–10) — usable

A licensed emergency shelter in Mesa for children ages 2–10 who have experienced abuse or neglect or whose families are in crisis. Children receive 24/7 care, meals, clothing, medical checks and counseling while arrangements are made; referrals come from the Arizona Department of Child Safety, Tribal Social Services, and from caregivers who need temporary safe care for a child. Call ahead about space.

Kinds of help: Children & youth crisis care; Groups: Youth

phone: (480) 969-2308; main line (480) 834-9424

address: 604 W 9th St, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://www.childcrisisaz.org/what-we-do/emergency-residential-services

email: info@childcrisisaz.org

hours: Open 24 hours a day, 7 days a week, year-round

Services Offered

A 24-hour, 7-days-a-week emergency shelter for young children: safe housing with nurturing care, balanced meals and snacks, clothing and personal-care items, medical screenings (including vaccinations and doctor or dentist visits as needed), educational support and counseling, plus enrichment activities. Child Crisis Arizona describes it as the only licensed emergency children's shelter facility for ages 2 through 10 in Maricopa County.

Eligibility Requirements

Children ages 2 through 10. Children are placed through referrals from the Arizona Department of Child Safety and Tribal Social Services, and also by parents or caregivers who cannot provide care and need a safe place temporarily. The program is described as open to anyone who needs help caring for their children, based on availability.

Population Served

Young children (ages 2–10) in Maricopa County whose families are in crisis or who have experienced abuse or neglect. It is not an adult, teen or family-housing program.

How to Best Connect

Call the Mesa shelter at (480) 969-2308 or Child Crisis Arizona's main line at (480) 834-9424 to ask about space and intake; email info@childcrisisaz.org for general questions. Location: 604 W 9th St, Mesa, AZ 85201 - the shelter has also been listed in community guides as 'Whiteman's Safe Haven for Children.' The shelter runs 24/7, but intake is coordinated by phone, so call before going. To report suspected abuse or neglect, call the Arizona Child Abuse Hotline at 1-888-767-2445; youth needing help can text 'safe' plus their location to 4HELP (44357).

Important Information to Know

The program's current community listing (findhelp, reviewed September 2026) lists the shelter as free to families; cost should not be a barrier - confirm at intake. Capacity is limited and changes daily, and child-welfare placements are prioritized. If the 480-969-2308 shelter line is not answered, use the main line (480) 834-9424. This is an emergency children's shelter, not a domestic-violence or adult shelter; staff can point elsewhere if the situation does not fit.

Sources: Child Crisis Arizona — Emergency Residential Services (group home description, current ages 11–17) · Child Crisis Arizona — Contact Us (phone, email, hotlines) · findhelp — Child Crisis Arizona (Mesa) program listing (shelter address, phone, eligibility, free; last reviewed Sep 24, 2026) · Boys & Girls Clubs of Arizona — Vivienda housing guide (2025) listing 'Whiteman's Safe Haven for Children', 604 W. 9th St., 480-969-2308

Child Crisis Arizona · Group home for teen boys in foster care (ages 11–17, Mesa) — usable

A Mesa group home run by Child Crisis Arizona that provides around-the-clock care for teenage boys in the foster care system (currently described as ages 11–17), including housing, meals, supervision and coaching in life skills. Placement is arranged through the child-welfare system rather than by walk-in, so families and caseworkers should call Child Crisis Arizona's main line to ask about referrals.

Kinds of help: Children & youth crisis care; Groups: Youth

phone: (480) 834-9424

address: Mesa, AZ (exact address not published; placement is arranged through Child Crisis Arizona and child-welfare caseworkers)

website: https://www.childcrisisaz.org/what-we-do/emergency-residential-services

email: info@childcrisisaz.org

hours: 24/7 residential care; inquiry-line hours are not published

Services Offered

Around-the-clock (24/7) residential group-home care for teenage boys in the foster care system: a stable home with supervision and meals, physical and mental health support, and life-skills guidance to prepare for adulthood. Child Crisis Arizona describes the home as providing wraparound care while the boys are in its program.

Eligibility Requirements

Teenage boys in the foster care system; Child Crisis Arizona's current program page says ages 11–17 (older materials have listed 10–15, 10–18 or 11–18 over the years, so confirm the current range). Placements are arranged through the child-welfare system - the Arizona Department of Child Safety, Tribal Social Services or the youth's foster-care team - rather than through an open community application. Availability depends on placement need and space.

Population Served

Teenage boys in foster care; the home is in Mesa (City of Mesa reports describe the group home as located in Mesa and operated 24/7) and serves the greater Maricopa County child-welfare system. This is not an adult housing program and it does not accept walk-in requests for shelter.

How to Best Connect

Call Child Crisis Arizona at (480) 834-9424 or email info@childcrisisaz.org and ask about the boys' group home and how placements are made. The home's exact address is not published, and placement runs through the youth's case manager (DCS or tribal social services). If a child is in immediate danger, call 911; to report abuse or neglect, call the Arizona Child Abuse Hotline at 1-888-767-2445. Youth in crisis can text 'safe' and their location to 4HELP (44357) to be connected with a Safe Place contact.

Important Information to Know

This is a placement-based home, not a drop-in shelter - a caseworker referral is the normal path. Current bed capacity and openings are not published; confirm availability when you call. No cost to families is published (placements are arranged through the child-welfare system). Age-range wording varies across materials (current page says 11–17; older materials say 10–15, 10–18 or 11–18), so verify the current cutoff.

Sources: Child Crisis Arizona — Emergency Residential Services (group home description, current ages 11–17) · Child Crisis Arizona — Contact Us (phone, email, hotlines) · Child Crisis Arizona — Who We Are (organization overview; shelter/stabilization services overall ages 2–21) · Child Crisis Arizona — Fall 2021 newsletter (group home described for boys ages 11–18) · City of Mesa — City Council report (April 2019): group home for adolescent boys located in Mesa, operated 24/7, placed by DCS or Tribal Social Services · City of Mesa — FY 2025/2026 Funding Process (Human Services/ABC: 'My Sister's Place: Family-Based Healing for Survivors of Domestic Violence')

Circle the City · Medical respite centers and care for adults experiencing homelessness — usable

Circle the City provides medical respite care in Phoenix for adults experiencing homelessness who are recovering from an acute illness, injury or surgery: a safe place to stay with medical care when someone is too sick for the street or a shelter but not sick enough to remain in a hospital. Two 50-bed centers and county-wide mobile medical teams serve Maricopa County; referrals usually come through hospitals, outreach teams or a call to intake.

Kinds of help: Health-linked housing (respite & HIV/AIDS); Groups: Medically vulnerable

phone: 602-776-0776 (administration/intake); Downtown respite 602-254-4282 (after-hours 602-884-4240); Midtown respite 602-776-9000 (after-hours 480-434-2690); mobile medical care 602-516-6825

address: Downtown Medical Respite Center: 210 S 12th Ave, Phoenix, AZ 85007; Midtown Medical Respite Center: 333 W Indian School Rd, Phoenix, AZ 85013

website: https://circlethecity.org/

hours: Administration and intake Monday–Friday 8:30 a.m.–5 p.m.; 24/7 care for admitted respite patients

Services Offered

Medical respite care: a safe place to stay while recovering from an acute illness, injury or surgery, with medical and nursing care, meals, physical therapy, behavioral-health support and help planning next steps. Two 50-bed centers (Downtown and Midtown Phoenix) serve adults who are too sick to recover on the street or in a shelter but not sick enough to stay in a hospital. Circle the City also runs primary-care clinics, mobile medical units and street-medicine teams across Maricopa County, plus hospital-based health navigators who connect patients to services at discharge.

Eligibility Requirements

Adults 18 and older who are homeless or about to become homeless, with acute or active medical needs expected to improve within about 4–6 weeks (or who need end-of-life care), and who can live in a group setting, follow facility rules and take part in their own care. Referrals typically come from a hospital or emergency-room team, community outreach workers, or family; hospital social workers and case managers can help with the process. Health insurance is not required.

Population Served

People experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including medically vulnerable adults discharged from hospitals with nowhere safe to recover. Mobile medical teams reach people living outdoors and in shelters county-wide.

How to Best Connect

Call Circle the City administration and intake at 602-776-0776 (Monday–Friday 8:30 a.m.–5 p.m.). Downtown Medical Respite Center: 210 S 12th Ave, Phoenix, 602-254-4282 (after-hours 602-884-4240). Midtown Medical Respite Center: 333 W Indian School Rd, Phoenix, 602-776-9000 (after-hours 480-434-2690). Ask a hospital social worker or case manager to refer directly; health navigators can be reached at 602-325-3535 or healthnavigators@circlethecity.org; mobile medical care is at 602-516-6825. Call before going - admission is coordinated, and the centers are not walk-in shelters.

Important Information to Know

Medical respite is free and does not require insurance, but there must be an open bed and the medical-need criteria must be met; space is limited. Stays are tied to recovery (typically weeks), not long-term housing, and discharge planning includes help finding the next place to stay. The centers are in central Phoenix, and the East Valley (including Mesa) has had no medical respite beds: Circle the City is opening an 85-bed East Valley Medical Respite Center in Mesa in fall 2026, so ask whether the new center is accepting patients yet. Confirm current referral routes and bed availability by phone; intake details can change.

Sources: Circle the City — official website (two 50-bed respite programs; county-wide mobile teams) · Circle the City — Contact Us (center addresses, phones, after-hours and administration hours) · Circle the City — 'The Hope and Healing Challenge' (third Medical Respite Center opening in the East Valley in fall 2026, 85 patients) · Circle the City — Growing East campaign (Mesa's first East Valley Medical Respite Center; 85-bed facility; East Valley has had no medical respite beds) · Circle the City — 2024 Impact Report (services and center locations/phones) · 211 Arizona — Circle the City (eligibility, hours, contact; last assured 02/01/2026) · 211 Arizona — Circle the City Downtown Medical Respite Center (eligibility, intake, fees; last assured 02/01/2026) · Circle the City — Community Resource Guide (health navigator contact)

City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Mesa Homeless Resource Line (480-644-HOPE) — usable

The City of Mesa's phone line for people who are experiencing homelessness in Mesa and for community members with questions or concerns. Staff answer calls, take referrals, and connect people to shelter, street outreach, and other homeless services.

Kinds of help: Housing navigation; Groups: Homeless

phone: (480) 644-4673

website: https://www.mesaaz.gov/Resident-Resources/Housing/Human-Services/Office-of-Homeless-Solutions

hours: Monitored Monday-Thursday; a City program document lists 7 a.m.-6 p.m. Current hours are not posted - confirm when calling. Non-emergency line.

Services Offered

A staffed navigation and referral line for homelessness in Mesa: people who are homeless (or someone calling on their behalf) can get help and be routed to shelter, street outreach and other services, and community members can ask questions or offer to help. The line is the City's public front door to the Office of Homeless Solutions. It is not itself a shelter, does not guarantee a bed or housing, and is not an emergency or crisis line.

Eligibility Requirements

No eligibility rules are published - the line is open to people experiencing homelessness in Mesa and to community members with questions, concerns or offers to help.

Population Served

People experiencing homelessness in Mesa, plus Mesa residents, businesses and others who need information about homelessness or want to help.

How to Best Connect

Call (480) 644-HOPE - (480) 644-4673 - Monday through Thursday (a City program document lists 7 a.m.-6 p.m.; confirm current hours when you call). For emergencies call 911; for a mental-health crisis, the Crisis Response Network is available 24/7 at 602-222-9444; for non-emergency police matters call 480-644-2211.

Important Information to Know

This is a referral line, not a walk-in shelter - shelter and housing options are limited and often have waiting lists. Published city materials say the line is monitored Monday-Thursday (one program document lists 7 a.m.-6 p.m.), but current hours are not posted; older city material described the line as paired with the shelter hotline for immediate assistance around the clock, so confirm hours when calling. If no one answers, try again on a weekday or use the crisis numbers above. The line does not publish an email; written questions about the Off the Streets shelter program can go to offthestreets@mesaaz.gov.

Sources: City of Mesa - Homeless Resources (helplines and crisis numbers) · City of Mesa - Resources and Information (Resource Line description) · City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Community Bridges, Inc. - Off the Streets Operations and Maintenance Plan (Mesa Legistar; resource line monitored M-Th 7 a.m.-6 p.m.) · City of Mesa - Addressing Homelessness presentation (Mon-Thu business-hour response; non-emergency) · City of Mesa - Council study session minutes, February 23, 2023 (older 'paired ... immediate assistance 24 hours' description)

City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Off the Streets emergency shelter (Windermere Hotel site) — usable

Mesa's main emergency shelter and temporary-housing program for people who are unsheltered: hotel rooms at the Windermere Hotel site (65 rooms / 111 beds) with meals, 24/7 on-site staffing, case management and a housing plan, generally up to about 90 days. Entry is by referral only - there are no walk-up beds; referrals run through Mesa first responders and nonprofit partners via the Mesa Homeless Resource Line. In 2026 the City selected Mercy House to take over day-to-day operations from Community Bridges, Inc., and decided to keep the program at the Windermere rather than move it to the city-owned Sunaire Hotel.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Families, Women, Seniors, Veterans, Domestic violence survivors

phone: 480-644-4673 (480-644-HOPE)

address: Windermere Hotel site - 5750 E. Main St. (East Main Street and Recker Road area), Mesa, AZ 85205; referral only, no walk-up services

website: https://www.mesaaz.gov/Resident-Resources/Housing/Human-Services/Office-of-Homeless-Solutions/Off-the-Streets

email: offthestreets@mesaaz.gov

hours: Program operates 24/7 for enrolled participants; new placements are by referral only - call 480-644-HOPE (480-644-4673; Mon-Thu business hours; hours vary - confirm) or email for referral questions.

Services Offered

Emergency shelter and temporary housing (generally up to 90 days, with extensions possible in special cases) for people who are unsheltered in Mesa: hotel rooms at the Windermere Hotel site (65 city-leased rooms / 111 beds as of 2026), meals, 24/7 on-site staffing, case management, help connecting to medical, mental-health and substance-use treatment, employment services, schools and housing programs, and help with the next housing step. The program reported an 86% positive exit rate for 2025 and is described as Mesa's only shelter for families, women and seniors. A closed campus with police presence day and night supports safety. The city pairs the shelter with street outreach and first-responder referral pathways.

Eligibility Requirements

People experiencing homelessness in Mesa, with placement priority for the most vulnerable - families with children, single women, seniors, veterans and domestic-violence survivors. Participants must be referred (Mesa first responders - police, fire/medical and park rangers - the Maricopa County Continuum of Care, or nonprofit partners) and must follow program rules: drug-free environment, 10 p.m. curfew, room checks, no unauthorized visitors, and active work on a housing plan. People who do not follow the rules or refuse services can be removed. There are no walk-ups or drop-ins.

Population Served

Mesa's most vulnerable unsheltered residents. In the 12 months before May 2026 the City reported 458 people served, including 235 children, 100 seniors and 202 domestic-violence survivors; many participants live with disabilities. Earlier 2025 reporting cited 489 people served, a roughly 70-day median stay and about 86% positive exits.

How to Best Connect

Do not send someone to the site to ask for a bed - there are no walk-ups or drop-ins. To ask about a referral, call the Mesa Homeless Resource Line at 480-644-HOPE (480-644-4673) during Monday-Thursday business hours (hours vary - confirm when calling), or email offthestreets@mesaaz.gov. Referrals come through Mesa police, fire and park rangers, the Maricopa County Continuum of Care, and nonprofit partners; community members and agencies can call the same line about someone who needs help. Existing participants with case-status questions: Office of Homeless Solutions, 480-644-4273. Site (for enrolled participants): Windermere Hotel, 5750 E. Main St., Mesa (East Main Street and Recker Road area).

Important Information to Know

Capacity is limited and waits are common - in May 2026 the City reported roughly a six-week wait with about 109 single adults on the waitlist, after reducing from 85 to 65 rooms. On May 21, 2026 the City Council voted 4-2 to keep Off the Streets at the Windermere Hotel for three more years using federal funds; the city-owned Sunaire Hotel at 6733 E. Main St. is not the operating site, and the city is still working out what to do with Sunaire - selling it could require repaying about $13 million in federal and county funds - so Sunaire addresses seen elsewhere are out of date. The city selected Mercy House, a nonprofit shelter operator (which also runs the city-owned Sue's Espacio bridge shelter in Tempe, 2101 E. Apache Blvd., about 40 rooms), to take over day-to-day operations in 2026, replacing Community Bridges, Inc.; some City pages still name Community Bridges, so confirm the current operator when you call. Because the operator, room count and referral details can change, confirm current status through the resource line before promising placement. No participant fees are published; the program is publicly funded - ask staff about any costs.

Sources: City of Mesa - Off the Streets (Bridge to Success / Helaman House) · City of Mesa - Off the Streets Emergency Shelter Program staff update, May 21, 2026 (458 served, 65 rooms/111 beds, 6-week wait, Mercy House selection) · Arizona Republic - Mesa keeps emergency shelter, may have to repay $13M for hotel (May 22, 2026) · azcentral via NewsBreak - Mesa keeps emergency shelter, may have to repay $13M for hotel (May 22, 2026) · City of Mesa - Off the Streets brochure (referral process, 90-day stay, closed campus) · City of Mesa - City Council Report: Off the Streets program (referral sources and rules) · Community Bridges, Inc. - Off the Streets Operations and Maintenance Plan (Mesa Legistar; resource line monitored M-Th 7 a.m.-6 p.m.) · Mercy House Living Centers - program overview (Tempe shelter RFP, March 2026) · City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · City of Tempe - Housing and shelter (Sue's Espacio bridge shelter, 2101 E. Apache Blvd.) · Arizona Republic via Valley Interfaith Project - Mesa faith leaders demand action on homeless hotel plan (March 5, 2026; 2025 data: 489 served, ~70-day median stay, 86% positive exits) · City of Mesa - Off the Streets program page (referral-only; Mesa Homeless Resource Line)

City of Mesa Housing Authority · Section 8 HCV, project-based, Mainstream, VASH, FUP, EHV and TBRA/deposit assistance — usable

The City of Mesa's housing authority administers rental assistance for Mesa residents: Section 8 Housing Choice Vouchers, project-based vouchers, Mainstream vouchers for adults with disabilities, HUD-VASH for homeless veterans, Family Unification Program (FUP) vouchers, Emergency Housing Vouchers, and security/utility deposit help. The Section 8 wait list has been closed since 2022 (a fall 2026 reopening was reported by community trackers but could not be confirmed officially), and several programs are referral-based; call the office or use the homeless-access points to find out what is open.

Kinds of help: Housing vouchers & subsidized rent programs, Rent, utility & deposit crisis assistance; Groups: Veterans, Spanish, People with disabilities

phone: 480-644-3536

address: 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210 (mailing: P.O. Box 1466, Mesa, AZ 85211)

website: https://www.mesaaz.gov/Resident-Resources/Housing

hours: Monday–Thursday 7:30 a.m.–5 p.m. (lobby; Housing Specialist visits by appointment)

Services Offered

Rental assistance programs for Mesa: tenant-based Section 8 Housing Choice Vouchers (help paying rent in the private market); project-based vouchers attached to La Mesita Apartments (2254 W. Main St.) and Escobedo at Verde Vista Phase II (435 N. Hibbert), which include on-site services; Mainstream vouchers for non-elderly adults with disabilities; HUD-VASH vouchers combined with VA case management for homeless veterans; Family Unification Program (FUP) vouchers; Emergency Housing Vouchers; and security/utility deposit assistance for low-income Mesa households (the city's HOME-funded tenant-based rental assistance [TBRA] deposit activity). The authority also offers a Family Self-Sufficiency program for voucher holders, and its March 2026 board presentation reported about $25.6 million administered in voucher assistance, assisting roughly 1,825 families.

Eligibility Requirements

Households must meet HUD income limits (generally very-low income; under current FY2026 limits, about $56,200 a year for a family of four in the Phoenix–Mesa area - confirm current limits). Project-based vouchers require proof of chronic homelessness as the city defines it (at least two episodes of shelter living plus a substantiated need for long-term case management and supportive services) and also elderly, disabled or supportive-service status. HUD-VASH requires referral through the VA. Mainstream vouchers are for non-elderly people with disabilities. Deposit assistance is income-limited (listed at up to 80% of area median income; confirm).

Population Served

Low- and very-low-income Mesa households - families, elderly and disabled individuals - plus homeless households and homeless veterans through HUD-VASH. The housing authority serves the City of Mesa, with homeless referrals coordinated through the Maricopa County Continuum of Care access points.

How to Best Connect

Call 480-644-3536 (Monday–Thursday 7:30 a.m.–5 p.m.; Spanish-language information is available at the same number; AzRelay 7-1-1 for deaf or hard-of-hearing callers). Office: 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210; fax 480-644-2923; mail: P.O. Box 1466, Mesa, AZ 85211; Housing Specialist visits are by appointment. The Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher wait-list application has been closed; check the city's 'Application & Wait List' page for a reopening and apply online when it opens - and note that community trackers reported a fall 2026 opening that the city had not confirmed, so verify first. Use the Applicant Portal to update your information. For homeless households seeking project-based vouchers or other homeless housing programs: adults without children can call the Brian Garcia Welcome Center at 602-229-5155; families with minor children should call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 before going in person. Veterans should ask the VA about HUD-VASH.

Important Information to Know

The Housing Choice Voucher waiting list has been closed since 2022, and status reports conflict: the city's page has said the application is closed, while some third-party wait-list sites reported a reopening scheduled for August 25, 2026 - confirm the current status with the office (480-644-3536) or the city's Application & Wait List page before acting. Project-based, VASH, Mainstream, FUP and Emergency Housing Vouchers are mostly referral-based with limited openings, and Emergency Housing Vouchers were a time-limited federal program - confirm current status with the office or a referral partner. Applying is free; never pay anyone to apply. If you are on a list, keep your address and phone current (the office says changes must be reported within 10 business days). Voucher holders generally pay about 30% of adjusted household income toward rent.

Sources: City of Mesa — Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher: Application & Wait List (closed status; phone, hours, Spanish line, 10-business-day updates) · City of Mesa - Project-Based Voucher Program (La Mesita referral-only status) · City of Mesa — Housing Staff Contacts (office address) · City of Mesa — Housing Services Governing Board presentation (March 26, 2026; HCV/PBV, Mainstream, VASH, FUP, EHV; $25.6M administered; FSS) · City of Mesa — 2026 Annual Action Plan draft (wait list closed; EHV time-limited context) · HUD — FY 2026 Income Limits Documentation System (Phoenix–Mesa-Chandler, AZ MSA; 4-person very-low-income limit $56,200, effective April 2026) · City of Mesa — FY 2025/2026 Funding Process (Human Services/ABC: 'My Sister's Place: Family-Based Healing for Survivors of Domestic Violence') · 211 Arizona — Mesa Public Housing Authority (lobby hours, appointment note, deposit eligibility up to 80% AMI; last assured 04/01/2026) · Affordable Housing Hub (unofficial tracker) — announcement reporting Mesa HCV wait list opening Aug 25, 2026

City of Mesa Housing Authority · Security and Utility Deposit Program — usable

One-time City of Mesa help paying the refundable parts of a security deposit and utility deposit so a low-income household can move into a rental in Mesa. Payments go directly to the landlord and the utility company. You must apply before you sign a lease or move in, and the unit has to pass an inspection.

Kinds of help: Rent, utility & deposit crisis assistance; Groups: No group assigned

phone: 480-644-3536

address: 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210

website: https://www.mesaaz.gov/Resident-Resources/Housing/Human-Services/DepositAssistanceProgram

hours: Lobby hours are published as Monday-Thursday 7:30 a.m.-5:00 p.m. (211 Arizona); Housing Specialist visits are by appointment. Confirm current hours and application status by phone - new applications were temporarily closed as of August 5, 2026 with a planned reopening on August 25, 2026.

Services Offered

Financial help with the refundable portion of a security deposit and, when requested with the deposit application, a utility deposit for a rental unit in Mesa. Security-deposit help is limited to two months' rent; utility-deposit help is only approved together with security-deposit help and can cover SRP, City of Mesa utilities (electric and/or gas, water, sewer or trash), or Southwest Gas accounts - the application form lists example caps (SRP up to $235; City of Mesa utilities up to $265). Payments are made directly to the landlord and the utility provider.

Eligibility Requirements

The prospective rental unit must be inside the City of Mesa; total household income must be at or below 80% of Area Median Income (limits published for 2025-26: $62,850 for one person, $71,800 for two, $80,800 for three, $89,750 for four, up to $118,500 for eight - ask staff for the current-year limits); the applicant must not have signed a lease or already moved in; the household must not have received City of Mesa security-deposit assistance in the last three years; the landlord and utility provider must accept the program's letter of intent; and the unit must pass an inspection before approval. Utility-deposit help requires a security-deposit application.

Population Served

Low-income Mesa families and individuals who are preparing to move into a rental unit in Mesa - including people who are homeless, older adults and people with disabilities (the application asks about these situations), along with other income-eligible Mesa residents.

How to Best Connect

Apply online through the City of Mesa Limited-Income Assistance Programs pages (mesaaz.gov, Resident Resources) or get and return a paper application at the Mesa Housing Authority office, 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210. Call 480-644-3536 with questions or to request a disability or language accommodation (AzRelay 7-1-1 for deaf or hard-of-hearing callers). Applications can also be faxed to 480-644-2923 or placed in the secure drop box at the office. Submit the application and documents at least 14 days before your move-in date; the 2026-27 application says to allow about 14 days for processing (older materials said 10 business days). New applications were temporarily closed as of August 5, 2026 with a planned reopening on August 25, 2026 - call first to confirm the program is accepting applications.

Important Information to Know

This assistance covers deposits only, not rent, and it depends on funding availability. Do not sign a lease or move in before approval - if you have already moved in or signed, you are not eligible, and the unit must pass inspection. Documents include photo ID, Social Security cards, an unsigned proposed lease showing the refundable deposit amount (no more than two months' rent), proof of income, and bank statements; utility help also requires a letter from the utility with your account number and deposit amount. If your household is not on Section 8, extra income/eligibility documents may be requested. One City document for the federally funded (HOME) version of this deposit assistance lists a lower 60% AMI limit while current program materials use 80% - ask staff which limit applies to your case.

Sources: City of Mesa - Security & Utility Deposit Assistance Program · City of Mesa Housing - Security and Utility Deposit Program overview, office location and hours · City of Mesa - Security and Utility Deposit Program qualifications and application (2026-27; 14-day filing; fax and drop box) · City of Mesa - Security and Utility Deposit Program qualifications (income limits effective 7/1/2025; three-year rule) · City of Mesa - Community Resources and Support Programs (application closure note, August 2026) · City of Mesa - Security/Utility Deposit and TBRA policy manual (HOME-funded version; 60% AMI limit; two-month cap) · 211 Arizona - Utility Deposit Assistance, Mesa Public Housing Authority (hours; appointment; 80% AMI) · 211 Arizona - Security Deposit Payment Assistance, Mesa Public Housing Authority

City of Phoenix Housing Department · Public Housing & Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher programs — usable

The City of Phoenix's public housing authority - one of the largest in Arizona - runs public housing apartments and the Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher program, plus special vouchers for veterans and other groups. Mesa residents can ask about applying, but the lists have their own rules and preferences, and the Section 8 waiting list is currently closed.

Kinds of help: Housing vouchers & subsidized rent programs; Groups: Veterans, Families, Seniors

phone: 602-534-1974 (Section 8 office) / 602-262-6794 (administration)

address: 830 E. Jefferson St., Phoenix, AZ 85034 (Section 8/HCV office); 251 W. Washington St., 4th Floor, Phoenix, AZ 85003 (administration)

website: https://www.phoenix.gov/administration/departments/housing.html

email: housing@phoenix.gov

hours: Call for current hours; 211 Arizona lists office hours Monday-Friday 8 a.m.-5 p.m. for the public-housing programs.

Services Offered

Long-term affordable housing and rent help through the City of Phoenix's public housing authority: (1) public housing - City-owned apartments where rent is generally about 30% of adjusted household income; (2) Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher - a subsidy paid to a private landlord of your choice, with the household paying at least 30% of its monthly income toward rent; (3) special-purpose vouchers, including HUD-VASH (903 vouchers for homeless veterans, with case management through the VA) and HOPWA housing help for people living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal Counties (through Care Directions, 602-264-2273). The department also manages affordable apartment communities that accept applications at each property.

Eligibility Requirements

HUD income limits apply - generally at or below 50% of Area Median Income for public housing and Section 8 (about $56,200 a year for a four-person household in the Phoenix-Mesa-Chandler area under FY2026 limits). At least one household member must be a U.S. citizen, national, or eligible non-citizen. Families and single people can apply, including seniors (62+) and people with disabilities. Local preferences (such as living or working in Phoenix) affect waiting-list position, and special vouchers like HUD-VASH require a referral rather than a standard application.

Population Served

Low- and very-low-income people in Phoenix and the surrounding area, including families, single adults, seniors, people with disabilities, veterans, people experiencing homelessness, domestic-violence survivors and people living with HIV/AIDS.

How to Best Connect

Call the Section 8/HCV office at 602-534-1974 (S8intake@phoenix.gov) or the Housing Department administration at 602-262-6794 (housing@phoenix.gov) to ask which waiting lists are open and how to apply. In person: 830 E. Jefferson St., Phoenix, AZ 85034 (Section 8/HCV) or 251 W. Washington St., 4th Floor, Phoenix, AZ 85003 (administration). The Section 8 (HCV) waiting list is currently closed - watch for the City's public notice when it reopens; openings are announced widely and can be brief. Homeless veterans seeking HUD-VASH should contact their local VA medical center or call 1-877-4AID-VET (877-424-3838).

Important Information to Know

The City's official Section 8 page says the HCV waiting list is closed. The public-housing waiting list is listed as open by multiple independent waiting-list trackers (updates in mid/late 2026), but that status is not posted on the City's website - call 602-262-6794 to confirm before telling anyone which list they can join. Waiting lists can close without notice, and applicants must keep their address, phone and household information updated or they can be removed. This is a City of Phoenix program: Mesa residents may be able to apply to some programs, but local preferences favor Phoenix residents, and voucher rules can require leasing in Phoenix at first. Federal housing applications are free - never pay anyone to apply. Special vouchers (HUD-VASH and similar) come through referrals from partner agencies.

Sources: City of Phoenix - HOPWA: contact Care Directions Housing Information Services, (602) 264-2273 · City of Phoenix - Housing Options (program descriptions; populations served; administration phone) · City of Phoenix - Housing Department (public housing authority role; contacts) · Section8Waitlist.org - City of Phoenix Housing Department waiting-list status (public housing listed open) · AffordableHousingOnline.com - Arizona public housing waiting lists (updated September 2026; Phoenix listed open) · AffordableHousingHub.org - City of Phoenix Housing Department profile (public housing listed open; data via section8waitlist.org; updated August 28, 2026) · PublicAffordableHousing.com - Open waiting lists in Arizona (September 2026; Phoenix listed open) · HUDHousingNetwork - Arizona public housing waiting lists (as of August 18, 2026; Phoenix listed open) · HUD — FY 2026 Income Limits Documentation System (Phoenix–Mesa-Chandler, AZ MSA; 4-person very-low-income limit $56,200, effective April 2026) · 211 Arizona - Phoenix Housing Department Public Housing Programs (hours and contact)

City of Tempe CARE & HOPE Line / HOPE Street Outreach Team — usable

Tempe's 24/7 CARE & HOPE Line (480-350-8004) is the city's main entry point for homeless outreach help. Anyone can call — for themselves or for another person — and the city's HOPE (Homeless Outreach Program Effort) team responds with street outreach, shelter and housing connections, and referrals. The line serves people located in Tempe (Maricopa County); email HOPE@tempe.gov for non-urgent concerns.

Kinds of help: Street outreach & mobile services, Housing navigation; Groups: Homeless

phone: 480-350-8004

website: https://homeless-solutions.tempe.gov/

email: HOPE@tempe.gov

hours: CARE & HOPE Line: 24 hours a day, 7 days a week.

Services Offered

A 24/7 phone line plus a city street-outreach team. Callers can ask for help for themselves or report a person in crisis or experiencing homelessness in Tempe. HOPE outreach specialists engage people in the field and connect them to emergency shelter, housing solutions and housing navigation, diversion help such as reconnecting with family or support systems, treatment programs, and practical assistance such as replacing IDs. The team also responds to emails sent to HOPE@tempe.gov and works with police, fire, parks and community partners. In a January 2024 update, Tempe reported that since April 2022 about 31% of outreach engagements resulted in accepted services, and about 25% of those who accepted services accepted a shelter or housing solution; current results may differ.

Eligibility Requirements

No application or enrollment is needed to call. The line accepts self-referrals and third-party reports for people who are in Tempe. What is offered depends on the person's situation, the person's consent, and the availability of shelter and housing options.

Population Served

People experiencing homelessness or in crisis in Tempe, including individuals and families. Outreach is concentrated in areas such as Mill Avenue, high-need parks and around ASU, and the team coordinates with campus and community partners.

How to Best Connect

Call 480-350-8004 — answered 24/7. Email HOPE@tempe.gov for general concerns or non-urgent requests. The HOPE team works in the field; no walk-in office for the team is published, and staff go to where the person is. For immediate danger, call 911. The line serves Tempe — for someone elsewhere in Maricopa County, use that area's homeless-services access point.

Important Information to Know

This is a request-and-response outreach service, not a guaranteed shelter bed or housing placement; Tempe has limited shelter capacity, so timing depends on availability, and some people choose not to accept services. Staffing and response times vary. Programs the team refers to (shelter, treatment, housing) have their own eligibility rules and waitlists. The 24/7 number and the HOPE@tempe.gov email are the city's documented current contact routes.

Sources: City of Tempe — Connect page (CARE & HOPE Line) · City of Tempe — Homeless Solutions · Tempe City Council work study session materials (Jan 11, 2024) — Step Into Housing agreement renewed; 95.7% housed in 9-month pilot · Arizona State University — Homelessness near ASU

Coffelt-Lamoreaux Apartment Homes — 30 units reserved for DDD members — usable

Coffelt-Lamoreaux Apartment Homes is a 301-unit affordable apartment community in Phoenix where 30 units are reserved for adults with developmental disabilities who are DDD members, through a partnership between the Arizona DES Division of Developmental Disabilities and the Housing Authority of Maricopa County. Access is by referral from a DDD Support Coordinator — not a general public waitlist. Rent is 30% of the household's combined income; a deposit and first month's rent may be required. Call the DDD Customer Service Center at 1-844-770-9500, option 1, to start.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities; Groups: People with disabilities

phone: 1-844-770-9500

address: 1510 S. 19th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85009

website: https://des.az.gov/services/disabilities/developmental-disabilities/individuals-and-families/supports-and-services/housing

email: DDDCustomerServiceCenter@azdes.gov

Services Offered

Affordable rental housing: 30 of the community's 301 units are made available for qualified DDD members under the DDD/HAMC partnership. The DDD affordable housing quick-reference guide describes the set-aside as 30 one- to four-bedroom duplex units; DES's current housing page describes the community as one-, two- and three-bedroom floor plans. Rent is 30% of the household's combined income, and the federally funded property pays for reasonable accommodations/modifications when a health care provider documents the need and its connection to the disability.

Eligibility Requirements

Referral by a DDD Support Coordinator. The member must be a DDD member; have a head of household aged 18–61; have a current Person-Centered Service Plan (per the DDD quick-reference guide); and meet household financial eligibility requirements and HUD requirements. The quick-reference guide also lists DDD and Arizona Long Term Care System (ALTCS) eligibility. Every adult living in the unit — including live-in caregivers — undergoes a criminal background check; only people listed on the lease may live there; federal property rules apply (no drugs or smoking). A deposit and the first month's rent may be required, and utilities may need to be set up before moving in; live-in caregiver income is not counted in the household's combined income.

Population Served

Adults with developmental disabilities who are eligible for Arizona DDD services and are 18–61, especially those who need an affordable, integrated apartment with DDD supports. Households may include family members or roommates when the combined household meets income and housing requirements.

How to Best Connect

Start with the member's DDD Support Coordinator and ask for a referral to the DDD affordable housing program / Coffelt-Lamoreaux. If there is no Support Coordinator, call the DDD Customer Service Center at 1-844-770-9500, option 1 (email DDDCustomerServiceCenter@azdes.gov); the DES housing page also lists the DDD Affordable Housing Unit contact (Megan Akens, 1-844-770-9500 option 1). The community is at 1510 S. 19th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85009 — do not apply to the property directly for the DDD set-aside; the referral goes through DDD.

Important Information to Know

Availability depends on vacancies and DDD referrals; waitlists and timing are not published, and current openings cannot be confirmed here. Exact rent (beyond the 30%-of-income rule), deposit amounts, utility costs and unit sizes should be confirmed with DDD and the property. Unit-size descriptions differ slightly between DDD documents (quick-reference guide: one- to four-bedroom duplexes reserved; current DES page: community floor plans of one to three bedrooms). The guide is also published in Spanish (DDD-2313A-S). DES's DDD housing page also lists other housing pathways for members (for example, housing vouchers and Section 811 PRA rental assistance) that a Support Coordinator can discuss.

Sources: Member Housing Opportunities — Arizona DES/DDD · Quick Reference Guide: DDD Affordable Housing (DDD-2313A, 06/25) · Guía de consulta rápida: Vivienda Asequible de DDD (DDD-2313A-S)

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) - Coordinated Entry, PATH Outreach & Hotel Bridge Housing — usable

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is a Maricopa County behavioral health and homeless services provider listed as a coordinated entry access point for people experiencing homelessness across the county. CBI's 24/7 access line (877) 931-9142 handles coordinated entry, shelter and housing referrals, while its 24-hour PATH hotline (844) 691-5948 connects people showing signs of mental illness to street outreach. CBI also operates emergency shelters and Healing Centers and a converted-hotel bridge shelter at Rio Fresco (2425 S. 24th St., Phoenix) with case management and housing navigation toward Rapid Rehousing or Permanent Supportive Housing.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points, Street outreach & mobile services, Transitional housing; Groups: Homeless, People with behavioral health needs

phone: (877) 931-9142 (24/7 Access to Care); (844) 691-5948 (24-hour PATH hotline)

address: Locations across Maricopa County; CBI administration office: 1855 W Baseline Rd #101, Mesa, AZ 85202

website: https://communitybridgesinc.org/service/shelters-outreach/

hours: 24/7 access-to-care line and 24-hour PATH hotline; appointments line (888) 224-6593

Services Offered

- Coordinated entry access: Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is listed among Maricopa County's coordinated entry access points, offering access to specialized programs for people experiencing homelessness, with locations across the county.
- PATH outreach: CBI PATH provides street outreach to people displaying signs and symptoms of mental illness, with a 24-hour PATH hotline at (844) 691-5948; outreach includes engagement, assessment, case management and referrals to shelter, housing, treatment and benefits.
- Shelter and housing: CBI's navigation teams operate emergency shelters and Healing Centers using a closed-campus model and provide housing resources such as Rapid Rehousing and Permanent Supportive Housing with wrap-around services (Housing First).
- Hotel bridge housing: CBI operates the Rio Fresco site at 2425 S. 24th Street, Phoenix, a converted hotel used as a bridge shelter with temporary lodging, case management, behavioral health connections, meals and 24/7 security while people wait to move into permanent housing.
- Other supports in CBI's continuum include mental health and substance use treatment, physical health services, peer support/navigation, counseling, life-skills building and vocational rehabilitation.
- Service area: CBI reports operating in Gila, Maricopa, Pima and Yuma counties, with this coordinated entry/PATH route centered on Maricopa County.

Eligibility Requirements

- People experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County; coordinated entry is the region's front door and uses an assessment to determine which programs and priority a person may qualify for.
- PATH outreach targets adults who show signs and symptoms of mental illness (PATH is designed for people with serious mental illness who are homeless or at risk of homelessness).
- Bridge housing at Rio Fresco is for people prioritized within the homeless service system and is arranged through referral and case management, not as a direct walk-up.
- No income limits, insurance requirements or fees are published for these programs; ask CBI what assessment or documents are needed.

Population Served

- CBI's shelter and outreach programs state they serve individuals and families experiencing homelessness, including veterans; pregnant or postpartum women with substance use disorders and their children; survivors of domestic violence and human trafficking and their children; and youth ages 18-24.
- The PATH outreach line serves people experiencing homelessness who show signs and symptoms of mental illness.
- CBI does not publish which specific site or program accepts which population; the access line can route people to the appropriate CBI program.

How to Best Connect

- Call CBI's 24/7 Access to Care line: (877) 931-9142 - the main route for coordinated entry intake, shelter/housing questions and program referrals.
- Call the 24-hour PATH hotline: (844) 691-5948 for street outreach related to mental illness.
- Appointments and scheduling: (888) 224-6593 or scheduling@cbridges.com; CBI administration office: 1855 W. Baseline Rd. #101, Mesa, AZ 85202; (480) 831-7566.
- Website: communitybridgesinc.org (see the Shelters & Outreach page).
- Locations are across Maricopa County; phone first, since no walk-up intake or online application is published for these programs and bridge housing is referral-based.
- Regional context: Maricopa County's homeless-help page routes single adults 18+ to the Brian Garcia Welcome Center (602-229-5155) and families to the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700); veterans can call the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (602-248-6040). CBI is listed as a separate coordinated entry access point, so ask CBI which route fits.

Important Information to Know

- Costs are not published; ask CBI directly. Nothing reviewed states whether shelter or bridge housing carries a fee.
- Space is not guaranteed: shelter and bridge housing depend on assessment, priority, referrals and current capacity, which can change daily.
- CBI uses a closed-campus shelter/Healing Center model, and bridge housing serves people prioritized in the system - expect an intake process rather than immediate self-placement.
- Current signal: City of Phoenix Ordinance S-52891, adopted May 20, 2026, extends CBI's contract for the Rio Fresco converted-hotel bridge shelter for fiscal year 2026-27 (term on or about July 1, 2026, through June 30, 2027); city records describe bridge placement for people prioritized within the homeless service system, with on-site case management, behavioral health connections, meals and 24/7 security.
- Program-specific hours, payment/insurance details and exact intake steps are not published; the 24/7 access line and 24-hour PATH hotline are the reliable contact routes.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: No group assigned

address: 8101 N Black Canyon Hwy, Phoenix, AZ 85021

Services Offered

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) · Supportive Housing for Adults with Serious Mental Illness — usable

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is an Arizona behavioral-health provider whose housing programs include permanent supportive housing and rapid rehousing with wrap-around services, plus housing supports for adults with serious mental illness. Clients can call CBI's 24/7 access line to ask about current openings, the SMI/Mercy Care referral path, and costs.

Kinds of help: Supportive housing; Groups: People with behavioral health needs

phone: (877) 931-9142

address: 1855 W. Baseline Rd., Suite 101, Mesa, AZ 85202

website: https://communitybridgesinc.org/service/supportive-housing/

email: scheduling@cbridges.com

hours: 24/7 Access to Care phone line; appointments scheduled individually by phone or email.

Services Offered

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) provides permanent supportive housing (PSH) and rapid rehousing (RRH) with wrap-around services for people who are experiencing homelessness or have significant barriers to stable housing, including on-site support services and individualized care plans at co-located apartment communities in the Phoenix-Mesa area. CBI's services for adults with serious mental illness (SMI) include SMI community programs (Forensic Assertive Community Treatment and Assertive Community Treatment) with supports such as housing, employment, managing finances and family life, and its PATH program serves people with SMI and/or co-occurring disorders who are homeless or at imminent risk of becoming homeless, including people with an SMI determination who are not well connected to a clinic. CBI also operates emergency shelters, street outreach/navigation teams and healing centers in Maricopa County as part of the same continuum.

Eligibility Requirements

- Supportive housing is described for individuals experiencing chronic homelessness or with significant barriers to stable housing; each program applies its own eligibility and priority rules - ask CBI to confirm.
- SMI services are for adults determined to have a serious mental illness; PATH serves people with SMI and/or co-occurring disorders who are homeless or at imminent risk.
- Coverage: CBI says it accepts all patients and is contracted with all Arizona Complete Care (AHCCCS) health plans (Mercy Care is one) and many private insurances; uninsured or underinsured patients are helped to apply for AHCCCS.
- Entry: CBI says a new-patient referral can be started by a secure form in person, by phone or electronically, and an initial appointment is usually made within 7 days. How SMI supportive-housing enrollment is routed (CBI care team vs. community referral) is not spelled out online - ask the access line.
- Rent and income screening details are not published; ask at intake.

Population Served

Adults with a serious mental illness (SMI), including people with co-occurring mental-health and substance-use conditions who are experiencing homelessness or at imminent risk of homelessness. CBI's housing and shelter programs also list individuals and families experiencing homelessness, veterans, pregnant or postpartum women with substance use disorders and their children, survivors of domestic violence and human trafficking (and their children), and homeless and runaway youth ages 18-24 among those served.

How to Best Connect

- Call CBI's 24/7 Access to Care line: (877) 931-9142 - ask about supportive housing for someone with a serious mental illness, current openings and referral steps.
- Appointments/scheduling: (888) 224-6593 or scheduling@cbridges.com.
- Administration office: 1855 W. Baseline Rd., Suite 101, Mesa, AZ 85202; (480) 831-7566 - call before visiting; this is CBI's administration office.
- If you are already connected with a CBI care team, ask them directly about SMI housing supports.
- Online: communitybridgesinc.org (Supportive Housing and Serious Mental Illness pages). CBI says initial appointments are usually made within 7 days of first contact, and its housing/shelter sites are across Maricopa County including Mesa and Phoenix.

Important Information to Know

- Capacity, waitlists and unit availability are not published - confirm by phone; asking does not guarantee a place.
- Costs are not published: ask at intake what a resident pays and how insurance or benefits apply.
- The combined "SMI supportive housing with Mercy Care" description is not shown as a named program on CBI's website - its SMI services and its supportive housing are documented separately. CBI says it contracts with all Arizona Complete Care plans, which include Mercy Care; call to confirm the exact referral path for a Mercy Care member with an SMI determination.
- CBI's shelters and outreach operate in Gila, Maricopa, Pima and Yuma counties; this listing covers the housing supports available in the Mesa/Maricopa area.

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) — Mesa Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA) — usable

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is a Mesa-based behavioral health and housing nonprofit that administers Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA) and supportive housing for people experiencing homelessness in Mesa. City of Mesa reports describe CBI's TBRA work — rental payments plus application fees, security and utility deposit help, and housing navigation/case management — and recent city funding continues the program (FY 2025/26: CDBG for CBI Mesa TBRA Navigator Staff and HOME TBRA funds for rental payments and deposits). Start by calling CBI's 24/7 access line at 877-931-9142 and ask about the Mesa housing/TBRA program.

Kinds of help: Rent and deposit help; Groups: Families, Single adults

phone: 877-931-9142

address: 1855 W Baseline Rd #101, Mesa, AZ 85202 (CBI Mesa office — call before visiting)

website: https://communitybridgesinc.org/

email: scheduling@cbridges.com

hours: 24/7 Access to Care line: 877-931-9142. CBI Mesa administration office listed as Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m. (211 Arizona); TBRA program hours are not published — call first.

Services Offered

Tenant-based rental assistance for households in Mesa: temporary rental subsidy paid on behalf of the household while it stabilizes in its own unit, combined with help for application fees, security and utility deposits, plus housing navigation and case-management support. City of Mesa reporting describes the TBRA program run with CBI as assisting people and families experiencing homelessness in Mesa (166 households in that reporting period), and says CBI's award covered rental payments, deposits, housing management fees and supportive housing services. CBI's wider Mesa continuum also includes street outreach/navigation, shelters and permanent supportive housing/rapid rehousing with wrap-around services, so a navigator can point people to other options if TBRA is not the right fit.

Eligibility Requirements

Households in Mesa who are experiencing homelessness or housing instability and meet program income rules. City of Mesa HOME TBRA documents describe eligibility as incomes at or below 60% of the area median for Mesa residents who are homeless, with a required tenant share of rent and utilities, unit inspections (Housing Quality Standards), and rental assistance contracts of one to two years. Participants work with a CBI navigator/case manager, and the rental unit must meet program requirements. Exact current income limits, documents and referral criteria are set by the program and its city funding — confirm by calling CBI.

Population Served

Individuals and families experiencing homelessness in Mesa, including people reached through Mesa's street-outreach and navigation system.

How to Best Connect

Call CBI's 24/7 access-to-care line at 877-931-9142 and ask for the Mesa housing / tenant-based rental assistance (TBRA) program. CBI's Mesa office is at 1855 W Baseline Rd #101, Mesa, AZ 85202 — call before visiting. CBI lists appointment scheduling at 888-224-6593 (scheduling@cbridges.com), and its Mesa location card also shows 888-224-2087; the general office number is 480-831-7566. If you are already working with a Mesa outreach team, police/fire referral or another navigator, ask them to refer you to CBI housing services.

Important Information to Know

This is time-limited, subsidized rental assistance — not an open-ended voucher and not a guaranteed apartment. Funding is awarded by the City of Mesa year by year; recent cycles used HOME TBRA funds for rental payments and deposits (the FY 2025/2026 draft CAPER reports $200,000 in HOME TBRA funds to CBI with a $50,000 agency match) and CDBG funds for CBI's Mesa TBRA navigator staff ($78,933 in FY 2025/26 funding recommendations). Current openings, income limits and how long a household can receive help are not published — City HOME TBRA documents describe one- to two-year contracts; confirm with CBI. The 877-931-9142 number is CBI's general access line, so expect to be routed — ask specifically for the Mesa TBRA/housing program. CBI's Mesa administration office is listed as open Monday–Friday (8 a.m.–5 p.m. per a 211 Arizona listing); program-specific hours are not published.
