Hand in Hand Street Outreach – Maricopa County Human Services Department — usable

Maricopa County's street-outreach program for people experiencing homelessness in unincorporated areas and targeted communities. Outreach teams connect people to housing, shelter, health services, benefits, substance-use help and case management, and the public can file a request for an outreach visit. Note: Mesa, Phoenix, Chandler, Scottsdale and Glendale are outside its coverage — callers there are directed to 2-1-1.

Kinds of help: Street outreach & mobile services; Groups: Homeless

phone: 602-506-0589

website: https://www.maricopa.gov/5808/Hand-in-Hand-Program

hours: No published hours; outreach teams are dispatched after a request is submitted — call to confirm follow-up timing.

Services Offered

Outreach teams go into targeted communities and unincorporated Maricopa County where people experiencing homelessness are known to gather, working alongside neighbors, businesses, service agencies and law enforcement. Support includes housing, food and benefit programs, mental and physical health services, substance-use help, case management, transportation, employment and long-term care. Teams assess each situation and refer to services that may include temporary and permanent housing and shelter placement; the public can initiate a request for service and an outreach team is dispatched.

Eligibility Requirements

Serves people experiencing homelessness within the program's coverage area: unincorporated Maricopa County plus targeted areas of Anthem, Apache Junction, Arlington, Avondale, Buckeye, Carefree, Cave Creek, Citrus Park, El Mirage, Fountain Hills, Gila Bend, Gilbert, Goodyear, Guadalupe, Litchfield Park, New River, Peoria, Queen Creek, Rio Verde, Sun City, Sun City West, Sun Lakes, Surprise, Tolleson, Tonopah, Waddell, Wickenburg, Wittman and Youngtown. Mesa, Phoenix, Chandler, Scottsdale and Glendale are NOT covered — people in those cities are directed to 2-1-1 or 211.org. No other eligibility rules are published.

Population Served

People experiencing homelessness or facing a housing crisis in the coverage area listed above.

How to Best Connect

- Request outreach for someone in the coverage area through the “request for service” form linked from the program page, or call Maricopa County Human Services at 602-506-0589.
- In Mesa or another excluded city: call 2-1-1 or visit 211.org for local services.
- For emergency shelter: call 2-1-1 or go to the Brian Garcia Welcome Center at the Key Campus, 204 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix (open 24/7).

Important Information to Know

- The program is county-funded; no fees are published for outreach services.
- It is outreach and navigation, not a shelter itself — teams refer into shelter, housing, treatment and benefit programs, where availability and program rules apply.
- Coverage excludes Mesa: county materials direct Mesa residents to 2-1-1. This is a stated gap for Mesa-based clients rather than a referral destination.
- History note: older materials (2017–2020, including a 2020 regional municipal best-practices report) described “Hand in Hand” as a jail-to-community effort connecting high-utilizer, justice-involved people to supportive housing, navigation and physical/behavioral health services, naming partners such as Correctional Health, Mercy Care, Community Bridges, La Frontera Empact, ABC Housing and Southwest Behavioral Health. The county's current published program under this name is street outreach, and no current public information describes a jail-based partnership — treat the reentry framing as historical/unconfirmed. For reentry-specific housing help, ask Maricopa County Human Services at 602-506-0589.

Sources: Maricopa County – Hand in Hand Street Outreach · Maricopa County news archive – 2019 description of “Hand in Hand” as a justice-involved housing collaboration (historical)

Homeward Bound — Thunderbird Apartments (A New Leaf) — usable

Homeward Bound, a Phoenix nonprofit founded in 1990 to shelter families experiencing homelessness, was acquired by A New Leaf in 2024 and renamed and repositioned in early 2026. Its campus at 2302 W. Colter St. now operates as Thunderbird Apartments — 75 affordable two-bedroom homes with optional on-site support — and A New Leaf continues serving families in crisis through the countywide Family Housing Hub.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities; Groups: Families

phone: 602-899-8089 (apartment leasing); 602-595-8700 (family shelter intake via Family Housing Hub)

address: 2302 W. Colter St., Phoenix, AZ 85015

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/housing-and-shelter/thunderbird-apartments/

email: thunderbirdapts@azpropertybrokerage.com

hours: Listed campus hours Mon–Fri 9 a.m.–5 p.m. (2024 fact sheet); leasing office hours not published — confirm by phone

Services Offered

Thunderbird Apartments (the former Homeward Bound campus at 2302 W. Colter St.) offers 75 two-bedroom apartment homes (76 affordable units on the campus) for low-to-moderate-income households, with housing vouchers accepted and a waitlist possible depending on availability. On-site services are optional and include case management, employment guidance, financial coaching, life-skills opportunities and referrals; amenities include playgrounds, outdoor fitness, laundry, gated parking and a secure pedestrian gate. The campus also hosts the Strong Foundations Early Learning Center (licensed child care for children 0–5, serving residents and neighbors) and partner appointments with Homeless ID Project, Adelante Healthcare, Family Legal Services and Phoenix Children's Hospital, plus a residents-only food and basic-needs pantry. Homeward Bound's family shelter and transitional housing legacy continues under A New Leaf; families experiencing homelessness enter through the countywide Family Housing Hub.

Eligibility Requirements

- Thunderbird Apartments: income qualifications for low-to-moderate-income households. Published rental criteria include household income at least twice the monthly rent (or twice the portion not covered by a voucher), government photo ID for all adults, an established credit file (no minimum score), and a background review — felony convictions must be more than 3 years old with sentence completion at least 1 year ago; no violent felony (or drug-manufacturing/distribution) convictions within the last 5 years; drug- or alcohol-related convictions with no injury must be more than 6 months old; no active warrants; no registered sex offenders; and no evictions in the past 12 months. Chapter 7 bankruptcy must be discharged before applying and Chapter 13 must have been filed at least 1 year before; balances owed to prior landlords over $5,000 may cause denial; guarantors are allowed (income at least 4.5× rent, credit score 600 or higher). Lease start must be within 21 days of the unit's available date. Pets are allowed (maximum 2, 50 lbs or less each, breed restrictions). Households generally may not exceed 2 people per bedroom plus 1.
- Family shelter: for families with children; entry is through the Family Housing Hub, and placement is not guaranteed.

Population Served

Families and individuals with low-to-moderate incomes (apartments), families with children experiencing homelessness (shelter routing through the Family Housing Hub), and children ages 0–5 (early learning center). Services are based in Phoenix and serve the greater Valley/Maricopa County.

How to Best Connect

- Apartments: call the leasing office at 602-899-8089 (AZ Property Brokerage) or email thunderbirdapts@azpropertybrokerage.com; submit an application and meet income/screening criteria; waitlists possible. Current unit listings may be posted on the leasing agent's site ("Thunderbird Apartments on 23rd Ave," linked from A New Leaf's Thunderbird page). Address: 2302 W. Colter St., Phoenix, AZ 85015.
- Family shelter: Family Housing Hub — 602-595-8700 or 2-1-1 / 877-211-8661; the hub is on the UMOM campus at 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix. Placement is not guaranteed.
- Child care: hbreception@turnanewleaf.org or 602-374-8770.
- General housing help: housing@turnanewleaf.org; A New Leaf main office: 868 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85203; 480-969-4024.

Important Information to Know

- Homeward Bound was acquired by A New Leaf in 2024, and the property was officially renamed and repositioned as Thunderbird Apartments in early 2026 — longer-term affordable homes replacing the former short-term shelter model. Unit counts vary by source (75 two-bedroom homes on the current page; 76 campus units on the merger page; a 2026 news report describes renovating 80 apartments) — confirm current numbers with the leasing office. When a family says "Homeward Bound," this is the current operation (the old homewardboundaz.org site redirects to A New Leaf); families needing shelter now enter through the countywide Family Housing Hub.
- Costs: rents are set for low-to-moderate-income households; a leasing-agency unit listing seen 2026-09-28 showed a 2-bedroom at $995/month with electric, water/sewer/trash included. Confirm current availability, rent, what is included, and any deposits or fees before applying — published criteria state deposits and fees may be required in as little as 24 hours after acceptance.
- Federal subaward records show a December 2024 City of Mesa CDBG award to Homeward Bound ($65,300) for housing, case management, employment and navigation services for 70 people. Mesa's FY 2025/26 funding lists name other A New Leaf programs rather than Homeward Bound, and the FY 2026/27 plan could not be reviewed during this check — treat current Mesa contracting details as unverified and confirm with A New Leaf.
- Key links: turnanewleaf.org (Thunderbird Apartments page and 2026 fact sheets); turnanewleaf.org/homeward-bound-merger.

Sources: Thunderbird Apartments — A New Leaf (former Homeward Bound campus; leasing contact, services, eligibility) · A New Leaf — 'A New Leaf Acquires Homeward Bound' (2024 acquisition; Thunderbirds Village Family Campus) · Thunderbird Apartments fact sheet (2026) — how to become a resident; leasing office 602-899-8089 · Thunderbird Apartments rental criteria — income, voucher, screening and pet rules · Homeward Bound fact sheet (2024) — shelter access via Arizona 211; 2302 W. Colter St. and hours · Homeward Bound — City of Mesa CDBG subaward record (federal subaward data, Dec 2024; $65.3K; 70 people) · A New Leaf — April 2026 update: 'Introducing Thunderbird Apartments' (rename; joined A New Leaf in 2024) · ABC15 (March 19, 2026) — newly renamed Thunderbird Apartments affordable housing community (renovation; former shelter conversion) · AZ Property Brokerage — Thunderbird Apartments on 23rd Ave listings (current units when posted) · City of Mesa — FY 2025/2026 Annual Funding Recommendations (A New Leaf awards; no Homeward Bound line) · City of Mesa — FY 2025/2026 Awarded Contracts and FY 2026/2027 process overview (September 4, 2025)

HOPWA (Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS) — STRMU, Permanent Housing Placement, TBRA and supportive housing (City of Phoenix) — usable

City of Phoenix is the HUD HOPWA grantee for the Maricopa–Pinal region. HOPWA helps households with an HIV/AIDS diagnosis keep or obtain stable housing — short-term rent, mortgage and utility assistance (STRMU), move-in help like deposits and first/last month's rent (Permanent Housing Placement), tenant-based rental assistance (TBRA), and transitional/supportive housing — plus housing information, advocacy and employment services. Mesa is inside the service area. Start with the City's Central Intake Line, (602) 534-2433 (option 3 — ask for HOPWA), or apply online through phoenix.gov/HOPWA.

Kinds of help: Health-linked housing (respite & HIV/AIDS), Rent, utility & deposit crisis assistance; Groups: People living with HIV/AIDS, Spanish

phone: (602) 534-2433

website: https://www.phoenix.gov/HOPWA

hours: Mon–Fri, 8 a.m.–5 p.m. per the current HOPWA flyer (appointments scheduled through the Central Intake Line; older city materials list 7 a.m.–6 p.m. for the appointment line — confirm when calling)

Services Offered

- Short-term Rent, Mortgage and Utility (STRMU) assistance to prevent eviction, foreclosure or utility disconnection, including rent, mortgage and utility payments and related late fees; short-term, need-based and limited — city materials describe up to three assists in a rolling 12-month period.
- Permanent Housing Placement (PHP) move-in help: security deposits, first/last month's rent, application, credit, administrative and holding fees, and utility connection/deposit fees.
- Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA) — a rental subsidy (administered via the City of Phoenix Section 8 Office) for a private-market unit; referral through the Area Agency on Aging's Care Directions housing information program, (602) 264-2273.
- Transitional housing (up to 24 months; rent is 30% of income, $0 if no income) and permanent supportive/independent housing through partner agencies such as Southwest Behavioral & Health Services; also housing information and waitlist placement, eviction-prevention advocacy, case management and employment services.

Eligibility Requirements

- At least one household member must be diagnosed with HIV or AIDS; household income at or below 80% of Area Median Income; the household must currently reside in Maricopa or Pinal County.
- STRMU is need-based and cannot duplicate housing or utility assistance from another housing program; households must show they would be at risk of homelessness or utility disconnection without help.
- The intake line confirms required documentation (identity, income, HIV/AIDS status, housing/utility obligation) when you apply.

Population Served

- Households living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal counties — individuals, couples and families, including households with children (a minor in the household can qualify the household); low-income households at or below 80% AMI. Program flyers are published in English and Spanish.

How to Best Connect

- Call the City of Phoenix Central Intake Line: (602) 534-2433 (602-534-AIDE) or toll-free 1-866-882-1778. Follow the prompts: choose option #3, choose a Family Services Center, then choose #3 to speak to center staff — and ask for HOPWA assistance.
- Or apply online through the City's HSD Community Services Program Portal, linked from phoenix.gov/HOPWA.
- Business hours: Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–5 p.m. per the current flyer (older city materials list 7 a.m.–6 p.m. for the appointment line — confirm when calling); appointments are required. Mesa clients can start by phone or online. For TBRA, ask about referral through Care Directions at (602) 264-2273.

Important Information to Know

- Applying is free.
- Funding is limited and demand is high; if no appointment is available, try again the following week.
- STRMU cannot duplicate assistance from another housing program and is limited in frequency (see Services).
- Which HOPWA programs and nonprofit partners are funded can change with funding cycles — confirm current availability, program caps and required documents when you call.
- Mesa residents are inside the service area (Maricopa County), but the standard eligibility rules above still apply.

Sources: City of Phoenix — HOPWA program and online application entry · HOPWA STRMU & Permanent Housing Placement flyer (English/Spanish) — eligibility, Central Intake steps, business hours · City of Phoenix — Crisis Assistance and Social Services (HOPWA application instructions and Appointment Intake Line) · City of Phoenix - HOPWA Fact Sheet (eligibility; Housing Information Services) · City of Phoenix HOPWA program presentation (Aug 2023) — services, TBRA/transitional terms, STRMU/PHP limits

House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI) · Transitional & supportive housing navigation and basic-needs referrals — usable

House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI) is a Phoenix-based nonprofit that helps people who are unhoused or low-income find stable, supportive housing and connects them with food, clothing, hygiene supplies, mental-health and substance-use support, and job-readiness help. HOMI works as a resource and referral agency - it assists clients in locating transitional and supportive housing rather than advertising its own housing units.

Kinds of help: Housing navigation; Groups: No group assigned

phone: (623) 203-7381

address: 1951 W. Camelback Rd. #255, Phoenix, AZ 85015

website: https://houseofmiraclesinc.com/

email: admin@houseofmiraclesinc.com

hours: Not published - call or email first; no walk-in hours are posted.

Services Offered

HOMI helps individuals and families locate transitional and supportive housing - its website says it assists people in finding stable, safe, and supportive living arrangements - and its Community Cares/Contexture referral program connects clients to affordable and supportive housing, workforce and job-readiness assistance, clothing, nutrition assistance, and hygiene supplies. HOMI also offers food-assistance referrals around Maricopa County, SNAP outreach and application help, mental-health and substance-use counseling and education, employment readiness training, mentoring and life coaching, financial and nutrition workshops, and youth programs. Once someone becomes a client, HOMI completes an entrance assessment and matches services to their needs.

Eligibility Requirements

No formal eligibility rules are published. HOMI says it serves people facing barriers such as eviction history, poor credit, mental illness, substance use, low but sustainable income, and criminal convictions, and it receives daily social-service referrals from partner agencies through Unite Us/Community Cares. For housing assistance, contact HOMI to ask what it can offer in your situation and whether a partner-agency referral is needed.

Population Served

People who are unhoused or at risk of homelessness and low-income individuals and families in Maricopa County. HOMI states its programs reach seniors, teens, low-income families, unhoused people, people with mental-health or substance-use conditions, and people connected to the criminal justice system; it also describes a history of serving men and women in early recovery from drugs and alcohol and people transitioning from homelessness.

How to Best Connect

Call (623) 203-7381 or email admin@houseofmiraclesinc.com (alternate email: homihouse2020@gmail.com) to ask about housing navigation and the Community Cares/Contexture program. Partner agencies can refer clients through Unite Us/Community Cares. No walk-in hours are posted, so call or email first; if a case manager at another agency already works with the household, ask them to send a referral.

Important Information to Know

HOMI operates as a navigation and referral service rather than a confirmed housing provider - it is not clear whether HOMI directly leases or operates housing units, so ask exactly what housing it can connect you to and how long that may take. No fees or costs are published; confirm any costs directly. The website does not list walk-in hours; a federal NPI registration lists an office at 1951 W. Camelback Rd., Suite 255, Phoenix - confirm by phone before visiting. HOMI is a 501(c)(3) nonprofit (EIN 86-1009934) and states it is retired-veteran operated. HOMI does not advertise emergency shelter; for immediate shelter needs call 2-1-1 (1-877-211-8661).

Sources: House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI) - Home · House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI) - Our Work (Community Cares/Contexture) · House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI) - About and history · Greater Phoenix Chamber member news - House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI), Feb. 3, 2026 · NPPES/NPI record for House of Miracles, Inc. (NPI 1184317141) via OpenNPI

House of Refuge · Transitional housing program for families experiencing homelessness (Mesa, AZ) — usable

House of Refuge is a faith-based nonprofit in southeast Mesa that provides transitional housing for families with children who are homeless, at risk of homelessness, or fleeing domestic violence. Families live in one of 88 furnished two-bedroom homes on a 20-acre neighborhood and receive case management, employment and education support, and children's programs while they work toward stable housing.

Kinds of help: Transitional housing; Groups: Families, Domestic violence survivors

phone: 480-988-9242

address: 6935 E. Williams Field Rd., Mesa, AZ 85212 (administrative/mailing office; housing neighborhood in southeast Mesa)

website: https://houseofrefuge.org/

email: info@houseofrefuge.org

hours: Administrative office: Monday-Friday, 9 a.m.-4 p.m. Housing intake is by phone/email/inquiry form; the program is not an emergency shelter - for immediate shelter call 211 (1-877-211-8661).

Services Offered

Furnished transitional housing in two-bedroom homes (88 homes on a 20-acre neighborhood in southeast Mesa) plus wrap-around services: an assigned case manager and individualized case plan, a housing plan for the move to permanent housing, employment and education support through an on-site Employment & Education Center, life-skills, financial-literacy and nutrition classes, after-school programming for children 5-11, a teen center for ages 12-17, and access to a donation center for clothing, furniture and household items plus a certified food bank. Participants sign a lease and pay $400 per month with utilities included; the program describes itself as a transitional housing work program with a roughly year-long stay.

Eligibility Requirements

You must be deemed homeless, have received an eviction notice, or be fleeing domestic violence; currently reside in Arizona; have verifiable income of at least $1,170 per month (examples: pay stubs, SSI, child support, spousal maintenance - cash jobs are not accepted) and maintain it throughout the stay; pass a urine drug test and a background check (no sex-offense charges or convictions); be drug- and alcohol-free; have an adult 18 or older to sign the lease; and have custody of at least one minor child living with you or be in your third trimester of pregnancy. If income at move-in is not from employment, you must agree to gain employment within 45 days. Eligibility does not guarantee acceptance.

Population Served

Families with at least one minor child and pregnant women in their third trimester who are homeless, at risk of homelessness, or fleeing domestic violence. House of Refuge reports serving about 160 families (514 individuals) in a recent year, with children making up roughly 62% of residents.

How to Best Connect

Call 480-988-9242 or email info@houseofrefuge.org. When the program has openings, the Housing Inquiry Form is posted on the Need Housing page at houseofrefuge.org/need-housing/ - the page says to check back often. Administrative/mailing office: 6935 E. Williams Field Rd., Mesa, AZ 85212; the housing neighborhood is in southeast Mesa. House of Refuge does not offer emergency or immediate shelter - if you need shelter right away, dial 211 (1-877-211-8661).

Important Information to Know

Costs: $400 per month rent (utilities included); new residents have also been required since January 1, 2025 to pay a $200 security deposit and the last month's rent ($400) at move-in. The inquiry form is only available when the program has openings, so it may not be possible to apply at any given time - call to ask about openings and timing. The program requires work, minimum income, sobriety, background and drug screening, Arizona residency, and custody of a minor child (or third-trimester pregnancy). Eligibility does not guarantee acceptance, and there may be wait times. The program is faith-based and located in Mesa.

Sources: House of Refuge - Need Housing / Transitional Housing Program · House of Refuge - Our Program · House of Refuge - Our Impact · House of Refuge - Our History (88 two-bedroom homes acquired 1995) · House of Refuge - Media Inquiries ("a 20-acre community of 88 homes") · House of Refuge - Contact (office hours and address)

Housing Authority of Maricopa County (HAMC) · Housing Choice Vouchers & special voucher programs (Emergency Housing Vouchers winding down) — usable

The Housing Authority of Maricopa County (HAMC) is the public housing agency for much of unincorporated Maricopa County outside Phoenix, Glendale, Scottsdale, Tempe, Chandler, and most of Mesa. HAMC administers Housing Choice Vouchers (Section 8), project-based vouchers, and referral-based special vouchers - HUD-VASH for homeless veterans, Mainstream vouchers for adults with disabilities, and Family Unification Program vouchers - and its federal Emergency Housing Voucher (EHV) program is winding down with the wait list closed.

Kinds of help: Housing vouchers & subsidized rent programs; Groups: Veterans, Families, People with disabilities

phone: 602-744-4500

address: 8910 N. 78th Ave., Building D, Peoria, AZ 85345

website: https://maricopahousing.org/

email: info@maricopahousing.org

hours: Office hours are generally Monday-Friday, 8 a.m.-5 p.m.; call 602-744-4500 to confirm. Wait-list status changes - check HAMC's Wait List Schedule.

Services Offered

Rental assistance for low-income households: tenant-based Housing Choice Vouchers (Section 8) used to rent privately owned housing; project-based vouchers tied to specific HAMC properties; and special voucher programs filled by referral - HUD-VASH (rental assistance plus VA case management for homeless veterans), Mainstream vouchers (households that include a non-elderly person with disabilities, age 18-61), and Family Unification Program vouchers (families whose children are at risk of out-of-home placement, and eligible young adults 18-24 leaving foster care). The Emergency Housing Voucher (EHV) program - which historically took referrals through the Maricopa Continuum of Care for people who were homeless, at risk of homelessness, fleeing domestic violence, sexual assault, stalking or human trafficking, or recently homeless - is winding down as federal funding ends, and its list is closed.

Eligibility Requirements

HCV: very low-income households under HUD limits (HAMC posts current income limits); households provide Social Security numbers (if applicable), birth dates, and income information; at least one household member must have legal residency. A felony is not an automatic disqualification (it depends on the conviction and how long ago it occurred), but registered sex offenders are ineligible, as are people ever convicted of manufacturing or producing methamphetamine on federally assisted housing premises. Referral programs have their own criteria (for example, HUD-VASH requires homeless-veteran status verified through the VA). Geography matters: HAMC's voucher jurisdiction excludes Phoenix, Glendale, Scottsdale, Tempe, Chandler and most of Mesa - within Mesa, only county islands in East Mesa (south and east of Power Road) are in HAMC's area; check the boundary map at geo.azmag.gov/maps/hamc.

Population Served

Very low-income households; people experiencing or at risk of homelessness (through coordinated-entry referral programs); homeless veterans (HUD-VASH); non-elderly adults with disabilities (Mainstream); families involved with child welfare and young adults leaving foster care (Family Unification Program); and current EHV families transitioning into the HCV program as EHV winds down.

How to Best Connect

Call 602-744-4500 or email info@maricopahousing.org; office: 8910 N. 78th Ave., Building D, Peoria, AZ 85345 (generally Monday-Friday, 8 a.m.-5 p.m.). Check HAMC's Wait List Schedule on maricopahousing.org for which lists are open - as of September 15, 2026: EHV closed; Section 8/HCV closed (closed since August 25, 2023); Family Unification, Mainstream and HUD-VASH open by referral only; some property/project-based lists open at times. For example, HAMC's September 15, 2026 notice opened a 1- and 2-bedroom Apache ASL Trails project-based voucher wait list for applications through October 15, 2026 (Rent Cafe applicant portal); check HAMC's public notices for current openings. When the HCV list opens, HAMC announces it publicly in advance and takes applications through its online applicant portal - watch maricopahousing.org/news and the Wait List Schedule. For coordinated-entry referrals: families with children call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700; single adults can call 2-1-1 (1-877-211-8661) for the nearest coordinated entry point; homeless veterans can contact the VA Community Resource & Referral Center at 602-248-6040.

Important Information to Know

EHV is not open to new applicants: HAMC's schedule lists EHV as closed and HAMC policy describes the program winding down as federal funds end, with current EHV families transitioned into the HCV program. The Section 8/HCV list is also closed and opens only when HAMC announces an opening - no application is accepted while it is closed, and waits can take years. Voucher households generally pay about 30% of adjusted income toward rent; confirm details with HAMC. No application fees are stated by HAMC; ask if any costs apply. HAMC's jurisdiction excludes Phoenix, Glendale, Scottsdale, Tempe, Chandler and most of Mesa - verify the specific address before applying, and ask HAMC or 2-1-1 which housing authority covers your address. HAMC also owns and manages affordable apartment communities with their own wait lists - ask about those as an alternative.

Sources: Housing Authority of Maricopa County - Home · HAMC - Programs & Services (Rental Assistance) · HAMC - Wait List Schedule as of September 15, 2026 · HAMC - Public Notice: Apache ASL Trails Project Based Voucher Wait List open Sept. 15-Oct. 15, 2026 · HAMC - Housing Choice Voucher Service Area (Jurisdiction) · HAMC - Emergency Housing Voucher FAQs (July 2021; historical referral and eligibility details) · HAMC - News & Public Notices (HCV wait list closed; wait-list opening notices) · HAMC - Updated Administrative Plan, effective July 23, 2025 (EHV sunset and transition provisions)

HUD-VASH (HUD-VA Supportive Housing) — VA Phoenix Health Care System — usable

HUD-VASH pairs a HUD Housing Choice Voucher (rental assistance for a private-market apartment or home) with VA case management and supportive services so veterans experiencing homelessness can move into and stay in permanent housing. Veterans connect through VA — the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (602-248-6040, walk-in) or the National Call Center for Homeless Veterans (877-424-3838, 24/7).

Kinds of help: Supportive housing, Housing vouchers & subsidized rent programs; Groups: Veterans

phone: 602-248-6040 (Phoenix VA Community Resource & Referral Center); 877-424-3838 (National Call Center for Homeless Veterans, 24/7)

address: 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014 (Phoenix VA Community Resource & Referral Center — walk-in veteran access point)

website: https://department.va.gov/homeless/hud-vash/

email: VHAPHOCRRCStaff@va.gov

hours: Walk-in access at the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center: Monday–Friday 7:30 a.m.–4:00 p.m. (excluding federal holidays); arrive early for same-day referrals. The National Call Center for Homeless Veterans phone line is answered 24/7.

Services Offered

Permanent supportive housing for veterans: a HUD Housing Choice Voucher that pays part of the rent for a qualifying privately owned apartment or house, paired with ongoing VA case management and clinical support — including connection to primary care, mental health care and substance-use treatment, help with budgeting and leases, and long-term support to remain housed. Vouchers are administered locally by public housing authorities in partnership with VA, and the Phoenix VA homeless programs team handles referrals for Mesa and Maricopa County veterans.

Eligibility Requirements

For veterans who are experiencing homelessness and need ongoing, intensive case management (for example, due to chronic health conditions or other complex challenges). Veterans must be eligible for VA health care and meet HUD-VASH income limits — as of HUD's 2024 policy change, initial income eligibility is set at the Low level (80% of area median income) for all housing agencies, and service-connected disability benefits are not counted when determining income eligibility. A referral through VA is required; veterans cannot get a HUD-VASH voucher directly from a housing authority. Veterans who do not need intensive case management are usually served through other VA or community programs.

Population Served

Veterans — including veterans with families — who are experiencing homelessness in the Phoenix VA catchment area, which includes Mesa and Maricopa County.

How to Best Connect

Call or walk in to the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (CRRC): 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014; phone 602-248-6040; email VHAPHOCRRCStaff@va.gov. Hours are Monday–Friday 7:30 a.m.–4:00 p.m., excluding federal holidays. It is a walk-in clinic, so arrive as early as possible for a same-day referral (very late arrivals may instead receive a community shelter referral). Veterans can also call the National Call Center for Homeless Veterans at 877-424-3838 (free, confidential, 24/7) or ask their VA primary care team or social worker for a HUD-VASH referral.

Important Information to Know

Cost: the voucher pays the portion of rent above what the veteran contributes — generally about 30% of adjusted monthly income; utility allowances can adjust that amount. A referral does not guarantee a voucher, an available unit, or a timeline — voucher and case-manager capacity are limited, and the veteran must find a landlord who accepts the voucher; ask about current local availability when you call. HUD-VASH is separate from the regular Section 8 waiting list (Phoenix's regular Housing Choice Voucher list is currently closed); the City of Phoenix Housing Department administers HUD-VASH vouchers with the VA (about 903 vouchers awarded), and referrals still come through the VA, not the city.

Sources: HUD-VASH — VA Homeless Programs (program overview, voucher plus case management, national contact) · VA Phoenix – Homeless Veteran care / Community Resource and Referral Center (hours, phone, email, services) · VA Phoenix Clinic – Community Resource and Referral Center location (address, hours, free services, walk-in) · VA: Understanding the Policy Change that Increased Access to HUD-VASH (Low 80% AMI income eligibility; disability benefits excluded) · City of Phoenix - HOPWA: contact Care Directions Housing Information Services, (602) 264-2273

I-HELP Mesa – Interfaith Homeless Emergency Lodging Program — needs-resolution

Rotating emergency lodging for adult women experiencing homelessness in the Mesa/East Valley area: participating faith congregations take turns hosting overnight shelter, typically with meals and case-management referrals. Current listings name Lutheran Social Services of the Southwest as the operator, but the organization's own current website no longer lists the program and details date from 2021–2024 — verify before referring anyone.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Women

phone: Reported intake: 480-684-0645 (phone intake, listed Mon–Thu 8am–noon; leave a message or text if no answer); 480-396-3795 (Lutheran Social Services of the Southwest main line); 480-654-4539 (older listing; on the host congregation's current site this number serves its utility/rental assistance appointments) — confirm before referring

Mesa host sites rotate; I-HELP nights for women have been hosted at Victory Lutheran Church, 5946 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85205

hours: Intake reported by phone Monday–Thursday, 8am–noon (February 2024 2-1-1 record); verify current hours

resolutionReason: Retained as needs-resolution after targeted checks on 2026-09-28: the earlier operator conflict is largely resolved toward Lutheran Social Services of the Southwest serving adult women (Tempe Community Action Agency's current Find Help Now page and a 2-1-1 record agree; the AZCEND contacts in one directory entry belong to AZCEND's separate Chandler I-HELP). However, LSS-SW's own current website no longer lists an I-HELP or shelter program, and the most recent program evidence is February–March 2024, so current operation and the intake line cannot be confirmed. The record preserves the supported numbers and details and routes confirmation through 2-1-1; kept for administrator confirmation rather than client referral.

Services Offered

Rotating overnight emergency lodging for adult women experiencing homelessness in the Mesa area: participating faith congregations take turns hosting guests overnight, and the broader East Valley I-HELP model includes evening and morning meals, case management and referrals to community resources, bus passes, mobile shower access, and medical referrals. New guests complete a pre-screening interview and must call first for bed availability. Exact current services could not be confirmed.

Eligibility Requirements

Per a February 2024 2-1-1 record for Lutheran Social Services of the Southwest I-HELP – Mesa: homeless women without children; the program did not accept sex offenders and did not allow animals, including service animals. All I-HELP listings require a pre-screening interview and a call to check bed availability before entering. Confirm current eligibility before referring anyone.

Population Served

Adult women experiencing homelessness in Mesa and the East Valley. Three sources — Tempe Community Action Agency's help page, a 2-1-1 record, and a Mesa host congregation — each describe Mesa I-HELP as serving women (women without children). Whether men are served by any current Mesa I-HELP site is not confirmed.

How to Best Connect

Call 480-684-0645 to confirm the program and check bed availability, then complete the required pre-screening interview. The 2-1-1 record lists phone intake Monday–Thursday, 8am–noon, with instructions to leave a message or text if there is no answer; the same number is listed by Tempe Community Action Agency for Mesa I-HELP. Lutheran Social Services of the Southwest's main line is 480-396-3795. Dialing 2-1-1 (1-877-211-8661) is the most reliable way to confirm the current operator and intake. Older listings also show an office at 2502 E. University Ave., Ste. 125, Phoenix; LSS-SW's current main office is 3636 N. Central Ave., Suite 300, Phoenix. Host sites rotate among Mesa congregations (I-HELP nights for women have been hosted at Victory Lutheran Church, 5946 E. University Dr., Mesa).

Important Information to Know

This lead is unresolved: current listings consistently name Lutheran Social Services of the Southwest as the operator, but the organization's own current website no longer lists an I-HELP or shelter program, and the most recent program details (including the 480-684-0645 intake line and the February 2024 eligibility record) date from 2021–2024. The 480-654-4539 number appears in older listings tied to a Mesa host congregation's assistance line rather than verified I-HELP intake. A claim that I-HELP includes motel vouchers and move-in/storage help comes from a third-party aggregator and is unverified — do not promise it. I-HELP programs elsewhere (AZCEND's I-HELP in Chandler; Tempe Community Action Agency's I-HELP in Tempe) are separate programs run by different agencies. Treat this as a follow-up lead rather than a confirmed bed, and confirm the current operator, eligibility, and intake before referring.

Keys to Change (Human Services Campus) · Key Campus emergency shelter & nightly overflow - Brian Garcia Welcome Center (single adults, Phoenix) — usable

Keys to Change (formerly Human Services Campus) operates Key Campus, a 13-acre downtown Phoenix hub at 12th Avenue and Madison for single adults experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. The Brian Garcia Welcome Center is the walk-up entry point for diversion, assessment, and shelter referrals; the campus includes the 600-bed CASS Single Adult Shelter, the 95-bed Respiro shelter run by Keys to Change, and a remaining St. Vincent de Paul overflow space - a reduced, funding-dependent version of the pandemic-era nightly overflow - plus meals, health care, ID, mail, showers, storage, employment and housing-match services. The campus shelters nearly 900 people per night.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Single adults, Homeless

phone: 602-883-4509 (Brian Garcia Welcome Center); 602-229-5155 (Key Campus); 602-282-0853 (main office)

address: 204 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (Key Campus at 12th Ave. & Madison)

website: https://keystochangeaz.org/

hours: Brian Garcia Welcome Center: open 24 hours a day. Key Campus is operating 24/7 through September 30, 2026 (heat relief); call 602-883-4509 to confirm timing and shelter availability.

Services Offered

Emergency shelter access for single adults. Shelter referrals are made through the Brian Garcia Welcome Center, which provides diversion services, vulnerability screenings, and shelter placement to the campus's shelter spaces: the 600-bed CASS Single Adult Shelter, the 95-bed Respiro emergency shelter (operated by Keys to Change, with housing navigation), and a remaining St. Vincent de Paul overflow space - the last remnant of the nightly overflow mats that began during the pandemic. The campus is also a one-stop service hub with 13 partner organizations: meals, mail service, ID and birth-certificate replacement, showers, safe storage, medical and dental care, mental-health and substance-use services, state benefits (AHCCCS/SNAP), SSI/Social Security advocacy, employment help, legal services and homeless court, pet services, heat relief, and coordinated entry/housing-match services that connect people to housing and vouchers.

Eligibility Requirements

Key Campus shelters and serves single adults (people without minor children) who are experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Shelter placement follows screening and referral at the Welcome Center and depends on bed availability - the campus says its shelters are full most nights and people are turned away when capacity is reached. Families with children are served separately through the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700); youth ages 12-17 should call the 24/7 youth hotline (602-841-5799); veterans can use the VA Community Resource & Referral Center at 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106 (602-248-6040).

Population Served

Single adults experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Keys to Change says it works with close to 14,000 individuals a year and the campus serves more than 1,000 people daily, whether they are unsheltered or staying in shelter.

How to Best Connect

Go in person to the Brian Garcia Welcome Center on Key Campus (204 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix - at 12th Avenue and Madison; the Welcome Center is also listed at 206 S. 12th Ave.) or call 602-883-4509. The Welcome Center is open 24 hours a day (Key Campus's Get Help page says to visit any time from 7 a.m. to 11 p.m. to begin an assessment); campus operations were extended to 24/7 through September 30, 2026 for heat relief. Call first to confirm timing and shelter availability. For a coordinated-entry point closer to home, call 2-1-1 (1-877-211-8661). Campus main line: 602-229-5155; general office: 602-282-0853. Keys to Change also hosts Project Connect pop-up resource events several times a year around the Valley.

Important Information to Know

The nightly overflow shelter has shrunk significantly: the pandemic-era overflow once added about 280 mats in two spaces; the Lodestar Day Resource Center overflow closed June 1, 2025, and the remaining St. Vincent de Paul dining-room overflow was reported at about 170 mats in 2025 and was still counted among the campus's three shelter spaces in spring 2026. Funding for overflow remains fragile, and shelter capacity changes - call before going. Older guides that say to arrive by 5:30 p.m. for an overflow mat may be outdated; ask the Welcome Center how evening placement works. Shelters are frequently full, and no cost information is published for shelter entry - ask staff whether any fees apply. The campus is in downtown Phoenix, not Mesa, and serves people from across Maricopa County, so plan transportation if traveling from the East Valley. Some older listings give a W. Jackson St. address for this campus; the current official address is 204 S. 12th Ave.

Sources: Keys to Change - Key Campus services (shelter, Welcome Center, housing match) · Keys to Change — Get Help (Welcome Center call-ahead 602-883-4509; 206 S. 12th Ave.) · Keys to Change reflection - Sept. 1, 2026 (nearly 900 people per night in three spaces) · Keys to Change reflection - Aug. 4, 2026 (campus hours, shelter counts) · Keys to Change reflection - April 1, 2026 (three shelter spaces including St. Vincent de Paul overflow) · KJZZ - Largest Phoenix shelter loses 110 beds as funding expires (June 7, 2025) · Keys to Change Community Outreach Meeting Minutes - August 5, 2025

Lost Our Home Pet Rescue – Temporary Care Program — usable

Tempe-based program providing up to 90 days of complete care — shelter, food, medical care, and enrichment — for dogs and cats whose owners in Arizona are in crisis (homelessness, eviction, domestic violence, medical treatment, foreclosure, financial hardship). Keeps pets safe so owners can seek shelter, treatment, or housing and then be reunited; application required; high demand.

Kinds of help: Pet housing support; Groups: Pet owners, Domestic violence survivors, Spanish

phone: 602-445-7391 (Temporary Care Program office); 602-445-7387 (main line)

address: 2323 S. Hardy Drive, Tempe, AZ 85282

website: https://www.lostourhome.org/get-help/temporary-care

email: TCP@lostourhome.org

hours: Temporary Care Program office: Tuesday–Saturday, 10am–5:30pm; urgent-case emails reviewed daily (responses within 48 hours)

Services Offered

Temporary Care Program: up to 90 days of complete care for dogs and cats whose owners are in crisis — shelter, food, medical care, and enrichment — with the goal of reuniting pets with their families afterward; clients of partner domestic-violence shelters can receive up to 120 days of free care. Lost Our Home also runs a pet food bank (Animeals on Wheels), a domestic-violence program that partners with local DV shelters (Sojourner Center and A New Leaf run pet-friendly campuses), rescue and adoption for abandoned or surrendered pets, and lifetime-care assistance. For owners facing homelessness, eviction, or housing that will not allow pets, this program keeps the animal safe while the owner seeks shelter, treatment, or housing.

Eligibility Requirements

Arizona residents facing a qualifying crisis: eviction, homelessness, domestic violence, medical treatment or rehabilitation, foreclosure, financial hardship, veteran/disabled veteran circumstances, or other crises. Pets must be spayed/neutered (before entering the program or during it) and meet health requirements — dogs: rabies, distemper/parvo (DA2PPv), and bordetella vaccinations plus proof of spay/neuter; cats: FVRCP vaccination, negative FeLV/FIV test, plus proof of spay/neuter. Lost Our Home can help with missing vaccinations. Applicants must respond within 48 hours when contacted, and services are not offered out of state.

Population Served

Arizona families and individuals in the Phoenix metro area (including Tempe and Mesa) whose pets need temporary care during a personal crisis; program materials describe it as the most comprehensive temporary pet care program in the Valley. Listed examples include people experiencing eviction or homelessness, domestic-violence survivors, and people entering hospital or rehabilitation care. Spanish-language program pages (Cuidado Temporal) are available.

How to Best Connect

Apply online with the Temporary Care Application at https://new.shelterluv.com/form/community/LOH/20738-temporary-care-program, or call the Temporary Care Program office at 602-445-7391, Tuesday–Saturday, 10am–5:30pm (main line: 602-445-7387). Urgent cases: complete the application, then email TCP@lostourhome.org with TCP Urgent in the subject line, including your name, your pet's names, and a brief statement of urgency — urgent cases are reviewed daily and answered within 48 hours. The shelter is at 2323 S. Hardy Drive, Tempe, AZ 85282. Spanish-language program page: https://www.lostourhome.org/get-help/cuidado-temporal.

Important Information to Know

No fee is mentioned in program materials and news reporting describes the service as free (food and medical costs covered) — confirm any costs when you apply. Demand is high and the program may not be able to help immediately; if you do not respond to staff within 48 hours, your application moves to the next person. The standard stay is up to 90 days (some cases, including domestic-violence clients, extend to 120), and the goal is reunification with your pet. Vaccination and spay/neuter requirements apply; pets cannot remain intact. This program cares for pets — it does not provide human housing or shelter; it removes the pet barrier while you seek housing, shelter, or treatment.

Sources: Temporary Care – Lost Our Home Pet Rescue (official; 90 days, application, hours, phone, pet requirements, urgent process) · Our Story – Lost Our Home Pet Rescue (official; 120 days of free pet care for partner DV-shelter clients) · Domestic Violence – Lost Our Home Pet Rescue (official; Sojourner Center and A New Leaf partnerships) · Hours & Location – Lost Our Home Pet Rescue (official; 2323 S. Hardy Drive, Tempe) · Temporary Foster and Housing Resources for Pets – Maricopa County · Lost Our Home Pet Rescue offers temporary care solution for families in crisis – ABC15 (free service; food and medical costs covered) · Temporary Care Application – Lost Our Home (online form)

Maggie's Place · Shelter services at The Hannah House and Bunkhouse — usable

Maggie's Place is a Phoenix-based nonprofit operating maternity homes across the Valley for women who are homeless and pregnant. The Hannah House is its Mesa home, and a five-bedroom bunkhouse addition on the same property (announced for completion in 2023) was planned to add more safe nights of shelter for expecting women. The program provides a stable home during pregnancy and after birth, plus coaching and support services. Intake is centralized through one intake specialist, there is no listed fee, and capacity is limited, so call early.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Women, Families

phone: 602-246-3724 (intake specialist); 602-262-5555 (main office)

address: Confidential location, Mesa, AZ 85204 (details shared during intake)

website: https://maggiesplace.org/

hours: Intake by phone; messages are returned in order. Office hours are listed as 9 a.m.-5 p.m. (2-1-1); call before visiting.

Services Offered

- Emergency shelter and a stable home at The Hannah House in Mesa for women who are homeless and pregnant, including a five-bedroom bunkhouse addition on the same property (announced for completion in 2023) planned to add more safe nights of shelter for expecting women.
- Wraparound support: case management, peer support, parenting and child-development programs, employment assistance, financial literacy and legal clinics, health and wellness classes, nutritious food and infant supplies, and help connecting to medical care and community resources; support continues for alumnae after moms leave.
- Maggie's Place operates several maternity homes across the Valley; women are placed where space is available, so openings at The Hannah House vary. Program materials describe The Hannah House as the only comprehensive service provider for this population in the Mesa area.

Eligibility Requirements

- Must be homeless and pregnant, age 18 or older, free from immediate physical danger, without other children in her immediate care, and willing to live in community.
- A 2-1-1 Arizona listing adds: substance-free for at least 30 days, no active warrants, and openness to sharing a room.
- Bring proof of pregnancy to the intake meeting.

Population Served

- Pregnant women experiencing homelessness (18 and older) and, after birth, mothers with their babies. A woman can move in at any point during pregnancy, and program materials describe support through baby's first year.
- 2-1-1 lists a statewide service area and classifies the program as a maternity home.
- Serves a small number of women and children each year - public figures vary (about 31 in a 2023 program page; a 2024 City of Mesa report describes services for 52 women and children, 36 mothers and 16 babies) - and program materials say demand exceeds capacity.

How to Best Connect

- Call the intake specialist at 602-246-3724. If no one answers, leave a message; calls are returned in the order received.
- The next step is an interview at the Family Success Center, 4001 N. 30th St., Phoenix, AZ 85016. The process can take from one day to a few weeks depending on availability.
- If you need immediate emergency housing, call 2-1-1. Main line: 602-262-5555; website: maggiesplace.org. A Spanish-language mom portal is available online; confirm Spanish-language intake support when calling.
- Ask about current openings at The Hannah House (Mesa) versus other Maggie's Place homes.

Important Information to Know

- No fee is listed for services.
- Stay length: Maggie's Place describes support through baby's first year, while its 2-1-1 listing says women can stay until the baby is 9 months old - confirm the current length at intake.
- Office hours are listed as about 9 a.m.-5 p.m. (2-1-1 lists daily 9 a.m.-5 p.m.); hours can vary, so call before visiting.
- The shelter location is confidential and shared during intake.
- The City of Mesa has supported The Hannah House through grant pass-through funding and describes the shelter as serving Mesa residents; this does not guarantee eligibility or a bed.
- Capacity is limited and demand exceeds it; openings depend on when families move on.

Sources: Maggie's Place - Intake and Qualifications · Maggie's Place - Expansion Project (The Hannah House, Mesa) · 2-1-1 Arizona - The Hannah House listing · City of Mesa - Council Report, April 15, 2024 (Hannah House emergency shelter) · Maggie's Place - Home (Family Success Center and main line) · Maggie's Place - Programs (support services) · Maggie's Place - New Addition Announcement (stay through one year after birth; Feb. 2, 2023)

MANA House · Veteran transitional housing — usable

MANA (Marines, Army, Navy, Air Force) House is a transitional housing program operated by Catholic Charities in Phoenix for veterans who are homeless or at risk of homelessness; current sources describe it as serving male veterans. Veterans live in a supportive program with an assigned case manager and a housing plan, plus employment, benefits and basic-needs services on site. Admission is only through a referral from the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (CRRC). Capacity is up to about 76 veterans at a time (recent figure), typical stays run three to nine months, and no fee is listed.

Kinds of help: Transitional housing; Groups: Veterans, Men

phone: 602-254-6785 (program questions); 602-248-6040 (VA CRRC - required referral)

address: 2422 W. Holly St., Phoenix, AZ 85009

website: https://manahouseaz.org/

hours: Administration listed Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-4 p.m.; facility staffed 24 hours. Call before visiting.

Services Offered

- Transitional housing for veterans who are homeless or at risk of homelessness, with an assigned case manager who helps build a plan to return to stable, permanent housing. Typical stays are three to nine months.
- On-site services: career counseling, job search, resume development, job placement and interview readiness in the Veterans Resource Center; help enrolling in VA, Social Security and DES benefits; guidance getting a DD-214 and filing VA claims; help finding permanent affordable housing; bus passes; transportation to and from the VA hospital; computer access; full kitchen and dining room; laundry and showers.
- A veterans dental care program for residents was announced in March 2026 (first-come, first-served; confirm current availability), and staff provide information and referrals to other community resources.

Eligibility Requirements

- Must be a veteran (the name stands for Marines, Army, Navy, Air Force).
- Only accepts referrals from the Phoenix VA Community Resource & Referral Center (CRRC) - admission is not arranged by contacting MANA House directly.
- A 2-1-1 listing describes eligibility as male veterans who are homeless or at risk of becoming homeless and notes discharge criteria may apply; current program announcements also describe services for male veterans. Women veterans and anyone with questions about discharge status should ask the CRRC what options are available.
- Contact the provider to confirm required documents.

Population Served

- U.S. military veterans experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness, described in current sources as male veterans; the facility can accommodate up to about 76 veterans at a time. Program reporting says about 15 veterans complete the program each month, with nearly 80 percent remaining housed after leaving.
- 2-1-1 lists all Arizona ZIP codes as the service area.

How to Best Connect

- Step 1 - Start at the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (CRRC): 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014; 602-248-6040; Monday-Friday about 7:30 a.m.-4:30 p.m. Ask for a MANA House referral.
- Step 2 - Questions about the program: call MANA House at 602-254-6785; 2422 W. Holly St., Phoenix, AZ 85009. Administrative hours: Monday-Friday 8 a.m.-4 p.m.; the facility is staffed 24 hours. Call before visiting.
- More information: manahouseaz.org/get-help/mana-house.

Important Information to Know

- A CRRC referral is required; there is no walk-in admission.
- No fee is listed (2-1-1 lists fees as none).
- Bed availability is not published; ask the CRRC about current openings - eligibility does not guarantee a place.
- Capacity figures vary by source (up to about 76 veterans in a March 2026 announcement; up to 70 in earlier materials).
- Details can change: the 2-1-1 listing was last assured 02/01/2026; confirm hours and documents by phone.

Sources: MANA House - Get Help · 2-1-1 Arizona - MANA House listing · Catholic Charities - MANA House (operator and capacity) · Circle the City — Community Resource Guide (health navigator contact) · Western Alliance Bank - veterans dental program at MANA House (capacity, typical stays, outcomes; March 19, 2026)

Maricopa Association of Governments (MAG) / Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care · Coordinated Entry Access Points list (PDF) — usable

The Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care's official list of Coordinated Entry access points - the places where a person experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County starts the housing assessment process (interview, connection to resources, housing plan). It includes Mesa locations (A New Leaf's East Valley Men's Center and COPA Health at Paz de Cristo) and countywide specialty points for veterans, youth, women, families, people who are medically vulnerable and others. It is a referral/navigation list, not a housing provider, and completing the process does not guarantee housing or a voucher.

Kinds of help: Housing navigation; Groups: Homeless

address: Maricopa County, AZ (multiple access-point locations)

website: https://azmag.gov/Portals/0/Homelessness/Resources/Coordinated-Entry-Access-Points.pdf

hours: Varies by access point (some open 24/7; others limited weekday hours) - call the access point to confirm.

Services Offered

- Official countywide list of Coordinated Entry access points - places where a person experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County can complete an initial interview, be connected to appropriate resources, and work on a housing plan with shelter and housing options.
- Mesa access points listed: A New Leaf - East Valley Men's Center (men), 2345 N. Country Club Dr., (480) 610-6722, listed hours M-F 8 a.m.-3 p.m.; COPA Health - Paz de Cristo, 424 W. Broadway Rd., (480) 464-2370, listed hours M-F 10 a.m.-3 p.m.
- Other entry routes in the list: single adults - Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, (602) 229-5155; families - Family Housing Hub (call 2-1-1; the County also lists 602-595-8700); veterans - VA Community Resource & Referral Center, (602) 248-6040; single women - Halle Women's Center, (602) 362-5833; medically vulnerable - Circle the City, (602) 516-6825; plus youth, Native American, HIV and outreach points.
- It is a navigation/referral list, not a housing provider: the actual assessment and services happen at each access point.

Eligibility Requirements

- Open to people experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County; each access point serves specific populations (single adults, families, veterans, youth, women, and others), so use the point that matches the situation.
- No cost to use an access point. Coordinated Entry prioritizes by assessed need; completing the process does not guarantee housing or a housing voucher.

Population Served

- People experiencing homelessness across Maricopa County: single adults, families with children, youth (12-24), veterans, single women without children, Native American adults and youth, people who are medically vulnerable, people living with HIV/AIDS, and domestic violence survivors through specialized lines.

How to Best Connect

- Use the access point for the situation: Mesa - East Valley Men's Center (men; 480-610-6722) or Paz de Cristo (480-464-2370); singles - Brian Garcia Welcome Center (602-229-5155); families - 2-1-1 / Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700); veterans - CRRC (602-248-6040); youth 12-17 - Safe Place (602-841-5799); youth 18-24 - UMOM Youth Outreach (480-868-7527); domestic violence - the SAFEDVS line (480-890-3039) is in transition - see the note below; anything else - dial 2-1-1.
- East Valley Men's Center intake update: A New Leaf now starts with an online pre-screen and asks that services not be requested by direct call - use the individuals' placement hotline (877) 211-8661.
- Call before going to confirm hours; some points are open 24/7 and others have limited daytime hours, and some sites may require a phone or online pre-screen before a bed is offered.
- The current list and companion pages are linked from maricopacoc.org/get-help and maricopa.gov Help with Homelessness (maricopa.gov/5917). If a number does not connect, dial 2-1-1.

Important Information to Know

- This is a referral/navigation list, not a housing provider; the list itself states the process does not guarantee housing or a voucher.
- The list's revision date is unclear and some entries may be dated - confirm locations and hours by phone.
- Domestic-violence route in transition: the countywide SafeDVS centralized screening reportedly transitioned to a pre-recorded message on May 15, 2026, directing callers to local shelters. The 480-890-3039 number is still published by the CoC, the County and A New Leaf; if it does not connect, call 2-1-1 or a shelter directly (e.g., Sojourner Center 602-244-0089; My Sisters' Place 480-821-1024; New Life Center 623-932-4404).
- Free to use. 2-1-1 also helps with rent and utility assistance and routes families to the Family Housing Hub.
- The CoC's Get Help page also links a seasonal heat-relief map for cooling, respite and hydration sites.

Sources: Coordinated Entry Access Points - MAG / Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care · Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care - Get Help · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · Maricopa County coordinated entry access points - Native American Connections (Community Bridges and CBI PATH hotline listings) · Circle the City — Community Resource Guide (health navigator contact) · A New Leaf - East Valley Men's Center · ABC15 - Domestic violence hotline ending full service in Maricopa County (SafeDVS transition) · City of Scottsdale - Resources for people experiencing homelessness (March 2025; East Valley access points)

Maricopa County – Help with Homelessness (Coordinated Entry Access Points) — usable

The official Maricopa County “Help with Homelessness” page for the region's coordinated entry system. It routes people experiencing homelessness (or those helping them) to the right front-door access point by population — domestic-violence survivors, single adults, families, veterans and youth 12–17 — with direct phone numbers and locations, plus 2-1-1 as a general fallback. Use it to get a client into the correct intake line for shelter and support services in Maricopa County, including Mesa. Note: countywide domestic-violence routing has been in transition since May 2026 — see Important Information to Know.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points, Housing navigation; Groups: Families, Single adults, Veterans, Youth, Domestic violence survivors

phone: DV 24-hr SAFEDVS: 480-890-3039 / 844-723-3387; Single adults: 602-229-5155; Families: 602-595-8700; Veterans: 602-248-6040; Youth (12–17): 602-841-5799

website: https://www.maricopa.gov/5917/Help-with-Homelessness

hours: Domestic-violence crisis line: 24 hours. Brian Garcia Welcome Center: open 24/7. Other entry lines — call to confirm hours.

Services Offered

Maricopa County's coordinated entry routing: the page identifies the correct shelter/support-services access point by population and gives the direct phone number or location for each — domestic-violence survivors, single adults 18+, families, veterans and youth ages 12–17. It also lists Maricopa.gov/ApplyforHelp and 2-1-1 as general fallbacks. Coordinated entry for the county is run through the Key Campus (Housing Match team on behalf of Maricopa County).

Eligibility Requirements

There is no application on the page itself; each entry point screens for its own programs and checks current availability. Call the line that matches the household: single adults 18+ without children, families, veterans, or youth 12–17. Anyone fleeing domestic violence can use the 24-hour crisis line. Contacting an entry point starts an assessment — it does not guarantee a shelter bed or housing.

Population Served

People experiencing homelessness or a housing crisis in Maricopa County, including Mesa: domestic violence survivors; single adults 18 and older; families; veterans; and youth ages 12–17.

How to Best Connect

- Domestic violence: 24-hour SAFEDVS crisis line 480-890-3039 or toll-free 844-723-3387 — still published, but countywide centralized screening reportedly changed in May 2026 (see notes); or use the “Chat Live” option with A New Leaf.
- Single adults: Brian Garcia Welcome Center at the Key Campus, 204 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (open 24 hours a day, 7 days a week); call 602-229-5155.
- Families: Family Housing Hub, 3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix; call 602-595-8700 (walk-in has been available on weekdays — confirm current hours).
- Veterans: Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center, 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Phoenix; call 602-248-6040.
- Youth (12–17): text “Safe” plus your address to 44357, or call 602-841-5799; stationary Safe Place sites include QuikTrip stores, libraries, fire stations, transit stops and some businesses.
- Not sure who to call: dial 2-1-1 or visit 211.org.

Important Information to Know

- Domestic-violence routing has been in transition: the countywide centralized DV screening function reportedly closed on May 15, 2026, with shelters shifting to direct calls, while the 480-890-3039 / 1-844-SAFEDVS number remains published and is still listed as A New Leaf's placement line for its own DV services. If the line can't connect someone, call a shelter directly (for example, Sojourner Center's 24/7 hotline, 602-244-0089) or dial 2-1-1.
- No fee is charged to call these lines or complete an assessment; each program has its own rules.
- Phone numbers, addresses and hours can change; confirm before sending someone. (Some county listings show the welcome center as 206 S. 12th Ave.; the Key Campus address is 204 S. 12th Ave. — same campus at 12th Ave. and Madison.)
- Expect an assessment and possible waitlists rather than immediate placement.
- The page itself does not take applications — each number connects you straight to the intake provider.

Sources: Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · Keys to Change - Key Campus services (shelter, Welcome Center, housing match) · 12News - Arizona domestic violence hotline discontinued (May 2026) · Sojourner Center - When Systems Shift, Safety Must Stay Strong (centralized referral line transition)

Mercy Housing – Mesa Senior Meadows (Mesa, AZ) — usable

Mesa Senior Meadows is a 40-unit Mercy Housing community for low-income seniors age 62+ near downtown Mesa, listed as currently accepting applications. Mercy Housing (Mountain Plains region) also operates other affordable communities in the area, including Page Commons in Gilbert (55+) and Guadalupe Senior/Nuestro Pueblito in Guadalupe.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities; Groups: Seniors

phone: (480) 615-7893; TTY 800-855-2880 or 711

address: 333 E. 6th Street, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://www.mercyhousing.org/mountain-plains/mesa-senior-meadows/

hours: Office: Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m.

Services Offered

Affordable rental apartments for low-income seniors: 40 units at 333 E. 6th St., Mesa, with a community room, on-site laundry, health-and-wellness programming, and resident services (newsletters/calendars, activities and age-in-place programs). Applications are taken by Mercy Housing directly (online through the property page or at the office). Mercy Housing also lists other Arizona communities in the region: Page Commons in Gilbert (100 units, ages 55+, incomes 30–60% of area median) and Guadalupe Senior/Nuestro Pueblito in Guadalupe (22 units, seniors, incomes at or below 50% of area median).

Eligibility Requirements

Mesa Senior Meadows: low-income adults age 62 and older; income limits apply. Page Commons: ages 55+ with household incomes between 30% and 60% of the area median. Guadalupe Senior: low-income seniors with incomes at or below 50% of the area median. Mercy Housing screens applications for each property.

Population Served

Low-income older adults — age 62+ (Mesa Senior Meadows), 55+ (Page Commons), and low-income seniors (Guadalupe Senior).

How to Best Connect

- Call Mesa Senior Meadows at (480) 615-7893 (TTY 800-855-2880 or 711), Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.; or apply via the “Apply Now” link on the property page at mercyhousing.org.
- Visit: 333 E. 6th Street, Mesa, AZ 85201 — about a five-minute walk from downtown Mesa.
- Other communities: Page Commons, 170 N. Oak St., Gilbert — (480) 813-4609; Guadalupe Senior/Nuestro Pueblito, 9403 S. Avenida Del Yaqui, Guadalupe — (480) 897-3273.

Important Information to Know

- Mesa Senior Meadows was listed as currently accepting applications in September 2026; availability and waitlists can change quickly — call before sending someone.
- Rent and subsidy terms are not published; ask about income limits, rent and whether project-based rental assistance applies.
- A third-party directory lists a separate property called “Mercy Housing Arizona I” at 520 N. Mesa Drive, Mesa (95 units); it does not appear on Mercy Housing's own site, so its owner/manager and waitlist status are unconfirmed — verify before relying on it, including any claimed link to City of Mesa Housing Authority waitlists.
- Applying to these properties is separate from City of Mesa Housing Authority public-housing/voucher waitlists; check the Housing Authority's current openings separately if a voucher is also needed.

Sources: Mercy Housing – Mesa Senior Meadows (Mesa, AZ) · Mercy Housing – Page Commons (Gilbert, AZ) · Mercy Housing – Guadalupe Senior/Nuestro Pueblito (Guadalupe, AZ) · Third-party LIHTC directory listing – “Mercy Housing Arizona I” (unverified)

My Sisters' Place — confidential domestic-violence shelter (Catholic Charities Community Services) — usable

A confidential emergency shelter in the East Valley for people and families fleeing domestic violence, operated by Catholic Charities Community Services. All services are free, and the 24-hour hotline 480-821-1024 is the intake and availability line.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Domestic violence survivors, Women, Families

phone: 480-821-1024 (24-hour hotline)

website: https://www.catholiccharitiesaz.org/domestic-violence

hours: 24 hours a day — the shelter is staffed and the hotline answered around the clock.

Services Offered

Confidential emergency shelter for people and families fleeing domestic violence: a private room (families), food and access to a full kitchen, shared laundry and recreational space, and staffing 24 hours a day. Residents get an individual service plan and weekly case management; individual and group counseling is offered to adults, children are encouraged to attend counseling, and staff help with replacing IDs and documents, enrolling children in school, employment and resume help, and applying for state assistance. Staff also refer residents to outside services such as emergency housing and housing assistance, medical and dental care, transportation and clothing. The shelter has a maximum of 30 beds in 11 rooms (nine rooms can accommodate families of various sizes; two are single-occupancy).

Eligibility Requirements

For people and families fleeing abuse. All services are free for clients. Space is limited and changes day to day; the hotline will confirm whether there is room and can suggest alternatives if the shelter is full.

Population Served

Women and men, and parents with children, escaping abuse; individuals without children may share a room with another adult. The shelter is in the East Valley and serves domestic-violence emergencies across the area, including Mesa.

How to Best Connect

Call the My Sisters' Place 24-hour hotline: 480-821-1024 — it is the intake and availability line, and the shelter's location stays confidential. Related Catholic Charities domestic-violence services: Pathways counseling intake 602-749-4405; Pathways victim advocacy 602-819-0082 or 480-267-5761. Backup 24/7 help: National Domestic Violence Hotline 1-800-799-7233; Arizona Coalition Against Domestic Violence 602-279-2900 or 1-800-782-6400; 211 Arizona / community information and referral 602-263-8900 or 1-800-799-7739.

Important Information to Know

Services are free and the location is confidential to protect survivors. My Sisters' Place is one of only two domestic-violence shelters in the East Valley. Mesa's human-services funding records list this program among funded services ("My Sister's Place: Family-Based Healing for Survivors of Domestic Violence," FY 2025/26; "My Sister's Place/Pathways," FY 2026/27), but the City does not arrange placements — admission is only through the 24-hour hotline. Length of stay is not published — an older community guide cited up to 120 days — so ask the hotline about length of stay and current bed availability. Some listings spell the name "My Sister's Place"; it is the same Catholic Charities shelter. If someone is in immediate danger, call 911.

Sources: Catholic Charities AZ — Domestic Violence Services / My Sisters' Place · Catholic Charities AZ — Ruby Thrives at My Sisters' Place (24-hour hotline; shelter model) · City of Mesa — FY 2025/2026 Funding Process (Human Services/ABC: 'My Sister's Place: Family-Based Healing for Survivors of Domestic Violence') · City of Mesa — FY 2026/2027 Funding Process (Human Services: 'My Sister's Place/Pathways: Walking Alongside Survivors of DV')

Native American Connections · HomeBase / youth and Native-adult coordinated entry — usable

Native American Connections (NAC) is a Maricopa County coordinated entry point for Native American individuals experiencing homelessness and for youth (listed as ages 18–26), and it runs the HomeBase emergency youth shelters and drop-in centers in Phoenix and Surprise. Call 602-648-9739 to start a housing assessment for Native adults, or 602-263-5531 for youth/HomeBase; a longer-stay youth option (Saguaro Ki transitional housing) is reached through the same youth number.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points, Emergency shelter, Children & youth crisis care; Groups: Youth, Native American

phone: 602-648-9739; 602-263-5531

address: HomeBase Phoenix: 931 E. Devonshire Ave, Phoenix, AZ 85014; HomeBase Surprise: 12215 W. Bell Rd, Surprise, AZ 85378

website: https://www.nativeconnections.org/housing/coordinated-entry-access-points/

email: PSHinquiry@nativeconnections.org

hours: Call to start intake; shelter and drop-in hours vary. NAC main office: 602-254-3247, Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.

Services Offered

- Coordinated entry (housing assessment) for Native American individuals experiencing homelessness — NAC's Housing Specialist starts the assessment that connects to NAC's permanent supportive housing and shelter programs: 602-648-9739 or PSHinquiry@nativeconnections.org.
- Coordinated entry for youth (listed as ages 18–26) with services at the HomeBase locations: call 602-263-5531.
- HomeBase emergency youth shelters and drop-in centers — dormitory-style shelter, meals, computers, laundry, a small gym, counseling/behavioral-health support and case management at no cost. Sites: HomeBase Phoenix (24 beds) and HomeBase Surprise (40 dormitory-style beds); NAC describes 65 shelter beds across the two communities.
- Longer-stay youth housing: Saguaro Ki transitional living — 25 furnished studio apartments, six-month lease with rent at 30% of income, renewable twice for a total stay of up to 18 months.

Eligibility Requirements

- Native adults: this coordinated entry point is specifically for Native American individuals experiencing homelessness — start with the Housing Specialist at 602-648-9739.
- Youth: must be experiencing homelessness; NAC's pages currently say 18–26 in some places and 18–24 or 18–25 in others, so confirm the age range when you call. You must provide ID verification and complete a housing assessment.
- Saguaro Ki adds income requirements (proof of income and about two months of work history at 25 hours/week).
- Single adults and youth only: NAC's access-point list notes these services are for single adults; families should call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 or 211.

Population Served

- Native American individuals experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County (adult coordinated entry).
- Youth and young adults experiencing homelessness from all backgrounds — NAC says its services are open to all community members and reported in 2024 that 80% of its youth are BIPOC.
- Not a family shelter; families with children are routed to the Family Housing Hub.

How to Best Connect

- Native adults: call 602-648-9739 or email PSHinquiry@nativeconnections.org.
- Youth and HomeBase: call 602-263-5531 (also covers Saguaro Ki).
- Sites: HomeBase Phoenix, 931 E. Devonshire Ave, Phoenix, AZ 85014; HomeBase Surprise, 12215 W. Bell Rd, Surprise, AZ 85378. Both sites are outside Mesa (north-central Phoenix and Surprise) — call to ask how East Valley/Mesa youth access the program and whether transportation help is available. Drop-in and shelter hours vary — call before going.
- General NAC questions: 602-254-3247, Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.

Important Information to Know
