- No cost is described for HomeBase shelter and drop-in services (NAC says 'at no cost'; community listings list no fees). Ask about any costs when you call; beds are limited and availability is not guaranteed.
- NAC's own pages disagree on the youth age cut-off (18–24, 18–25 and 18–26 all appear) — verify current eligibility before referral.
- Both intake numbers are current on NAC's website: 602-648-9739 for Native adult coordinated entry; 602-263-5531 for youth/HomeBase. Note: at least one city resource list labels 602-648-9739 a 'homeless youth hotline' — NAC's own number-to-service mapping is the one to follow.
- NAC does not offer rental assistance. If NAC is not a fit: call 211; for families, Family Housing Hub 602-595-8700; for youth under 18, the county lists Safe Place (call 602-841-5799, or text 'SAFE' and the address to 44357).

Sources: Maricopa County coordinated entry access points - Native American Connections (Community Bridges and CBI PATH hotline listings) · HomeBase Youth Services – Native American Connections · Housing Options: HomeBase and Saguaro Ki requirements – Native American Connections · Contact Us – Native American Connections · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155)

New Life Center · Emergency domestic-violence shelter — usable

New Life Center is one of Arizona's largest domestic-violence shelters — a 110-bed, trauma-informed emergency shelter in the Goodyear/West Valley area with a confidential location, serving singles and families from across Maricopa County, and one of the few that welcomes pets. Call 623-932-4404 to start shelter screening and to ask about survivor support services.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Domestic violence survivors, Families, Spanish

phone: 623-932-4404

address: Goodyear, AZ (confidential shelter location); mail: PO Box 5005, Goodyear, AZ 85338

website: https://newlifectr.org/

email: info@newlifectr.org

hours: Shelter/support line: call 623-932-4404 — city and community listings describe it as a 24-hour line; call any time for screening. Business/office hours are not posted.

Services Offered

- Emergency domestic-violence shelter: a 110-bed, trauma-informed shelter that meets basic needs and helps families move toward independent living — New Life Center reports 18,117 safe nights provided and 2,517 survivors served last year.
- Survivor support services: customized support plans for people impacted by domestic violence, sexual violence and human trafficking — advocacy and case management, trauma treatment, group and individual therapy, child care, and more — for shelter residents and for survivors living safely in the community.
- Sunshine Healing Center: a stand-alone sexual-violence healing center open to anyone who has experienced sexual violence (including secondary victims); a police report is not required to receive services.
- Pet-friendly shelter: New Life Center is one of the few domestic-violence shelters in Arizona that welcomes pets, with onsite pet accommodations.

Eligibility Requirements

- For survivors of domestic violence, sexual violence or human trafficking; serves singles and families (ABC15's 2026 shelter list describes it as 'singles and families, pet friendly').
- New Life Center describes its shelter service as 'for shelter within Maricopa County.'
- Eligibility is determined by phone screening; the shelter has a confidential location, so there is no walk-in process.

Population Served

- Survivors and their children from across Maricopa County — New Life Center reports serving more than 1,000 children and adults each year.
- Community directories list served populations including women, men, teens and families; call to discuss your household's situation.
- Pet owners — one of the only pet-welcoming domestic-violence shelters in Arizona.

How to Best Connect

- Call 623-932-4404: New Life Center directs people seeking shelter or support services to this number, and city/community listings (including DomesticShelters.org) describe it as a 24-hour line, so you can call at any time for screening.
- Email info@newlifectr.org is for general agency questions only — not for shelter requests.
- The shelter is in the Goodyear/West Valley area with a confidential address; do not go to the location without screening. Ask about access from Mesa, transportation help and current bed availability.
- Mailing address: PO Box 5005, Goodyear, AZ 85338. Spanish-language pages are available on the website; ask for a Spanish-speaking advocate if needed.

Important Information to Know

- The countywide SafeDVS referral hotline reportedly ended full service in 2026 and now plays a recorded message directing callers to local shelter services (ABC15; transition to the recorded message on May 15, 2026). Maricopa County's 'Help with Homelessness' page (checked September 2026) still lists a 24-hour SAFEDVS crisis line at 480-890-3039 — calling New Life Center directly at 623-932-4404 is the practical route.
- Bed availability varies; calling does not guarantee a bed. No fees are published — ask about any costs during screening. Published figures differ: New Life Center's own site reports a 110-bed shelter; the DomesticShelters.org profile (last updated May 2023) lists 104 beds, a 24/7 hotline and up to about 120 days maximum stay — confirm current details when you call.
- Because the shelter is West Valley-based and its location is confidential, ask how Mesa-area survivors are served and what transportation options exist.
- Safety tip: call from a safe phone; the website includes an emergency-exit button for people using a shared device.

Sources: Get Help – New Life Center · Learn / How We Help – New Life Center · ABC15 - Domestic violence hotline ending full service in Maricopa County (SafeDVS transition) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · New Life Center profile – DomesticShelters.org (24/7 hotline; 104 beds; English/Spanish; last updated May 2023)

Oxford House, Inc. · Oxford House sober-living residences (self-run recovery housing) — usable

Oxford House is a national network of self-run, self-supported sober-living homes for adults recovering from substance use disorder, with houses in Arizona including the Phoenix metro/Mesa area. There is no time limit on staying; residents share expenses (about $125–$250 a week per Oxford House's FAQ), and rooms are filled by a vote of current residents after an interview. Use the official vacancy locator at oxfordvacancies.com or call (800) 689-6411.

Kinds of help: Recovery housing; Groups: People in substance-use recovery

phone: (800) 689-6411; (301) 587-2916

address: Oxford House, Inc. national office: 1010 Wayne Avenue, Suite 300, Silver Spring, MD 20910 (houses are located in communities nationwide, including Arizona)

website: https://www.oxfordhouse.org/

hours: No fixed hours; call a house contact to arrange an interview (interview times vary by house and are listed on the vacancy locator).

Services Offered

- Sober-living recovery residences: shared houses run democratically by the residents, providing a drug- and alcohol-free living environment with peer support; no paid clinical staff and no fixed length of stay (average stay is about a year, and some members stay longer).
- Application pathway: find houses with openings on the official vacancy locator (oxfordvacancies.com), call the house contact to arrange an interview, or use the online application to send your information to all nearby houses with openings; current residents vote on acceptance.
- Oxford House World Services can answer questions and help with referrals: (800) 689-6411 or (301) 587-2916.

Eligibility Requirements

- Adults in recovery from drug and alcohol addiction (substance use disorder) who commit to staying drug- and alcohol-free; anyone who returns to using alcohol or drugs is immediately expelled.
- No specific amount of sober/clean time is required; Oxford House says people generally come into a house after a 28-day rehabilitation program or at least a 5–10-day detoxification program.
- You must be able to pay your equal share of house expenses and live without being disruptive; acceptance is by a vote of the current residents after an interview.
- Houses are gender-specific (men's houses, women's houses, and some houses for parents with children).

Population Served

- Adults in recovery from substance use disorder, including people leaving treatment or detox and people rebuilding income and stability; the network includes houses across the U.S., with Arizona houses in the Phoenix metro area including Mesa.

How to Best Connect

- Official vacancy locator: oxfordvacancies.com — filter by state/county to see houses with openings, each listing a contact person and an interview time; phone before visiting (there is no walk-in admission).
- Online application: the 'Apply Online' option at oxfordvacancies.com sends your application to all houses near you with openings.
- Phone: Oxford House, Inc. at (800) 689-6411 (toll-free) or (301) 587-2916; national office: 1010 Wayne Avenue, Suite 300, Silver Spring, MD 20910.
- For Mesa/Phoenix metro houses, search Arizona in the locator and follow up with each house contact. Arizona has no separate state website — use these national routes.

Important Information to Know

- Cost: each member pays an 'equal expense share' (EES) covering rent, utilities and household staples; Oxford House's FAQ says the amount varies from house to house and can range from about $125 to $250 per week — ask each house. Move-in usually requires a nonrefundable fee plus payment for the first two weeks.
- Vacancies and house lists change often — check the live locator rather than relying on a saved list; if one house does not accept you, apply at another.
- Oxford Houses are not treatment programs and have no clinical staff; they are self-run. Staying requires sobriety, paying your share and not being disruptive.
- The network is large nationally (more than 3,500 houses) — plan to contact more than one house and ask each about their interview time.

Sources: Oxford House, Inc. – official site (model, how to apply, FAQ, contact) · Oxford Vacancies – official house vacancy locator · State Websites – Oxford House, Inc. (Arizona: no state site)

Paz de Cristo (COPA Health) · Mesa coordinated-entry access point — usable

A Mesa coordinated-entry access point operated by COPA Health at the Paz de Cristo community center: help with shelter and housing connections, behavioral health referrals, ID and document assistance, AHCCCS and SNAP applications, and employment search for people who are homeless or unstably housed.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points; Groups: Spanish

phone: 480-993-7740 or 480-620-8695 (COPA Health community navigation office); 480-464-2370 (Paz de Cristo main)

address: 424 W. Broadway Rd., Mesa, AZ 85210

website: https://pazdecristo.org/

hours: Listed navigation-office hours: Monday–Friday, 10 a.m.–3 p.m. (call to confirm the office is staffed before visiting)

Services Offered

A community navigation office (Mesa coordinated-entry access point) operated by COPA Health at the Paz de Cristo community center. Staff help people who are homeless or unstably housed get connected to housing and shelter resources, including shelter lists, low-income housing information and the coordinated-entry assessment process, plus behavioral health referrals (COPA Health), employment search help, Arizona ID and birth-certificate assistance, and AHCCCS and SNAP applications. The same center runs a daily evening meal (about 5:15–6:30 p.m.), showers (recent listing: Wednesdays 9 a.m.–1 p.m.; the schedule can change by season), clothing and haircuts, food boxes (recent listing: the first three Tuesdays of the month, 9–11 a.m.) and a mail service (listed weekdays 4–6 p.m.), so people who come for a meal can also ask about the navigation office.

Eligibility Requirements

No eligibility rules are published specifically for the navigation office; coordinated entry serves people who are homeless or need housing help in Maricopa County. Call first to confirm requirements and whether an appointment is needed. For the center's own services, published listings state that services are for adults 18 and older, with children required to be accompanied by an adult for meal service; photo ID is required for food boxes and mail service, and food boxes also require proof of local residence within the last 30 days.

Population Served

People who are homeless or housing-insecure in Mesa and nearby East Valley communities. Paz de Cristo serves adults, seniors, families and children (children must be accompanied by an adult), and its services are available in English and Spanish.

How to Best Connect

Visit 424 W. Broadway Rd., Mesa, AZ 85210. Listed hours for the navigation office are Monday–Friday, 10 a.m.–3 p.m.; call before going to confirm the office is staffed that day. Phone options: 480-993-7740 or 480-620-8695 (COPA Health community navigation office) and 480-464-2370 (Paz de Cristo main line). COPA Health's main office: 480-969-3800, 924 N. Country Club Dr., Mesa.

Important Information to Know

This is an assessment and connection point, not an emergency shelter — visiting or calling does not guarantee a shelter bed or housing; it starts the connection and assessment process. City resource guides have listed it as a Mesa/East Valley coordinated-entry site (Scottsdale guide, March 2025); confirm current staffing, hours and the assessment process by phone before sending someone. No cost information is published for the navigation services; ask when you call. The office is located inside a community center that serves meals, which makes it reachable for people who are unsheltered.

Sources: Coordinated Entry Access Points - MAG / Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care · City of Scottsdale - Resources for people experiencing homelessness (March 2025; East Valley access points) · City of Mesa Police Department — Quick Reference Guide (COPA Health Community Navigation Office) · Paz de Cristo — Contact Us · 2-1-1 Arizona — Paz de Cristo Community Center listing (last assured 04/01/2026) · Copa Health — Contacts & Locations

Phoenix Dream Center · Life Recovery Program (residential recovery for men and women) — usable

No-cost, Christ-centered residential recovery for men and women leaving homelessness and addiction, with 160 beds for young men and 45 beds for young women, onsite medical and behavioral health care, eye and dental care, and career training. Intake by phone.

Kinds of help: Recovery housing; Groups: People in substance-use recovery, Re-entry

phone: 602-816-6582 (men's program); 602-706-1876 (women's program); 602-346-8716 (general assistance); 602-346-8700 (main)

address: 3210 Grand Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85017

website: https://phoenixdreamcenter.org/get-help/

hours: Not published; intake is by phone — allow up to 72 business hours for a callback

Services Offered

A free residential recovery program for people who are homeless and/or struggling with addiction. It provides safe housing (160 beds for young men and 45 beds for young women), structured daily programming, addiction-recovery classes, counseling and mentoring, an onsite medical center, a behavioral health team, eye care and dental care, physical wellness programs, life-skills training and career/job preparation. The center reports serving about 500 people a year through this program. The organization also runs a crisis-response outreach team and a separate residential program for survivors of human trafficking (Where Hope Lives).

Eligibility Requirements

The program is for men and women dealing with addiction and homelessness; all services are described as completely free, and the center says its services are not conditional on a person's faith. Specific rules — age range, medical or mental-health criteria, program length — are not published. Call the men's or women's intake line to confirm fit and requirements.

Population Served

Men and women; the center describes its beds as for young men and young women and serves people leaving homelessness, addiction and related crises. Exact age limits are not published — confirm by phone. The organization also describes serving people who have experienced incarceration (including formerly incarcerated individuals) or foster care, and a separate program serves trafficking survivors.

How to Best Connect

Call the program directly: men's Life Recovery 602-816-6582; women's Life Recovery 602-706-1876; general assistance 602-346-8716; main line 602-346-8700. Or use the contact form on the Get Help page: phoenixdreamcenter.org/get-help. The page advises that call lines are busy and asks callers to allow up to 72 business hours for a response; if you are in an emergency or an unsafe situation, call 911 or seek medical care. Location: 3210 Grand Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85017 (public tours Tuesdays at noon; for help, call first).

Important Information to Know

There is no charge for the recovery program or its services. The program is faith-based (Christ-centered) while the center states services are not conditional on faith. Current openings and wait times are not published — ask when you call. Length of stay, age limits and screening rules are not published and older community listings may not reflect current policy, so verify before referring.

Sources: Phoenix Dream Center — Get Help · Phoenix Dream Center — What We Do (Life Recovery Program) · Phoenix Dream Center — Who We Are · GuideStar — Phoenix Dream Center nonprofit narrative (Life Recovery helps formerly incarcerated individuals)

Phoenix Rescue Mission · Changing Lives Center (women and children residential recovery) — usable

Phoenix Rescue Mission's women-and-children campus: a 12-month, faith-based residential recovery program plus a 7–30 day short-term crisis stay for women facing homelessness, addiction and trauma; children can stay with their mothers, with childcare and school continuity. Intake by appointment.

Kinds of help: Recovery housing; Groups: Women, Families

phone: 602-233-3000 (intake)

address: 338 N. 15th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007

website: https://phoenixrescuemission.org/help/substance-abuse-trauma/recovery-program/

hours: Intake office: Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–4 p.m. (appointments required; no walk-ins)

Services Offered

Residential recovery for women and their children at the Changing Lives Center for Women & Children. The core program, Transformations, is a 12-month faith-based residency with four 13-week phases: addiction and mental-health treatment classes, individual and group counseling, case management, life skills, workforce development and job preparation, legal support, medical care and medication access, and transportation to appointments. Childcare is provided for infants and toddlers, and a local elementary-school partnership keeps school-age children in class; parent reunification is a goal when child-welfare involvement exists. Graduates receive six months of aftercare plus alumni support. The first step can be short-term residential care (Rescue-Assess-Place): a typical 7–30 day stay with food, a bed, clothing, showers, assessment and referrals.

Eligibility Requirements

For women dealing with homelessness, addiction and/or trauma; mothers may bring their children. The programs are voluntary — the person must want change. Intake includes an assessment to place each person in the right program; age and medical criteria are not published. No fee is stated for the short-term program, and basic needs are covered in the year-long program — confirm any other costs at intake.

Population Served

Women, including mothers with children, experiencing homelessness, addiction and trauma. The Mission's short-term care also serves women fleeing domestic violence. Men are served separately at the Transforming Lives Center (1801 S. 35th Ave., Phoenix).

How to Best Connect

Call (602) 233-3000 to schedule an intake appointment. Intake office hours: Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–4 p.m.; appointments are required and walk-ins are not allowed, but same-day appointments are often available. Location: Changing Lives Center for Women & Children, 338 N. 15th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007. Family members may call on behalf of a willing loved one. Program information: phoenixrescuemission.org/help/substance-abuse-trauma/recovery-program/.

Important Information to Know

The program is faith-based (Christ-centered). The short-term stay is typically 7–30 days; the recovery program runs 12 months in four 13-week phases. Appointments are required — no walk-ins. Current capacity, wait times and exact screening rules are not published; call to ask about openings. Some older listings show different intake numbers; (602) 233-3000 is the current intake line.

Sources: Phoenix Rescue Mission — Recovery Program (Transformations) · Phoenix Rescue Mission — Short-term Care (Rescue-Assess-Place) · Phoenix Rescue Mission — Contact & Locations

Phoenix Rescue Mission · Street Outreach Hotline (Hope Coach) and Get Help line — usable

Street-outreach hotline and help line for people living unsheltered in Maricopa County: request a Hope Coach visit (water, hygiene supplies, case management, ID help, housing navigation) or start the Mission's housing-assistance and residential-program intake by phone.

Kinds of help: Street outreach & mobile services; Groups: Spanish, Homeless

phone: 602-346-3361 (street outreach/Hope Coach hotline); 602-233-3000 (main line — outreach requests, intake, housing help)

address: 1540 W. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (Mission Support Center — call first; outreach is mobile)

website: https://phoenixrescuemission.org/help/homeless-outreach/

email: outreach@phxmission.org

hours: Hotline hours not published; residential intake office Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–4 p.m. by appointment

Services Offered

A hotline to request help from the Mission's street-outreach team: Hope Coach vehicles travel the streets of Maricopa County with water, hygiene kits (Hope Totes), seasonal items and case management from peer-support-certified case managers, help obtaining IDs and documents, and connection to housing or recovery programs. Through the Housing Assistance Program, staff help with housing applications, documents, locating an apartment, vouchers (decided case-by-case), security deposits and basic move-in needs, furniture and household items, and continuing case management. The Mission's short-term residential care (7–30 days) and recovery programs also start through the same intake line.

Eligibility Requirements

No eligibility rules are published for the outreach hotline; it serves people experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Residential programs have their own screening and are voluntary; housing vouchers are generally state- or federally funded and decided case-by-case. Requirements differ by program — ask when you call. Families with children are generally routed to Maricopa County's Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700).

Population Served

People living unsheltered in Maricopa County; the Mission describes outreach to men, women and children on the streets, and its case managers place hundreds of people into housing or recovery programs each year. Spanish-language pages and materials are available; ask about Spanish-speaking staff when you call.

How to Best Connect

Official pages list two phone routes: the Get Help page directs Hope Coach visit requests to 602-346-3361; the Homeless Outreach and Housing Assistance pages direct visit requests, intake and housing help to 602-233-3000. Give your nearest cross streets when requesting a visit. Online: help/inquiry form at phoenixrescuemission.org/help. Email: outreach@phxmission.org. Address for mail/administration: Mission Support Center, 1540 W. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85007 — call first; services are mobile or by appointment. Residential campuses: men's, 1801 S. 35th Ave.; women and children's, 338 N. 15th Ave.

Important Information to Know

This is not a walk-in emergency shelter — help starts with a phone call, and outreach teams come to people on the street. Both published numbers are official Mission routes; if one does not connect, try the other. For emergencies, call 911. No fees are listed for Mission programs; the short-term program states no fee and housing vouchers are case-by-case. Hotline hours and outreach schedules are not published — call to confirm; teams serve Maricopa County. Phoenix Rescue Mission serves as a Maricopa County coordinated-entry point for adults without children through this outreach hotline. Capacity and wait times are not published.

Sources: Phoenix Rescue Mission — Homeless Outreach (Hope Coach) · Phoenix Rescue Mission — Get Help · Phoenix Rescue Mission — Housing Assistance Program · Maricopa Association of Governments — Coordinated Entry Points (Adults without Children) · Maricopa County coordinated entry access points - Native American Connections (Community Bridges and CBI PATH hotline listings) · City of Scottsdale - Resources for people experiencing homelessness (March 2025; East Valley access points) · City of Scottsdale — Homelessness and Panhandling in Scottsdale (updated June 9, 2026) · City of Mesa - Homeless Resources (helplines and crisis numbers)

Prisma Community Care (formerly Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS) – HIV housing assistance (HOPWA) — usable

Phoenix HIV/AIDS health and wellness organization (renamed from Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS on Jan. 1, 2025) that people living with HIV/AIDS can contact about housing assistance; the City of Phoenix HOPWA program it is connected to provides short-term rent, mortgage and utility help, move-in costs and housing case management in Maricopa and Pinal counties.

Kinds of help: Health-linked housing (respite & HIV/AIDS); Groups: People living with HIV/AIDS, Spanish

phone: 602-307-5330 (Prisma); 602-534-2433 (City of Phoenix HOPWA intake)

address: 1101 N. Central Ave., Ste. 200, Phoenix, AZ 85004

website: https://prismacare.org/

email: contactus@prismacare.org

hours: Wellness center (primary care, mental health, social services): Mon–Thu 8 a.m.–7 p.m.; Fri 8 a.m.–3 p.m. Call before visiting — other listings show different hours.

Services Offered

Prisma Community Care — formerly Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS — is a Phoenix HIV/AIDS health and wellness organization that people living with HIV/AIDS can contact about housing assistance. It is one of the local organizations funded through the City of Phoenix HOPWA (Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS) program. HOPWA housing help in this area includes short-term rent, mortgage and utility payments to stop an eviction, foreclosure or utility shut-off; move-in costs such as security deposits, first and last month's rent, and application fees; and housing case management. Ask specifically about HOPWA housing assistance when you call.

Eligibility Requirements

At least one household member must be diagnosed with HIV or AIDS; household income must be at or below 80% of area median income; the household must currently live in Maricopa or Pinal County; and for short-term rent/utility help, the household generally must not already be receiving housing or utility assistance from another housing program. Prisma serves people living with HIV and the wider community; final eligibility for each housing program is decided by the agency administering it.

Population Served

People living with or affected by HIV/AIDS and their families, including partners and spouses. The organization also serves 2SLGBTQIA+ people, people of color, and uninsured or underinsured Arizonans. Services are provided in English and Spanish, with interpretation available for other languages.

How to Best Connect

Call Prisma at 602-307-5330 (1101 N. Central Ave., Ste. 200, Phoenix; email contactus@prismacare.org) and ask about HOPWA housing assistance. The citywide HOPWA intake is also available: call 602-534-AIDE (602-534-2433), choose option 3, then choose a Family Services Center, then option 3 again to speak with staff — say you are asking about HOPWA. You can also apply online at phoenix.gov/HOPWA. Intake hours are Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–5 p.m. The office is in downtown Phoenix; plan for a drive or transit trip from Mesa.

Important Information to Know

The organization changed its name from Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS on January 1, 2025 — both names mean the same place. Prisma's website highlights medical and wellness services and does not spell out its housing help, so confirm what housing support is currently available for your situation when you call; the citywide HOPWA intake line is the most reliable route to HOPWA housing programs. HOPWA help is grant-funded for eligible households and is not guaranteed — waitlists, appointment availability and funding vary. Published office hours differ slightly between Prisma's website and other directories, so call before visiting. Expect to verify HIV diagnosis, income and county of residence during eligibility screening.

Sources: Contact Us – Prisma Community Care (address, phone, hours) · About – Prisma Community Care (formerly Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS, renamed Jan. 1, 2025) · CDC National Prevention Information Network – Prisma Community Care (HOPWA among support services; phone, email, languages) · HOPWA STRMU & Permanent Housing Placement flyer (English/Spanish) — eligibility, Central Intake steps, business hours · Maricopa County Board of Supervisors minutes, Aug. 28, 2023 – HOPWA Fact Sheet listing organizations directly funded by City of Phoenix HOPWA

Respiro Emergency Shelter (Keys to Change · Key Campus) — usable

A 95-bed emergency shelter for adults experiencing homelessness on Key Campus in downtown Phoenix, operated by Keys to Change. Guests can stay 24/7, and pets, service animals, and emotional support animals are allowed; on-site housing navigation and Key Campus services are available. Placement is made through the Brian Garcia Welcome Center's Coordinated Entry process.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Pet owners

phone: 602-883-4509 (Brian Garcia Welcome Center intake); 602-229-5155 (Key Campus main line)

address: Key Campus, 204 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007

website: https://keystochangeaz.org/key-campus/

hours: Guests may stay on site 24/7; intake through the Brian Garcia Welcome Center, open 24 hours a day (assessment visits guided to 7am–11pm)

Services Offered

Respiro is a 95-bed emergency shelter on Key Campus operated by Keys to Change (opened March 2022; 6,300-square-foot structure). Guests can stay in the shelter 24/7, not just overnight. The co-ed space has air conditioning, beds, showers, restrooms, storage for belongings, vending machines, and an outdoor patio. Pets, service animals, and emotional support animals are allowed, and Key Campus has a designated dog run plus pet services through the Arizona Pet Project. On-site housing navigation staff work with guests toward housing, and the campus provides access to ID replacement, health and dental care, state benefits, meals, showers, and more.

Eligibility Requirements

Adults experiencing homelessness who come through the single-adult Coordinated Entry process — placement starts at the Brian Garcia Welcome Center on the same campus, which provides diversion, screening, and shelter referrals. No shelter fee is listed; confirm any costs at intake. Pet owners are welcome; confirm current pet requirements when applying.

Population Served

Adults experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County who are referred through Coordinated Entry, including pet owners. The shelter is co-ed and has 95 beds.

How to Best Connect

Go to the Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix (Key Campus) — open 24 hours a day; visit from 7am–11pm to begin an assessment. Call ahead: 602-883-4509; Key Campus main line: 602-229-5155. No separate Respiro intake line is published — ask for Respiro by name at the Welcome Center. From Mesa, you can call 2-1-1 to find the nearest Coordinated Entry point, but Respiro is located on Key Campus in Phoenix.

Important Information to Know

Beds are limited: Respiro has operated at or near full capacity since it opened, so placement is not guaranteed and depends on shelter availability and Coordinated Entry priority. The shelter permits pets and service/emotional support animals and lets guests remain on site during the day — confirm current pet rules, storage limits, and the referral flow with Keys to Change. Published details about capacity and pet policies can become dated, so verify current conditions at intake rather than relying on secondhand information.

Sources: Keys to Change - Key Campus services (shelter, Welcome Center, housing match) · Keys to Change e-Newsletter (June 12, 2024) – Respiro shelter details (co-ed; service and emotional support animals) · Keys to Change / Human Services Campus – CauseIQ profile · Keys to Change community meeting minutes – August 13, 2024 (Respiro full at 95) · Keys to Change – 2-1-1 Arizona record (last assured 03/01/2026; eligibility, fees, in-person service, no documents)

Safe Place — Youth Crisis Entry (Ages 12–17), Maricopa County — usable

Immediate crisis help for youth ages 12–17 in Maricopa County who are homeless, have run away, or need help for any reason. Youth can call or text for help, or walk into any designated Safe Place site — QuikTrip stores, Valley Metro locations, libraries, fire departments, transit stops and participating businesses — ask staff for Safe Place, and wait in a safe, private area until responders arrive to arrange placement and services.

Kinds of help: Children & youth crisis care; Groups: Youth

phone: 602-841-5799 (24-hour crisis line); text SAFE and your location to 44357

website: https://www.childcrisisaz.org/

hours: Crisis line answered 24 hours a day; Safe Place sites (QuikTrip stores, libraries, fire departments, public transit stops and other participating businesses) can be used during each site's public hours. Valley-wide sites and the phone/text lines cover Mesa and all of Maricopa County.

Services Offered

Safe Place is a national youth-outreach program. Youth ages 12–17 who have run away, are homeless, or need help for any reason can use it to get immediate assistance. A youth can go into any designated Safe Place site — look for the Safe Place sign — ask staff for help, and wait in a safe, private place until trained responders arrive. QuikTrip convenience stores, libraries, fire departments, public transit stops and local businesses across the Valley are designated Safe Place sites (per Maricopa County's current help page), and Child Crisis Arizona states that all QuikTrip and Valley Metro locations are designated as Safe Place locations. Youth (or anyone helping them) can also call the 24-hour crisis line at 602-841-5799 or text SAFE and their location/address to 44357 (4HELP). Child Crisis Arizona is the local Safe Place agency: its staff respond to these calls and several beds in its Independent Living Program are reserved for youth who need an overnight place to stay.

Eligibility Requirements

Youth ages 12–17 in Maricopa County who are runaway, homeless, or 'seeking assistance for any reason,' including family conflict and abuse. No income test, application, or formal referral is described — help starts by calling, texting, or going to a Safe Place site. The program serves youth only; adults need different entry points in the county's homeless services system.

Population Served

Youth ages 12–17 across Maricopa County, including Mesa and the East Valley, through valley-wide sites and transit access. Users include youth who are homeless, have run away, or are in an unsafe or unstable family situation.

How to Best Connect

- Call the 24-hour crisis line: 602-841-5799.
- Text SAFE plus your current location or address to 44357 (4HELP).
- Or go to any Safe Place site and ask staff for help: QuikTrip stores, libraries, fire departments, transit stops and other participating businesses across Maricopa County. Staff will contact the Safe Place line, and the youth can wait on site until help arrives; site staff can make the call for a youth who arrives in person.
Use the current instructions above (see the note below about the older 69866 code).

Important Information to Know

- The currently published instruction is to text SAFE and your location to 44357; the older code 69866 — still used by Safe Place programs elsewhere and in older Maricopa-area guides — was the earlier text-to-locate instruction. Use 44357 or the crisis line to avoid confusion.
- Child Crisis Arizona has been the local home of Safe Place since 2021; the program was run locally before that by Tumbleweed and then UMOM. The county's current help page lists the same crisis line for youth ages 12–17.
- What happens after contact depends on the situation and bed availability; overnight shelter is not guaranteed, and no costs or fees are published — ask when calling.

Sources: Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · Child Crisis Arizona — 'Child Crisis Arizona is home to Safe Place' (local program; 24-hour crisis line; Independent Living Program beds; all QuikTrip and Valley Metro locations) · Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care - Get Help · Maricopa Regional CoC Homeless Resources (older guide listing the legacy youth text code 69866)

Sanctuary Recovery Centers / Sanctuary Sober Living — Residential Treatment and Recovery Housing, North Phoenix — usable

Residential behavioral-health treatment with integrated substance-use care and built-in supportive housing at licensed recovery homes in the Phoenix–Scottsdale area. The core model is a recommended 90-day residential program, with step-down to PHP, IOP, virtual IOP, sober living, and case management; Phoenix sites include SRC 28th (17632 N 28th St) and SRC Friess (4324 E Friess Dr), plus additional provider-listed homes. Entry is through admissions and a clinical assessment, not a rental application.

Kinds of help: Recovery housing; Groups: People in substance-use recovery

phone: 877-978-8783 / 844-978-8783 (toll-free); 480-309-9945 (local / intake); 602-612-3816 (registry-listed main)

address: SRC 28th: 17632 N 28th St, Phoenix, AZ 85032; SRC Friess: 4324 E Friess Dr, Phoenix, AZ 85032; office: 11645 N Cave Creek Rd, Phoenix, AZ 85020 (home addresses are not published by the operator; use for reference)

website: https://www.sanctuaryrecoverycenters.com/

hours: Office: Monday–Friday 8:30 a.m.–5:30 p.m.; residential homes provide 24-hour care. Intake/admission begins with a call or the website's insurance-verification/contact form — call to schedule a screening.

Services Offered

- Residential (inpatient-style) behavioral health treatment with integrated substance-use care; a 90-day residential stay is the recommended core program, with stabilization and, when clinically appropriate, ambulatory detox as part of the plan.
- Supportive housing is built into the residential program — clients live in the agency's licensed behavioral health residential homes during treatment — and continued care includes sober living, a partial hospitalization program (PHP), intensive outpatient (IOP), virtual IOP, case management, medication management (including medication-based treatments for opioid and alcohol use disorders), and individual, group, and family therapy.
- Homes listed by the provider include The Friess Home, The Rose Garden, and The Roadrunner Home (the adolescent home), among others (Blossom, Rosemonte, Charter Oak, Mercer). Registry and Medicaid-directory addresses include SRC 28th (17632 N 28th St) and SRC Friess (4324 E Friess Dr), with SRC Rose Garden at 2027 E Rose Garden Ln; the administrative office is 11645 N Cave Creek Rd, Phoenix 85020.
- The provider also runs a reentry program (work and life skills) and discharge/next-step planning; the provider states it is Joint Commission certified at all levels of care and LegitScript certified.

Eligibility Requirements

- Adults who need residential-level care for substance use and/or co-occurring mental-health conditions; admission follows a clinical assessment arranged through the admissions/intake line.
- Directory listings describe the Friess and Rose Garden homes as adult men's residences (ages 18–25 and 26–64), and the operator describes gender-specific homes for adults — confirm current gender and age rules for the specific home at intake.
- The agency also runs a separate adolescent program (ages 12–17) at its Roadrunner Home in Phoenix; that is a different program from the adult recovery residences described here.
- No income-based eligibility is published; payment and coverage are reviewed at intake.

Population Served

Adults (18+) in the Phoenix metro area with substance-use and co-occurring mental-health needs who require residential structure and recovery housing; at least two homes (Friess, Rose Garden) are listed as serving adult men, and the provider states its homes are gender-specific. Separate adolescent services exist for ages 12–17.

How to Best Connect

- Call admissions/intake: 877-978-8783 or 844-978-8783 (toll-free), 480-309-9945 (local/intake), or 602-612-3816 (registry-listed main number).
- Website: sanctuaryrecoverycenters.com (contact form and insurance verification). Ask directly about current openings, an assessment appointment, which home fits (SRC 28th vs. Friess vs. Rose Garden), and coverage.
- Office: 11645 N Cave Creek Rd, Phoenix, AZ 85020; office hours Monday–Friday 8:30 a.m.–5:30 p.m.
- If you have AHCCCS/Medicaid or private insurance, ask intake to confirm network participation for the specific home before committing.

Important Information to Know

- This is treatment-linked housing: entry is through a clinical admissions process (assessment), not a housing waitlist; expect a structured program rather than a standard lease.
- Costs are not published, and coverage signals conflict: one third-party directory for the Friess home lists private and public insurance accepted but no payment assistance or sliding fee scale; listings for other homes show more limited insurance data; the registry record carries an Arizona Medicaid identifier. Verify cost and coverage for the specific home at intake; low- or no-cost placement is not established.
- Bed availability and exact referral requirements are not published; call for current capacity. The company is registered in the national provider registry as a substance-abuse rehabilitation facility and describes its homes as licensed behavioral health residential properties.
- Numbers and hours differ across pages and directories (the website displays multiple phone lines; a Medicaid-plan directory lists SRC 28th as Monday–Friday 9 a.m.–4 p.m. while the operator site lists office hours 8:30 a.m.–5:30 p.m.) — admissions can route callers and confirm a specific home's hours.
- A third-party description of the reentry/sober-living track says securing work is required for continued housing — confirm this for the specific program at intake.

Sources: Sanctuary Recovery Centers — Inpatient Recovery Program (90-day residential model; licensed behavioral health residential properties; built-in supportive housing; office address and hours; toll-free and local numbers) · Sanctuary Recovery Centers — The Friess Home (residential behavioral health treatment; 24/7 support) · Sanctuary Recovery Centers — Adolescent Program (ages 12–17; The Roadrunner Home, Phoenix) · SAMHSA National Directory of Mental Health Treatment Facilities 2025 — Sanctuary Sober Living LLC DBA Sanctuary Recovery Centers / SRC 28th / SRC Rose Garden (main 602-612-3816; intake 480-309-9945) · Molina Healthcare of Arizona (Medicaid) provider directory (current as of 03/18/2026) — SRC 28th, SRC Rose Garden, and Sanctuary Sober Living entries · National provider registry — Sanctuary Sober Living LLC, 4324 E Friess Dr (SRC Friess; phone 602-612-3816; updated 3/30/2026) · National provider registry — Sanctuary Sober Living LLC, 17632 N 28th St (SRC 28th; phone 602-612-3816) · National provider registry — Sanctuary Sober Living LLC, 2027 E Rose Garden Ln (SRC Rose Garden; phone 602-612-3816) · Facility profile — SRC Friess (adult men 18–25 and 26–64; residential levels; no payment assistance or sliding scale listed)

Save the Family — Rapid Rehousing and FACES Supportive Services (Mesa) — usable

Rapid Rehousing for families with children facing homelessness in Mesa and Maricopa County — help finding housing plus time-limited, stepped-down rental assistance — paired with Save the Family's FACES (Families, Adults and Children's Empowerment Services) supportive services, case management, and children's programming on the path to permanent housing and self-sufficiency.

Kinds of help: Rent and deposit help; Groups: Families

phone: (480) 898-0228 (Save the Family); 602-595-8700 (Family Housing Hub intake)

address: 125 E. University Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://savethefamily.org/

hours: Office: Monday–Friday 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m.; closed weekends. Family intake goes through the Family Housing Hub — call 602-595-8700 for the current intake process and hours.

Services Offered

- Rapid Rehousing: help locating housing in the community (in many cases families hold their own lease) and time-limited, stepped-down rental assistance — public descriptions include help with utility deposits and rental application fees, with support scaled back as the family's income grows.
- Case management: a case manager helps parents set and reach goals — employment, budgeting, benefits, documentation, referrals — throughout the family's journey and after move-in.
- FACES (Families, Adults and Children's Empowerment Services): the organization's supportive-services program — career development services, parent support services (life skills, budgeting, parenting, domestic-violence/trauma healing, legal services), mental health and substance-abuse support, and children's services (childcare, after-school/summer programs). FACES also serves residents of ARM affordable housing.
- Additional programs: Save the Family also operates Tenant-Based Rental Assistance and Step Up to Independence transitional housing (described as up to two years, targeting families in doubled-up or temporary situations); ask which programs are accepting families now.

Eligibility Requirements

- Families with children who are experiencing homelessness; the program serves parents and children together, and children's programming is a standard part of services.
- Families in Maricopa County are screened through the Family Housing Hub, the county's coordinated-entry point for families — call 602-595-8700. Save the Family states it accepts incoming clients from the Family Housing Hub.
- Specific income rules and required documents are not published; intake staff will explain what is needed.

Population Served

Families with children who are homeless or facing homelessness in Mesa, the East Valley, and Maricopa County. Program descriptions include families in shelter and families who are doubled up, living in cars, or couch-surfing.

How to Best Connect

- First step for families: call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 (3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix) — the county's coordinated entry for families — and ask about Save the Family's programs.
- Direct questions: Save the Family, (480) 898-0228, 125 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85201; office hours Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m. Ask for Rapid Rehousing and supportive-services (FACES) information.
- For Step Up to Independence, Save the Family's Find Help page links a pre-screen questionnaire; website savethefamily.org ('How We Help' and 'Find Help').

Important Information to Know

- Rapid Rehousing is time-limited, stepped-down rental assistance — not a guaranteed long-term subsidy or an immediate placement. Current openings depend on funding and capacity, which are not published; call to check.
- Eligibility is not guaranteed by the office; families are screened through the coordinated-entry process, and participation includes working with a case manager.
- Save the Family describes support over 6–12 months as families build income and stability, with follow-up care continuing up to six months after exit.
- Costs: families take on more of the rent as assistance steps down; exact rent-share and any fees are not published — ask intake.

Sources: Save the Family — How We Help (three housing programs; supportive services; Mesa office, hours, phone) · Save the Family — Find Help (accepts incoming clients from the Family Housing Hub; Step Up pre-screen questionnaire) · Save the Family 2025 Impact Report — ARM (170+ scattered homes; co-developed communities; Resource Centers; 6–12 month support) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · City of Mesa Council Report (May 19, 2025) — Save the Family Homeless Families Intervention Project: Rapid Rehousing, TBRA, Step Up to Independence · City of Mesa Council Report (April 20, 2020) — FACES: Families, Adults and Children's Empowerment Services

Short-Term Crisis Services (STCS) — Arizona DES emergency housing help for families with children — usable

State emergency-assistance program for low-income families with children who are in a crisis. Help can include rent or mortgage payments to prevent homelessness, emergency shelter/temporary housing, utility help, and move-in costs; applications are made through the local Community Action Agency (MesaCAN in Mesa).

Kinds of help: Rent, utility & deposit crisis assistance; Groups: Families

phone: 480-833-9200 (Mesa intake); 602-506-5911 (Maricopa County Human Services)

address: 635 E. Broadway Rd., Mesa, AZ 85204 (Mesa intake)

website: https://des.az.gov/stcs

email: mesacanclient@turnanewleaf.org

hours: Mesa intake site (MesaCAN): Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:30 p.m.; call to confirm intake steps and funding availability.

Services Offered

- Emergency financial assistance for eligible families during a crisis: rent or mortgage payments to prevent homelessness, emergency shelter/temporary housing, utility payments and deposits, rental deposits, and move-in assistance (first and last month's rent, security deposits, application fees).
- Help related to getting or keeping a job, such as transportation assistance and tools or equipment.
- Applications, decisions, and payments are handled by the local Community Action Agency (or another contracted partner); DES administers and funds the program.

Eligibility Requirements

- Arizona resident at the time of application.
- A dependent child under age 18 must live in the home and meet U.S. citizen or qualified legal resident criteria.
- Total gross household income at or below 125% of the Federal Poverty Guidelines — or 150% if the household includes someone age 60 or older or a person with a disability.
- Must be facing a temporary, unforeseen emergency crisis the household cannot meet with its own income or resources, and be able to explain and prove the emergency need (examples: family separation caused by domestic violence, loss of income, an unforeseen event, or a situation that endangers the family's health and safety).
- Help is limited to once in a 12-month period and is not guaranteed.

Population Served

- Low-income Arizona families with minor children in a housing or financial crisis, including families facing homelessness or eviction and families leaving unsafe homes. Mesa and Maricopa County residents apply through their local Community Action Agency.

How to Best Connect

- Apply through your local Community Action Agency — applications are handled at the local level.
- Mesa: Mesa Community Action Network (MesaCAN, a program of A New Leaf), 635 E. Broadway Rd., Mesa, AZ 85204; call (480) 833-9200; email mesacanclient@turnanewleaf.org; office hours Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:30 p.m. Call to confirm current intake steps and what to bring — expect to explain the emergency and show proof (for example an eviction or utility notice and proof of income).
- Other Maricopa County areas: Maricopa County Human Services, 234 N. Central Ave., Suite 3000, Phoenix, AZ 85009, (602) 506-5911; Glendale: Glendale Community Action Programs, (623) 930-2000; Phoenix: City of Phoenix Human Services Department. The full statewide list is at des.az.gov/CommunityActionAgencies.
- Phone lines get very busy and demand is high — keep trying.

Important Information to Know

- Funding is limited; assistance can be unavailable even for eligible families, and agencies may take applications only while funds last.
- The program can help with emergency shelter/temporary housing, and DES policy defines temporary housing as possibly including a hotel/motel or other transient setting — but whether motel-type help is available for a given family depends on the local agency and current funding. Confirm by phone before relying on it.
- DES materials describe the income ceiling slightly differently (a community-services brochure lists 150% overall); the program standard is 125% generally, or 150% for households with someone 60+ or a person with a disability.
- DES program policy took effect July 1, 2026; eligibility and documents are verified by the local agency.

Sources: Short-Term Crisis Services — Arizona Department of Economic Security · DES Short-Term Crisis Services Policy (effective 07/01/2026) · DES Community Action Agencies — local intake contacts · Community Action Services (CAP-1001A) — program list and agency directory · Mesa Community Action Network (MesaCAN) — A New Leaf

Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Coordinated Entry Access Point and Charitable Assistance — usable

St. Vincent de Paul's Phoenix campus is a central charitable-help location in Maricopa County and is listed as a coordinated-entry access point for seniors experiencing homelessness. It connects people to shelter and housing resources and offers one-time rent/utility help through neighborhood church conferences, food and meals (including a Mesa dining room), weather relief, and medical/dental services. Call 602-266-4673 (602-266-HOPE) to confirm what is available before going.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points, Rent, utility & deposit crisis assistance; Groups: Seniors

phone: 602-266-4673 (602-266-HOPE)

address: 420 W. Watkins Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85003

website: https://stvincentdepaul.net/get-help

email: info@svdpaz.org

hours: Hours vary by listing: Main Campus page Monday–Saturday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.; senior coordinated-entry listing Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–4 p.m.; 2-1-1 lists Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m. for administration. Call before visiting.

Services Offered

- Coordinated-entry access point for seniors experiencing homelessness, listed at SVdP's 420 W. Watkins Rd. campus, connecting people to shelter, services and housing resources.
- One-time rent, mortgage and utility assistance through volunteer-run St. Vincent de Paul conferences based in neighborhood Catholic churches; each conference is limited to its own geographic area.
- Food boxes and hot meals, including the Mesa Dining Room at 49 W. Broadway Rd. (meals daily 9:30–11:30 a.m.; summer heat relief with cold water, air conditioning and temporary shelter June 1–Sept. 30, 12–4:30 p.m.).
- Shelter and seasonal weather relief across the Valley; medical and dental clinics for people without insurance (adult dental uses a lottery); diabetes classes in English and Spanish; pet services (veterinary care, food and supplies) for people at risk of or experiencing homelessness.
- Help finding the correct neighborhood conference for your address.

Eligibility Requirements

- Call for eligibility screening; required documents depend on the service, and fees vary by service.
- Rent/utility assistance is one-time and restricted to the neighborhood served by each volunteer conference.
- Medical clinic: uninsured or under-insured patients. Dental clinic: uninsured patients; adult dental uses a lottery.
- The coordinated-entry resource list that includes this site focuses on seniors experiencing homelessness.

Population Served

- People in Maricopa County who are experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness; the senior resource list places a coordinated-entry access point at SVdP's campus for seniors.
- SVdP describes its wider programs as serving older adults, adults living with disabilities, veterans and work-ready adults, plus families (the family meal program requires children under 18).

How to Best Connect

- Call 602-266-4673 (602-266-HOPE); email info@svdpaz.org. Campus: 420 W. Watkins Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85003.
- Hours vary by listing: the Main Campus page lists Monday–Saturday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.; the senior coordinated-entry listing lists Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–4 p.m.; 2-1-1 lists Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m. for administration offices. Call before visiting.
- Rent/utility help: find your neighborhood conference at stvincentdepaul.net (Get Help) or call 2-1-1; if no conference serves your area, call 602-619-9903.
- Transitional-housing screening (Ozanam Manor, De Paul Manor, Rosalie's Place) runs on a separate line: 602-850-6900, 8 a.m.–3 p.m. For SVdP's year-round overnight shelter at the Phoenix Dining Room, call 602-478-8172 about open beds.
- Mesa Dining Room: 49 W. Broadway Rd., Mesa; 602-619-9411.

Important Information to Know

- Coordinated-entry routing differs by population: Maricopa County's current homeless-services page directs single adults (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 602-229-5155), families (Family Housing Hub, 602-595-8700), veterans (VA Community Resource & Referral Center, 602-248-6040), youth 12–17 (Safe Place, 602-841-5799) and domestic-violence survivors (SAFEDVS, 480-890-3039 or 844-723-3387) to other entry points; SVdP is not on that county list — confirm which entry point fits before going specifically for coordinated entry.
- Help is limited and not guaranteed: conference funds, hours and capacity vary; some services are by appointment or lottery; no place is reserved by calling.

Sources: Get Help — Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Rent and Utility Bill Assistance — Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · St. Vincent de Paul Programs & Services (updated 5.13.26) · Main Campus — Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Mesa Dining Room — Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · Additional Resources — Justa Center (senior coordinated entry points) · Saint Vincent de Paul — Diocese of Phoenix (211 Arizona listing)

Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Ozanam Manor (Interim/Transitional Housing) — usable

Ozanam Manor is a St. Vincent de Paul interim/transitional housing community in Phoenix for men and women experiencing homelessness who are 55 or older or 18+ with a disability, with select beds for veterans. Residents get a bed in a dorm-style community, case management, activities and help moving into permanent housing. Intake starts with a phone screening at 602-850-6900 (8 a.m.–3 p.m.), followed by an in-person interview. No cost if you have no income; otherwise 30% of income.

Kinds of help: Transitional housing; Groups: Seniors, People with disabilities, Veterans

phone: 602-850-6900

address: 320 W. Watkins Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85003

website: https://stvincentdepaul.net/locations/ozanam-manor

hours: Intake/screening calls: 8 a.m.–3 p.m. (602-850-6900).

Services Offered

- Interim/transitional housing at Ozanam Manor, part of St. Vincent de Paul's Interim Housing Communities in Phoenix (alongside De Paul Manor and Rosalie's Place).
- Residents have a bed in a dormitory-style community and can join group activities and life courses; case managers meet one-on-one to work toward independent living and finding an affordable place to live.
- The same intake line screens for SVdP's other transitional sites — De Paul Manor (310 W. Watkins Rd.) and Rosalie's Place (320 W. Watkins Rd.).

Eligibility Requirements

- Adults 55+; adults 18+ living with a disability; adults 18+ working or looking for work (workforce housing); select beds for veterans at Ozanam Manor — veterans should work through the VA.
- A phone pre-screen is followed by a face-to-face interview with a case manager to confirm the program is a good fit.

Population Served

- Men and women experiencing homelessness who are older adults (55+), adults 18+ with a physical or mental disability, or adults 18+ working or looking for work; veterans (select beds).
- SVdP describes its interim housing communities as serving veterans, seniors and adults living with disabilities.

How to Best Connect

- Call 602-850-6900 between 8 a.m. and 3 p.m. for eligibility screening; then an in-person interview if screened in.
- Location: 320 W. Watkins Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85003. Program page: stvincentdepaul.net/locations/ozanam-manor.
- Veterans: work through the VA to enter Ozanam Manor's veteran beds.

Important Information to Know

- Cost: no cost if you have no income; otherwise 30% of income.
- Bed availability and waitlists vary; a pre-screen does not guarantee a bed.
- Age rule: current SVdP materials say 55+; older listings sometimes said 50+ — confirm at screening.
- Ozanam Manor is separate from SVdP's coordinated-entry/charity campus nearby; transitional housing intake runs on its own phone line (602-850-6900).

Sources: Ozanam Manor — Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Shelter and Weather Relief — Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Shelter and Transitional Housing — Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · St. Vincent de Paul Programs & Services (updated 5.13.26)

Sojourner Center · Emergency Domestic-Violence Shelter and 24/7 Hotline — usable

Sojourner Center runs one of Maricopa County's largest domestic-violence shelters and a 24/7 hotline. It offers up to 120 days of emergency shelter with 24-hour staff, children's services and pet support, plus transitional housing for up to two years. Call the 24/7 hotline at (602) 244-0089 for safety planning, screening and current availability; the shelter location is confidential.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Domestic violence survivors, Families

phone: (602) 244-0089 (24/7 hotline); (602) 244-0997 (main office)

address: Confidential shelter location, Phoenix; mailing address: P.O. Box 20156, Phoenix, AZ 85036

website: https://www.sojournercenter.org/

email: info@sojournercenter.org

hours: 24/7 hotline; main office phone during business hours (hours not published); shelter location is confidential and access is by phone.

Services Offered

- 24/7 hotline for crisis support, safety planning and direct access to shelter when available.
- Emergency shelter: up to 120 days of services in a secure living area with 24-hour staff support; trauma-informed case management; three meals daily; children's and youth services; a licensed Quality First child development center (birth to age 12); pet companion services (pet daycare and support); support groups and workshops; on-site community partner services (health, behavioral health, housing, workforce); and lay legal support for orders of protection and court.
- Transitional housing: up to two years of supportive services in 32 transitional apartments; referrals by email at TransitionalHousingReferrals@sojournercenter.org.
- Community outreach: mobile case management and safety planning for survivors who are not seeking shelter; the outreach team can be reached at (602) 244-0089 during business hours (in addition to the 24/7 hotline).
- Sojourner says it operates about 30% of Maricopa County's emergency shelter beds for domestic-violence survivors and their children.

Eligibility Requirements

- For people affected by domestic violence, sexual assault or human trafficking, including adults and children; call the hotline for screening and current availability.
- Emergency shelter stays up to 120 days; transitional housing up to 2 years by referral.
- Pets are supported; confirm pet logistics at screening.

Population Served

- Survivors of domestic violence, sexual assault and human trafficking in the Phoenix area (Maricopa County), including adults and children; families with children are served.

How to Best Connect

- Call the 24-hour hotline at (602) 244-0089 — call 911 if in immediate danger. The hotline does screening and can connect directly to shelter when available.
- Main office: (602) 244-0997; info@sojournercenter.org; mail: P.O. Box 20156, Phoenix, AZ 85036. The shelter location is confidential and access is by phone.
- Transitional housing referrals: TransitionalHousingReferrals@sojournercenter.org.
- Other support: National Domestic Violence Hotline 800-799-7233; 2-1-1 Arizona.

Important Information to Know

- Availability varies and waitlists are possible. Maricopa County's current homeless-services page lists SAFEDVS (480-890-3039 or 844-723-3387) as the county domestic-violence crisis line; Sojourner's own 24/7 hotline remains active, and Sojourner says it strengthened hotline capacity when the county centralized line closed (May 2026).
- Name check: some older lists tie 'Autumn House' to this shelter; current public information describes Autumn House as a separate East Valley shelter run by A New Leaf (480-890-3039). If you were told to call 'Autumn House,' confirm the agency.
- No fee is published for shelter; confirm program rules, pet arrangements and child development center enrollment when you call.

Sources: Sojourner Center — Home · Get Help — Sojourner Center · Emergency Shelter Services — Sojourner Center · Transitional Housing Services — Sojourner Center · Community Outreach Services — Sojourner Center · Sojourner Center - When Systems Shift, Safety Must Stay Strong (centralized referral line transition) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · A New Leaf — Autumn House (211 Arizona listing)

Southwest Behavioral & Health Services (SB&H) · HOPWA Housing Programs (HIV/AIDS) — usable

Southwest Behavioral & Health Services (SB&H) is a long-time HOPWA housing provider for people living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal counties. Its HOPWA program provides transitional housing (up to 24 months in leased or agency-owned units) with intensive supportive services and help moving toward permanent housing; clients contribute a portion of income toward rent, and referrals come through Care Directions' Housing Information Services (602-264-2273). Call SB&H at 602-285-4288 or 602-265-8338 to ask about current openings.

Kinds of help: Health-linked housing (respite & HIV/AIDS), Supportive housing; Groups: People living with HIV/AIDS

phone: 602-285-4288 (SB&H information/appointments); 602-265-8338 (SB&H main office); 602-264-2273 (Care Directions HOPWA Housing Information Services)

address: Main office: 3450 N. 3rd St., Phoenix, AZ 85012; HOPWA housing services are arranged by phone

website: https://www.sbhservices.org/services/housing/

hours: No walk-in hours published; call SB&H or Care Directions during business hours. City of Phoenix HOPWA Central Intake: Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.

Services Offered

- HOPWA transitional housing: leased or agency-owned units for up to 24 months with intensive supportive services, aimed at moving residents into permanent housing; residents contribute a portion of their income toward rent.
- Housing case management and support: intake, screening and assessment; individualized housing plans; unit oversight; referrals and follow-up; help with health, income and community resources.
- SB&H has provided HOPWA-funded housing and supportive services since 1995 (per City of Phoenix funding materials) and describes itself as the largest provider of housing and support services for persons with HIV (HOPWA).
- The broader City of Phoenix HOPWA network (accessed through the routes below) also offers permanent supportive housing, permanent independent housing, tenant-based rental assistance through the Section 8 office, short-term rent/mortgage/utility assistance (STRMU), move-in assistance (PHP), housing information/referrals and employment services.

Eligibility Requirements

- At least one household member diagnosed with HIV or AIDS; household income at or below 80% of the Area Median Income; currently residing in Maricopa or Pinal counties.
- STRMU requires that the household is not receiving housing or utility assistance from another housing program.
- Transitional housing residents pay part of their income toward rent; ask about documentation (HIV diagnosis, income) at intake.

Population Served

- Low-income people living with HIV/AIDS and their families in Maricopa and Pinal counties.

How to Best Connect

- Call SB&H: 602-285-4288 (information/appointment line; ask for the HOPWA housing program) or 602-265-8338 (main office). Website: sbhservices.org (Services → Housing).
- Referral route: Care Directions Housing Information Services, 602-264-2273 — the City's HOPWA housing information program, which matches callers to HOPWA housing providers in Maricopa and Pinal counties (1366 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 108, Phoenix, AZ 85014).
- For STRMU/move-in assistance: City of Phoenix Human Services Central Intake, (602) 534-AIDE (2433), option #3; or apply online at phoenix.gov/HOPWA; business hours Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.
- No walk-in hours are published for the housing program; call ahead to confirm.

Important Information to Know

- Phone history: older materials list a 2020-era SB&H HOPWA line at 602-318-1715 (program appointments at 5717 N. 7th St.) that may be dated; the City's current HOPWA Fact Sheet (revised 12.3.2025) lists 480-589-5890 for SB&H's HOPWA Employment Services. Start with SB&H 602-285-4288 / 602-265-8338 and Care Directions 602-264-2273.
- Availability and waitlists vary; HOPWA eligibility is income- and diagnosis-based; this is not an emergency shelter — if you need shelter tonight, call 2-1-1.
- Help is limited to Maricopa and Pinal counties.
- Costs: transitional housing has an income-based resident contribution; STRMU/move-in help depends on eligibility and available funding.

Step Into Housing — City of Tempe / Tempe Community Action Agency shelter-to-housing pathway — usable

Step Into Housing is a City of Tempe shelter-to-housing pathway run with Tempe Community Action Agency (TCAA): people staying in TCAA's I-HELP shelter use a 90-day stay to become document- and housing-ready, then move into available non-congregate shelter (bridge shelter) aiming for a housing resolution within 30–90 days. It is not a separate walk-in program — enrollment works through TCAA's I-HELP shelter intake, (480) 590-5514 (or (480) 389-1277 to check availability), or the City's CARE & HOPE Line, 480-350-8004.

Kinds of help: Transitional housing; Groups: Single adults

phone: (480) 590-5514 (I-HELP intake); (480) 389-1277 (I-HELP hotline)

address: TCAA services site: 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe, AZ 85281 (in-person intakes 8–10 a.m. weekdays; call first)

website: https://tempeaction.org/get-help/housing-stability/

hours: In-person I-HELP intakes 8–10 a.m. Mon–Fri at the services site (call first); CARE & HOPE Line 24/7

Services Offered

- A coordinated shelter-to-housing pathway: case management at TCAA's I-HELP shelter helps guests get documents and become housing-ready during a 90-day stay, then moves them into available non-congregate shelter (bridge shelter) with a goal of a housing resolution within 30–90 days.
- I-HELP itself provides overnight shelter at rotating faith-community sites, meals, assessments, service plans, ID/benefit help and housing navigation.

Eligibility Requirements

- People experiencing homelessness in Tempe who are in (or entering) TCAA's I-HELP shelter and the city shelter system; screening happens through shelter intake, and participants work with a case manager on a housing plan.

Population Served

- Adults experiencing homelessness in Tempe (I-HELP is an adult shelter program; about 40 adults are hosted nightly).

How to Best Connect

- Start with TCAA about I-HELP: call (480) 590-5514 to schedule an intake appointment, or (480) 389-1277 to check availability. In-person intakes run 8–10 a.m. Monday–Friday at TCAA's services site, 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe (listed as the Oasis Drop-In Center in some older city guides — call first). TCAA's front desk is (480) 422-8922 ext. 5882 as a fallback.
- Or call the City's CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 (24/7) or email HOPE@tempe.gov, and ask specifically about the Step Into Housing pathway.

Important Information to Know

- This is a pilot-style pathway inside the shelter system, not a separate walk-in service — confirm with TCAA or the City that it is currently operating and that space is available before counting on it.
- The city renewed its agreement with TCAA for the Step Into Housing pilot (January 2024); the first nine months reported 22 of 23 participants (95.7%) housed. Current 2026 capacity is not published and regional shelter space is limited.
- Phone numbers differ across TCAA's own pages ((480) 590-5514 vs. (480) 389-1277) — if one does not answer, try the other.
- TCAA is building an expanded shelter campus on Apache Blvd. (planned around Fall 2026, 80 beds) — ask where intake currently happens.
- No fees and no separate application form — access is through shelter intake.

Sue's Espacio bridge shelter — City of Tempe (Mercy House case management) — usable

Sue's Espacio is the City of Tempe's city-owned bridge shelter — 40 private, motel-style rooms (formerly the Rodeway Inn) at 2101 E. Apache Blvd. for individuals, couples and families experiencing homelessness in Tempe. Guests stay short-term (typically about 30 days) while case managers help them move to longer-term shelter or permanent housing, employment (Tempe Works), health care and veterans services. Pets are allowed. Admission is by referral through the City's HOPE team and partner agencies: call the 24/7 CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 or email HOPE@tempe.gov.

Kinds of help: Transitional housing; Groups: Families, Single adults

phone: 480-350-8004

address: 2101 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe, AZ 85281

website: https://www.tempe.gov/government/community-health-and-human-services/housing-services/ending-homelessness/housing-and-shelter

email: HOPE@tempe.gov

hours: Shelter operates 24/7; CARE & HOPE Line answered 24/7

Services Offered

- Non-congregate bridge shelter: private motel-style rooms (full bathroom, locking storage/wardrobe, mini-fridge; rooms can be set up for double occupancy), on-site laundry and a small dog park — one of the few Tempe shelters that allows pets.
- On-site case management (with partner Mercy House Living Centers) to move residents into longer-term shelter or permanent housing; help connecting to employment (Tempe Works), health care and veterans services; occasional hot meals, food boxes and hygiene supplies from community partners.

Eligibility Requirements

- For people experiencing homelessness in Tempe. Admission is low-barrier: guests are not denied shelter because of sobriety, mental-health treatment or low/no income. All guests enter by referral through the City's identified referral partners — it is not a walk-up shelter.

Population Served

- Individuals, couples and families (including many families with children) experiencing homelessness in Tempe, including people with chronic homelessness, mental or physical health needs, or substance-use issues.

How to Best Connect

- Call the City of Tempe CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 (answered 24/7) or email HOPE@tempe.gov. The HOPE homeless outreach team does assessments and places people into the bridge shelter; people have been placed shortly after meeting the team.
- HOPE's office is in the Tempe Public Library building at 3500 S. Rural Road (call or email before visiting — the team is often out in the field). If you already work with a Tempe provider (for example TCAA), ask them to refer you.

Important Information to Know

- This is a bridge shelter, not permanent housing: stays are short — typically about 30 days — while residents work on housing plans.
- Rooms and vacancies vary; placement depends on a HOPE assessment and current capacity.
- Pets are generally allowed and there is a small dog park on site, but city discussions have tied pet accommodation to guests actively working toward housing within their stay — confirm pet details when you call.
- The city also bought a second motel — the former Apache Inn (about 58 rooms) near Apache Blvd. and McClintock Dr. — to add bridge-shelter capacity; ask HOPE whether it is open and accepting referrals now.
