Tempe Community Action Agency (TCAA) – I-HELP Emergency Shelter — usable

Rotating overnight emergency shelter for adults experiencing homelessness (men and women), run by Tempe Community Action Agency with faith-community host sites; about 40 spaces with meals, showers, laundry and case management; intakes take place weekday mornings and Mesa's emergency-solutions funding supports this shelter.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Homeless

phone: (480) 389-1277 (TCAA I-HELP shelter line); (480) 590-5514 (I-HELP intake scheduling); (480) 422-8922, ext. 5882 (services-site front desk)

address: 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe, AZ 85281 (intake / services site)

website: https://tempeaction.org/get-help/housing-stability/

hours: I-HELP intake: weekday mornings at 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe — TCAA lists intakes 8–10 a.m.; a 211 record updated April 2026 describes an 8 a.m. first-come waitlist with intakes 10 a.m.–2 p.m.; call first to confirm.

Services Offered

Overnight emergency shelter for adults experiencing homelessness, operated by Tempe Community Action Agency (TCAA) through a rotating network of faith-community host sites in Tempe. Participants receive a safe sleeping space each night, evening meals, shower and laundry services, and intensive case management and resource navigation aimed at moving from the street to shelter to housing. Daytime resources at TCAA's services site include computers and phones, employment help through TCAA's Financial Success Center, emergency to-go food bags and a hydration station. The shelter serves up to about 40 adults at a time.

Eligibility Requirements

Adults (18 and older) who are experiencing homelessness — men and women. Intakes are handled in person on weekday mornings at 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe; call first to confirm the current intake window and process. A City of Mesa resource list notes that I-HELP does not accept individuals with sex-offender status. Whether the Mesa funding contract gives priority to particular households (for example seniors or couples) could not be confirmed — ask staff what is currently being accepted.

Population Served

Adults experiencing homelessness in Tempe and the East Valley, including men and women. Mesa's emergency-solutions grant funding supports this shelter for FY 2025/26, and City of Mesa and East Valley shelter lists include it among options for people in the area.

How to Best Connect

Call TCAA's I-HELP shelter line at (480) 389-1277 — or the intake-scheduling line at (480) 590-5514 — to check availability, or go to the intake site at 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe, on a weekday morning. Published intake details differ: TCAA's program page lists intakes 8–10 a.m., while a 211 listing updated April 2026 describes arriving at 8 a.m. to get on a first-come waitlist, with intakes between 10 a.m. and 2 p.m. Call first to confirm which process is in effect. Space is limited (about 40 adults) and a bed is not guaranteed; 211 notes that no ID or other documents are required for intake. TCAA's services-site front desk is (480) 422-8922, ext. 5882.

Important Information to Know

There is no fee to stay in I-HELP (211 lists fees as none). Demand is high — TCAA reports more than 1,000 adults and children experiencing homelessness in Tempe at any given time, while I-HELP assists up to about 40 people at a time — so call early and have a backup plan. TCAA's website lists separate I-HELP shelters in Mesa and Chandler run by other organizations (the Mesa program is for adult women), so if you are in Mesa, ask TCAA which program fits your situation. Host-site schedules, intake rules and bed availability can change; treat this listing as a lead to verify by phone, not a guaranteed place.

Sources: TCAA — I-HELP Emergency Shelter page (intakes 8–10 a.m. at 2146 E. Apache Blvd.; I-HELP hotline) · TCAA — Find Help Now overview (I-HELP intake appointments at (480) 590-5514) · TCAA – Contact Us (I-HELP shelter and daytime resources: 480-389-1277; locations) · 211 Arizona – I-HELP Shelter Program – Tempe (eligibility; April 2026 intake process; fees; no-ID note) · City of Mesa — FY 2025/2026 Awarded Contracts and FY 2026/2027 process overview (September 4, 2025) · City of Mesa Police Department — Quick Reference Guide (COPA Health Community Navigation Office)

Tempe Housing Services / Tempe Housing Authority — Section 8 HCV (waitlist closed), HOME TBRA and special-purpose vouchers — usable

Tempe Housing Services (Tempe Housing Authority, AZ031) runs the city's rental-housing programs: Section 8 Housing Choice Vouchers (waitlist currently closed), a HOME-funded Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA) program for households who are homeless or at risk of homelessness in Tempe — including domestic-violence survivors — and special-purpose vouchers (Mainstream for non-elderly adults with disabilities, HUD-VASH for veterans via the VA, and Emergency Housing Vouchers via coordinated entry). Programs prioritize people who live, work or are homeless in Tempe. Call 480-350-8950; office in Suite 202 on the 2nd floor of the Tempe Public Library building, 3500 S. Rural Road.

Kinds of help: Housing vouchers & subsidized rent programs, Rent and deposit help; Groups: Veterans, People with disabilities, Domestic violence survivors

phone: 480-350-8950

address: 3500 S. Rural Road, Suite 202 (2nd floor, Tempe Public Library building), Tempe, AZ 85282

website: https://www.tempe.gov/government/community-health-and-human-services/housing-services

hours: Mon–Fri, 8 a.m.–5 p.m.; phone help 8 a.m.–noon and 1–5 p.m.

Services Offered

- Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher (HCV) rental subsidies — tenants pay about 30% of adjusted income; the waitlist is currently closed (last opening Feb 28–Mar 13, 2023, using a lottery for 3,000 pre-applications).
- HOME Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA HPP): monthly rental help for up to 24 months for households who are homeless or at risk of homelessness in Tempe, including survivors of domestic violence, dating violence, sexual assault or stalking; can cover security deposits (up to two months) paid to the landlord; designed as a bridge until longer-term assistance.
- Special-purpose vouchers: Mainstream (non-elderly adults with disabilities), HUD-VASH (veterans, referred through the VA) and Emergency Housing Vouchers (referred through the region's coordinated entry system); landlord recruitment and housing-search support for voucher holders.

Eligibility Requirements

- HCV: HUD income limits apply (very low income, updated annually); waiting-list preferences have included people who live or work in Tempe, are homeless in Tempe, are elderly or disabled, or were involuntarily displaced. Applications are only accepted when the waitlist is open.
- TBRA: must reside or be homeless in Tempe (or work/be hired to work in Tempe); income at or below 50% of area median income at move-in (assistance may continue until income exceeds 80% AMI); must sign a self-sufficiency agreement; cannot receive another rental subsidy at the same time; a case manager is required to help access the program, and referrals from community organizations or local government programs under agreement with the City of Tempe (including homeless and domestic-violence providers) receive preference.
- Mainstream, VASH and EHV follow their own program rules (disability; veteran status via the VA; coordinated-entry eligibility).

Population Served

- Low-income individuals and families; households experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness in Tempe; domestic-violence survivors; veterans; non-elderly adults with disabilities. Elderly and disabled applicants receive preference.

How to Best Connect

- Call 480-350-8950 (Monday–Friday; phone help 8 a.m.–noon and 1–5 p.m.).
- Visit the office in Suite 202 on the 2nd floor of the Tempe Public Library building, 3500 S. Rural Road, Tempe, AZ 85282 — call before visiting to confirm current hours.
- While the Section 8 waitlist is closed, no application can be submitted — ask to be notified about future openings (openings are announced at least 10 days in advance) and ask about TBRA and referral-based vouchers.
- For TBRA, ask how a case-manager or partner-agency referral can be arranged; for VASH, referrals go through the VA; for EHV, through the Maricopa coordinated entry system.

Important Information to Know

- The Housing Choice Voucher waitlist is closed per current city and 2-1-1 information — there is no open direct application for most Mesa households right now.
- TBRA and special-purpose vouchers are referral-based and limited; availability depends on funding and turnover.
- Tempe programs prioritize people who live, work or are homeless in Tempe, so Mesa households generally need a Tempe connection or a referral through a Tempe-serving agency.
- Applications are free — never pay anyone to apply for a voucher or waitlist. Some older listings show a different downtown address; current listings point to 3500 S. Rural Road — call before visiting.

Sources: City of Tempe Housing Services — program and contact information · City of Tempe — Housing Choice Voucher waitlist is closed; lottery process and phone hours · Tempe Housing Authority Administrative Plan — waiting-list preferences, MOU referral preference for special programs, VASH/Mainstream/EHV policies · Tempe Housing Services — HOME TBRA HPP requirements (homeless/at-risk eligibility, income limits, 24-month term, case manager) · City of Tempe resource guide — Tempe Housing Services location (Tempe Public Library building, 3500 S. Rural Rd., Suite 202), phone and hours · 211 Arizona — Tempe Housing Services Division (waitlist closed; hours; last assured 04/01/2026)

Tempe River Bottom – Resource Village (City of Tempe with nonprofit/agency partners) — needs-resolution

A multi-partner service village that the City of Tempe ran near the south bank of the Salt River bed during its 2022–2023 river-bottom homeless response. It bundled shelter and housing access with medical care, veterans supports, warrant resolution, mobile cooling centers, restrooms, showers, storage for personal belongings and pet services. City materials do not show the village operating after that operation, so treat it as historical and unverified and use Tempe's current CARE & HOPE Line for any present-day need.

Kinds of help: Street outreach & mobile services; Groups: Pet owners

website: https://homeless-solutions.tempe.gov/

resolutionReason: Targeted checks of Tempe's Salt River Bottom response page, the May 2023 River Bottom Update, the Sept. 2022 FAQ and the Oct. 2022 council presentation show the village as part of the concluded 2022–2023 river-bottom operation (site near the south bank; the river bed is a no-trespass area), with no current listing, hours or contact. Whether the village or its pet services still operate could not be confirmed, so the record is retained with a clear warning and the current CARE & HOPE Line as the only supported route; the documented amenity list was restored to include showers.

Services Offered

During Tempe's 2022–2023 Salt River bed response, the Resource Village gave people living in the river bottom a daily place to meet outreach staff and partner agencies in air-conditioned trailers and connect with services. Documented services included shelter and housing access, medical care, veterans supports, Tempe Municipal Court's Warrant Resolution Program, and pet services; documented amenities included mobile cooling centers, restrooms, showers, bottled water, and dedicated storage for personal belongings.

Eligibility Requirements

The village served people experiencing unsheltered homelessness in and around the Tempe river bottom as part of the city's outreach operation; no separate application or eligibility rules were published. For current help, anyone can call Tempe's CARE & HOPE Line about themselves or another person.

Population Served

People experiencing homelessness in the Tempe river-bottom area, including people with pets and veterans; shelter, treatment and family-reconnection options were offered from the site. This was a time-limited response population, not an ongoing program.

How to Best Connect

There is no current phone number, address, hours or intake for the Resource Village; the former site was near the south bank of the Salt River bed, and the river bottom is a no-trespass area. Do not send anyone to the former site. For any current Tempe outreach need — including pet-friendly help — call the City of Tempe CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 (24/7) or email HOPE@tempe.gov. In an emergency, call 911.

Important Information to Know

The Resource Village appears in City of Tempe materials from the 2022–2023 river-bottom operation, which ended with people vacating the area and the city moving into debris removal and long-term maintenance. It could not be confirmed that the village — or any of its services, especially pet services — still operates anywhere in Tempe, and no current city page or listing shows it as an active service. Treat this record as historical and unverified; confirm with the city before relying on it.

Sources: City of Tempe — Salt River Bottom response · City of Tempe — River Bottom Update (May 18, 2023) · City of Tempe — Salt River bed response FAQ (Sept. 29, 2022) · City of Tempe — Joint City Council/ASU presentation (Oct. 25, 2022): Tempe River Bottom – Resource Village

The Haven (Central Arizona Shelter Services) - emergency shelter for adults 55+ — usable

The Haven is an emergency shelter exclusively for adults 55 and older, operated by Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) in the I-17/Northern area of Phoenix (reported street address 8152 N. Black Canyon Hwy). It provides 130 private, non-congregate units (used for single or double occupancy, up to about 170 beds) plus case management, meals, help connecting to medical and behavioral health care, dementia screening, and housing navigation toward permanent housing. Placement has been described as referral-based - call CASS or 211 to ask about current intake.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Seniors, Medically vulnerable

phone: 602-256-6945; TTY 800-367-8939

address: I-17 and Northern (reported street address 8152 N. Black Canyon Hwy), Phoenix, AZ 85051

website: https://www.cassaz.org/services

hours: Emergency shelter operates year-round; no walk-in hours published, and placement has been described as referral-based - call 602-256-6945 (TTY 800-367-8939) or 211 to confirm intake and availability.

Services Offered

- Emergency shelter exclusively for adults 55 and older, with 130 private, non-congregate units (used for single or double occupancy) and age- and accessibility-informed design.
- Wraparound case management, with low reported caseloads (about one case manager per 15 residents).
- Connections to medical care, behavioral health services, and public benefits; dementia/cognitive screening described at check-in, and a memory clinic announced for clients experiencing cognitive decline.
- Nutritious meals served daily, including lunch and dinner; reporting has described a communal kitchen and meals provided with St. Vincent de Paul.
- Specialized housing navigation to help residents secure safe, affordable long-term housing.
- Additional wrap-around supports have included help with legal issues and pet support, including medication for residents' animals.

Eligibility Requirements

- Adults ages 55 and older who are experiencing homelessness; CASS describes a focus on older adults facing medical, mobility, and housing challenges.
- Placement has been described as referral-based (from CASS's downtown single adult shelter, first responders, or other agencies) rather than direct from the street. CASS materials also say its shelters operate at full capacity, so a bed is not guaranteed.
- Detailed intake criteria, documents, and priority rules are not published; call CASS at 602-256-6945 or 211 to confirm eligibility and the current intake process.

Population Served

- Older adults (55+) experiencing homelessness in Phoenix and the greater Maricopa County area.
- Residents commonly have chronic health conditions or mobility limitations; reporting describes COPD, heart disease, and behavioral health needs such as PTSD, and CASS screens for cognitive decline.

How to Best Connect

- Call CASS at 602-256-6945 (TTY 800-367-8939) to ask about The Haven, current referral/intake steps, and availability.
- To check coordinated shelter bed availability, call 211 (211arizona.org); 211 can also explain Maricopa County's coordinated entry process for homeless services.
- Location: I-17 and Northern in Phoenix (reported street address 8152 N. Black Canyon Hwy, Phoenix, AZ 85051; near 27th Avenue and Northern). Call before going so staff can confirm whether arrival is by referral and what the current steps are.

Important Information to Know

- Cost: CASS shelter services are provided at no cost to residents; confirm current details when calling.
- The Haven opened in January 2025 in a renovated former Phoenix Inn (grand opening celebrated Jan. 16, 2025) and is described as Arizona's first emergency shelter designed exclusively for adults 55+.
- Stays are generally around 90 days; CASS has said that is not a hard stop, and extensions have been allowed when medical needs require more time. Confirm current stay limits.
- Capacity figures have been reported differently (130 private units; up to about 170 beds when units are used for double occupancy), and a 2026 financing announcement said the project serves more than 500 people annually. The shelter has been reported full, so availability can change day to day.
- No walk-in hours are published, and placement has been described as referral-based, not direct from the street; call first and ask about the current process.

Sources: CASS — Homeless Services (Single Adult Shelter; Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) · CASS — Get Help (single adult intake; same-day placement subject to availability) · Arizona PBS Horizon - An emergency homeless shelter for seniors (Feb. 10, 2025: 130 units, up to 170 residents, ~90-day stays, dementia screenings) · Banner Health - Community grant helps support shelter for older adults (July 2026) · AZ Big Media - CASS receives $31 million in New Markets Tax Credit allocations (Feb. 2026: up to 170 beds; 500+ served annually; memory clinic) · KTAR - The Haven opens for unhoused seniors in Phoenix (Jan. 2025: 130 units, up to ~170; referral-based placements) · Arizona Republic/azcentral - Arizona's 1st shelter for seniors in Phoenix (Feb. 16, 2025: opened January 2025)

Chandler Salvation Army center offering emergency financial help that can include short-term rent and utility/energy assistance, plus food boxes and a cooling/heat-relief site; help is limited to the Chandler, Gilbert or Sun Lakes area, funding is not guaranteed, and requests are handled by phone message.

This is stabilization help, not shelter: no shelter beds, housing units or motel-voucher program are listed for this location.

Eligibility Requirements

People in need who live in the Chandler, Gilbert or Sun Lakes area and who have not received funding from this Salvation Army location in the past 24 months. Funding is limited and not guaranteed even when you qualify, and an application/screening process applies. Mesa households are outside the documented help area — ask about eligibility before applying.

Population Served

Individuals and families in financial hardship in the Chandler, Gilbert and Sun Lakes communities. Services are available in English and Spanish.

How to Best Connect

Call 480-963-2041 and leave a message about your rent or utility need — this location handles assistance requests by phone message, and staff return calls. You can also visit 85 E. Saragosa St., Chandler, during general hours (Monday–Friday 8:30–11 a.m. and 1–4 p.m.; a separate listing shows service hours of 8:30–11:30 a.m. and 1–4:30 p.m., with food boxes Tuesday–Friday mornings). Call first to confirm you are in the service area and that funds are available.

Important Information to Know

There is no fee to apply. Do not promise a motel or shelter voucher through this location: current Salvation Army and 211 listings show rent/utility assistance, food boxes and heat relief, not shelter or motel vouchers — ask when you call in case programs have changed. Assistance is limited and allocated first-come; even eligible households may be turned away when funds run out. Hours and services can change seasonally — verify by phone before going.

Sources: The Salvation Army – Chandler Corps Community Center (official location page: phone, hours) · 211 Arizona – Rent Payment Assistance, The Salvation Army Chandler Corps Community Center (eligibility, hours, application process, languages)

U.S.VETS Phoenix — veteran housing and supportive services — usable

U.S.VETS Phoenix provides housing and wraparound services for veterans in Maricopa County: transitional housing for veterans experiencing homelessness, on-site permanent supportive housing, and SSVF help (rapid re-housing, rent and security-deposit assistance, eviction prevention) for very low-income veteran families — plus mental health counseling, career services and case management. Veterans seeking housing should contact the outreach team.

Kinds of help: Transitional housing, Supportive housing, Rent and deposit help; Groups: Veterans, Women

phone: 602-717-6682 (main); 602-757-9723 or 602-859-7329 (housing outreach team); 602-441-0612 (Supportive Services for Veteran Families)

address: 3507 N. Central Ave., Suite 200, Phoenix, AZ 85012 (offices); residential campus: M.D. Hawkins Veterans Center, 12027 N. 28th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85029

website: https://usvets.org/locations/phoenix/

hours: Supportive Services for Veteran Families: Monday–Friday 7 a.m.–4 p.m. and by appointment; aftercare: 9 a.m.–5 p.m.; residential campus open 24/7; case management by appointment. Call before visiting — hours vary by program.

Services Offered

Transitional housing for veterans experiencing homelessness with wraparound support (case management, life skills, counseling, career training, VA benefits assistance); on-site permanent supportive housing where veterans can take over the lease; SSVF (Supportive Services for Veteran Families) rapid re-housing and homelessness-prevention help, including rent payments, security deposits, moving costs and transportation; free mental health and substance-use counseling; employment and career services; aftercare case management after a veteran moves into permanent housing. Housing campuses include the M.D. Hawkins Veterans Center (opened 2026, with a dedicated wing for women veterans) and Ashley's Place, a transitional housing program for women veterans.

Eligibility Requirements

Veteran who served in the active military, naval service or Coast Guard with a discharge other than dishonorable; must be homeless or at imminent risk of losing housing; very low income for most services. Permanent housing programming is aimed at veterans who can live independently and are economically self-sufficient. Ashley's Place serves women veterans who experienced military sexual trauma, intimate partner violence or domestic violence during or after service and who can live independently in a shared housing environment; eligibility screening applies. Typical documents: photo ID, DD Form 214 (or letter of service) showing discharge status, recent proof of household income, proof of homelessness (or current lease and eviction notice if housed), and Social Security cards for household members.

Population Served

Veterans in Maricopa County, including women veterans and veteran families. U.S.VETS Phoenix reports it supports more than 300 veterans experiencing homelessness every day and has served more than 10,000 veterans since opening in 2001.

How to Best Connect

For transitional housing, call the outreach team at 602-757-9723 or 602-859-7329. For SSVF and family housing help, call 602-441-0612. Main line: 602-717-6682. Offices: 3507 N. Central Ave., Suite 200, Phoenix, AZ 85012; residential campus: M.D. Hawkins Veterans Center, 12027 N. 28th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85029. Calling, emailing or visiting the office starts the process; veterans working with VA can ask their VA case manager to refer them. Hours: SSVF Monday–Friday 7 a.m.–4 p.m. and by appointment; aftercare 9 a.m.–5 p.m.; residential site open 24/7; case management by appointment.

Important Information to Know

Most services are free; transitional housing fees vary. Program openings, beds and screening outcomes are limited — call ahead to confirm current availability, and do not count on immediate placement. Some programs serve specific groups (Ashley's Place: women veterans; SSVF: very low-income veteran households). Hours and program details differ by service and can change; confirm by phone before traveling.

Sources: U.S.VETS Phoenix, Arizona (housing programs, outreach and SSVF contacts, campus, hours) · 211 Arizona: US VETS – Phoenix (eligibility, required documents, hours, fees, services) · 211 Arizona: Transitional Housing/Shelter — US Vets Phoenix – Ashley's Place (eligibility, documents, fees, hours, languages)

UMOM Family Emergency Shelter (New Day Center) — usable

UMOM's New Day Center family emergency shelter provides safe temporary shelter and intensive support for households with children experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Families are screened through the UMOM-operated Family Housing Hub, the countywide coordinated entry point for families; the campus provides private rooms, meals, case management, employment services, children's programs and help moving to permanent housing.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Families, Spanish, Veterans

phone: 602-275-7852

address: 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008

website: https://www.umom.org/shelter

hours: Family shelter intake is by phone through the Family Housing Hub: call 2-1-1 or 602-595-8700 (a current 211 listing shows Hub office hours Mon 1-6 p.m. and Tue-Fri 10 a.m.-3 p.m.; earlier listings differ, so confirm by phone). UMOM's main line is staffed Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m.; a city resource listing also describes the UMOM campus as open daily 8 a.m.-5 p.m. for resource access, with intake/waitlist handled by phone.

Services Offered

Emergency shelter on the UMOM New Day Center campus for households with children. Families receive private rooms; fresh meals served 7 days a week; one-on-one case management; employment services and job training (including UMOM's Homegrown culinary and warehouse/logistics tracks); help connecting to benefits such as WIC and unemployment; advocacy with property managers, landlords and courts; connections to community resources; help finding permanent housing, including housing vouchers when available; rapid rehousing and permanent affordable/supportive housing pathways after shelter; on-site children's services, including an on-site Boys & Girls Club and a children's health clinic run by Phoenix Children's Hospital; adult health care through partners (Health Care for the Homeless and Circle the City); and daily school bus service that keeps children in their home school.

Eligibility Requirements

Families with dependent children (children under 18) who are experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Families are screened, prioritized and placed on the shelter waitlist through the Family Housing Hub, the single countywide coordinated entry point for families, rather than applying directly at the shelter. Families that include a veteran can also access support through the Family Housing Hub on the UMOM campus.

Population Served

Families with children experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including larger families (a 2022-2024 county-funded expansion targeted families averaging six members) and families with non-traditional heads of household. UMOM reports serving about 75% of families in Maricopa County shelters and sheltering roughly 155 families (about 800 people) on campus each night.

How to Best Connect

Start with the Family Housing Hub: call or text 2-1-1, or call 602-595-8700 (also 877-211-8661); online at fhhub.org. UMOM asks families to call 2-1-1 or visit 211arizona.org before going in person so they are matched with the best options, which may include help to avoid shelter. The Family Housing Hub is at 3307 E. Van Buren St. #108, Phoenix, AZ 85008, next to the UMOM campus at 3333 E. Van Buren St.; intake is handled by phone and English/Spanish are available there. UMOM's main line is 602-275-7852. Confirm current hours and any in-person arrangements before sending a family.

Important Information to Know

No fees are charged (211 lists no fees for the shelter or the Hub). Placement is not immediate: UMOM manages the single countywide family shelter waitlist and reported in mid-2026 that more than 200 families were experiencing homelessness and in need of shelter; capacity and waitlist status change daily. Length of stay varies - recent UMOM participant materials describe a short-term emergency shelter stay (30 days) focused on diversion and housing search, while older materials cite stays of up to 120 days - so confirm current terms at intake. Shelter is a step toward permanent housing; a 2024 county case study reported 802 individuals moved into permanent housing from the program that year.

Sources: UMOM New Day Centers - homepage (shelter and service counts) · UMOM New Day Centers - Emergency Shelter (family program and services) · UMOM New Day Centers - Jobs & Supportive Services (school bus, Boys & Girls Club, workforce) · UMOM New Day Centers — Find Help (Family Housing Hub single point of entry; call 2-1-1 before visiting) · 211 Arizona: Housing Related Coordinated Entry — UMOM Family Housing Hub (eligibility, hours, application process, fees) · 211 Arizona (Spanish) - UMOM Family Emergency Shelter (Family Housing Hub referral required; languages English/Spanish) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · Maricopa County case study - Emergency Shelter Expansion for Families (UMOM New Day Center) · Arizona Housing Coalition - Homelessness (Family Housing Hub intake by phone; online option) · UMOM - Short-Term Emergency Shelter Participant Handbook (30-day family shelter) · SRP district meeting packet, June 2026 - UMOM Family Emergency Shelter Program (waitlist context: more than 200 families)

UMOM Halle Women's Center — usable

UMOM's Halle Women's Center is an emergency shelter in Phoenix for single women age 18 and older who are experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including women fleeing domestic violence. Women call the intake line to get onto the shelter waiting list, then receive meals, case management, employment support and help finding permanent housing.

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Women, Domestic violence survivors

phone: 602-362-5833

address: 3424 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008

website: https://www.umom.org/find-help/

hours: Call 602-362-5833 for intake and the waiting list. Reported intake hours 6 a.m.-2:30 p.m.; administration Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m.; the shelter operates 24/7 (per a current 211 listing, last assured 04/01/2026).

Services Offered

Emergency shelter specifically for single women, with rooms shared by two or three women and private bathrooms. Residents receive fresh meals served daily; one-on-one case management; employment services to help secure and keep a job; advocacy with property managers, landlords, courts and partner agencies; connections to community resources; and help with the search for permanent housing. The center serves up to about 130 women each night and is designed for single women without children.

Eligibility Requirements

Single women age 18 and older who are experiencing homelessness; no children in their care for this program (women with children go through the Family Housing Hub instead). Victims of domestic violence are explicitly included, but the shelter is not limited to domestic-violence survivors. There is always a waiting list for shelter.

Population Served

Single adult women experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including survivors of domestic violence. UMOM describes Halle as the first shelter in Arizona designed specifically for single women.

How to Best Connect

Call the Halle Women's Center intake line at 602-362-5833 to get onto the waiting list for shelter. Reported intake hours are 6 a.m.-2:30 p.m.; one city shelter list advises calling at 6:30 a.m. on weekdays for the intake prescreen - confirm the current intake window by phone. Administration is Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m.; the shelter operates 24/7. Location: 3424 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008, across the street from the UMOM main campus (3333 E. Van Buren St.). UMOM's main line is 602-275-7852; program information is at umom.org/find-help.

Important Information to Know

No fees are charged (211 lists none). A bed is not guaranteed: there is always a waiting list and availability changes daily. A 211 listing marks the exact shelter location as confidential even though UMOM publishes the street address - call before going. For women who need a confidential domestic-violence placement, UMOM currently lists DVSTOP (A New Leaf) at 480-890-3039 / 844-SAFE-DVS, described as short-term emergency placement when Maricopa County domestic-violence shelters are full. There is a conflict to be aware of: Maricopa County's and the Continuum of Care's pages still list that number as the 24-hour 'SAFEDVS' hotline, while 2026 reporting says the former countywide SAFEDVS centralized line stopped full service on May 15, 2026 and now directs callers to shelters directly - confirm what the line currently handles when you call. Additional 24-hour routes: National Domestic Violence Hotline 800-799-7233; ArizonaSurvivors.org lists current Maricopa shelter options. Women with children should be routed through the Family Housing Hub instead.

Sources: UMOM New Day Centers — Find Help (Family Housing Hub single point of entry; call 2-1-1 before visiting) · UMOM New Day Centers - Emergency Shelter (family program and services) · 211 Arizona - UMOM Halle Women's Center (eligibility, hours, fees, waiting list; last assured 04/01/2026) · Arizona Housing Coalition - Homelessness (Family Housing Hub intake by phone; online option) · UMOM press release, December 2025 - nightly shelter counts (130 single women) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · ABC15 - Domestic violence hotline ending full service in Maricopa County (SafeDVS transition) · City of Tolleson - Shelters (Halle intake timing note: call 6:30 a.m. weekdays)

UMOM New Day Centers / Helping Hands Housing Services — Permanent Affordable Housing Communities — usable

UMOM New Day Centers builds and operates permanent, income-restricted apartment communities around the Valley through its subsidiary, Helping Hands Housing Services (founded 1993, merged into UMOM in 2009). The communities are managed by Biltmore Properties, Inc., and applications go directly to the property — not through UMOM's shelter or crisis intake.

Kinds of help: Affordable housing communities, Supportive housing; Groups: Families, Seniors, Veterans

phone: 602-275-7852

address: 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008 (program offices; communities in Phoenix and Glendale)

website: https://www.umom.org/affordable-housing

hours: UMOM administration listed Mon–Fri 8 a.m.–5 p.m.; leasing hours vary by community — call ahead

Services Offered

UMOM New Day Centers builds and operates permanent, income-restricted apartment communities around the Valley through its subsidiary, Helping Hands Housing Services. Current communities include:
- La Esperanza Terrace — 3101 W. McDowell Rd., Phoenix; 96 units (1–3 bedrooms); families at or below 60% of Area Median Income; opened April 2025; community room, computer lab, fitness center, playground; computer training, financial literacy and job-seeking support for residents.
- Salamanca Square — 4439 N. 39th Ave., Phoenix; under development (groundbreaking in 2026); planned at roughly 192 units (1–4 bedrooms) for families at 50–60% AMI; not leasing yet.
- 9 South — 60 units (2–3 bedrooms), opened 2023.
- Newsom Village — 60 units (2–3 bedrooms) in south Phoenix, opened 2022.
- Bethany Crossing — Glendale; 72 family units, opened 2021.
- 19 West — north Phoenix; 54 family units, opened 2019.
- South 7th Village — south Phoenix; community for people 55+, with priority given to veterans.
- Legacy Crossing — 80 permanent affordable units across from UMOM's main campus.
- Parsons Village — 48 units of affordable and permanent supportive housing, plus employment services, a computer lab and life-skills classes.
Many communities offer on-site resident services (job readiness, financial coaching, computer classes, English-language and life-skills support). UMOM states it aims to open one new affordable community per year.

Eligibility Requirements

- Income limits apply at every community (commonly at or below 60% of Area Median Income; newer projects at 50–60% AMI); standard rental screening (income verification, background/credit checks) is handled by the property manager.
- South 7th Village is for people 55+ (veterans priority); other communities primarily serve families and individuals.
- UMOM shelter or program participants may apply, but they receive no preference, and UMOM does not subsidize rent at its own properties.

Population Served

Low-income families and individuals, older adults 55+ (South 7th Village, with veterans priority), and people moving out of homelessness into permanent supportive housing (Parsons Village). Communities are in Maricopa County (Phoenix and Glendale).

How to Best Connect

- Apply to a specific community: all UMOM affordable communities are managed by Biltmore Properties, Inc. (main office: 2330 W. Mission Ln., Suite 10, Phoenix, AZ 85021; 602-997-0013, weekday hours). Leasing hours and waitlists vary by property — call ahead.
- Program questions: UMOM, 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008; 602-275-7852; umom.org/affordable-housing; listed office hours Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.
- If you are a family in an immediate housing crisis, call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 or 2-1-1 — the affordable communities are not crisis or shelter intake.

Important Information to Know

- These are permanent apartments, not emergency shelter; demand is high and waiting lists are common.
- Rents are income-restricted but not published (one project announced rents of roughly $828–$1,600 depending on unit size and income while it was under construction — confirm current figures).
- Applications go to the property manager; UMOM clients receive no preferential treatment.
- UMOM states it has built hundreds of Valley units to date (its site prints "over 1,00 units" — treat the exact total as approximate).
- Key links: umom.org/affordable-housing; umom.org/find-help; Maricopa County release on La Esperanza Terrace (maricopa.gov).

Sources: UMOM New Day Centers — Permanent Affordable Housing (communities, AMI limits, Biltmore Properties management, application notes) · UMOM Find Help — family housing crisis routes (Family Housing Hub, 2-1-1) · Maricopa County — La Esperanza Terrace grand opening (96 units, at or below 60% AMI, announced rent range) · Arizona Department of Real Estate — Biltmore Properties, Inc. license and contact (2330 W. Mission Ln., Suite 10, Phoenix)

UMOM Summer Family Heat Relief — heat respite and hotel stays for families (City of Phoenix partnership) — usable

A seasonal, referral-based heat-relief program run by UMOM with the City of Phoenix for families with minor children experiencing homelessness: daytime and overnight shelter (including indoor tent and cot space) on UMOM's Phoenix campus, plus hotel stays used as overflow when respite space is full. Families get help through the family homeless-services system — call 2-1-1 or the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700).

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Families

phone: 602-275-7852 (UMOM main); 2-1-1 for family housing and shelter referrals; 602-595-8700 (Family Housing Hub)

address: UMOM campus, 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008 (access by referral; not a public walk-in intake)

website: https://www.umom.org/

hours: Seasonal heat-season program; the 2026 City of Phoenix contract runs through October 31, 2026, and UMOM reported extending heat relief through the end of October 2026. The heat relief site operated 24/7 (daytime and overnight) during the season. No direct public intake — access is by referral.

Services Offered

Heat-relief respite for families with minor children experiencing homelessness: a 24/7 space with daytime and overnight shelter (including cots in indoor tents — 16 tents were added as the site filled up), meals and staff support, operated on UMOM's Phoenix campus during the extreme-heat season. UMOM also assesses each family for diversion and shelters those who cannot be diverted, and provides hotel stays as overflow when respite capacity is full. By late August 2026, UMOM reported serving 757 people, including 449 children (data as of Aug. 25), with expectations of more than 1,000 people served before the season ended; the city reported more than twice as many families served this year as in all of summer 2025.

Eligibility Requirements

Families with minor children who are experiencing homelessness. Access is by referral — the City of Phoenix contract specifies referrals from other heat relief sites and City of Phoenix outreach workers; there is no public application or walk-up intake for the hotel stays. Families should be assessed through the family homeless-services system (call 2-1-1 or the Family Housing Hub).

Population Served

Families with minor children experiencing homelessness in Phoenix and the surrounding Maricopa County area. UMOM is the region's family coordinated entry provider and the state's largest family shelter provider.

How to Best Connect

There is no direct intake line for this program — referrals come through heat relief sites and City of Phoenix outreach teams, and UMOM's own guidance for families in a housing crisis is to call 2-1-1. Families with children can also call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 for assessment and shelter referral. UMOM main line: 602-275-7852; campus: 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008. Ask specifically about summer heat relief and family shelter availability when you call.

Important Information to Know

This is a seasonal program tied to the heat season, and 2026 availability is time-limited — the City of Phoenix contract runs through October 31, 2026, and UMOM reported extending heat relief through the end of October 2026. Capacity has been under heavy strain: in September 2026, some families with hotel stays were reportedly told to check out earlier than planned — ahead of a reported Sept. 30 end date for the hotel portion — while UMOM looked into the cause, so treat hotel availability as limited and confirm at the time of need. The service is city-funded; no family fees are described in the contract. Outside the heat season, use 2-1-1 and the Family Housing Hub for year-round family shelter and housing help.

Sources: City of Phoenix File #26-1767, Ordinance S-53179 (adopted 9/9/2026): amend UMOM heat-respite contract; hotel vouchers as overflow; term ends Oct 31, 2026 · UMOM: Summer Family Heat Relief program sees surging demand for 24/7 summer heat shelter · UMOM: Collaboration in Crisis (summer heat-relief initiative — daytime and overnight shelter on UMOM's Phoenix campus) · 12News via Yahoo News, Sept 4, 2026: Phoenix family shelter adds tents (16 tents with cots); heat relief extended through end of October; about 200 families waiting via Family Housing Hub · FOX 10 via Yahoo News, Sept 19, 2026: family shelter waitlist of 200+ families described as about three months

UMOM Youth Outreach (ages 18-24) — usable

UMOM's youth outreach team is a coordinated-entry access point for young adults ages 18-24 experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, combining street outreach with a youth resource day center. Young adults can get food, water, showers, hygiene items and clothing, case management, a housing assessment, housing navigation, and connection to HUD housing programs such as rapid rehousing and permanent supportive housing. Call 480-868-7527.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points, Street outreach & mobile services; Groups: Youth, Homeless

phone: 480-868-7527

address: 215 E. University Drive, Tempe, AZ 85281

website: https://www.umom.org/

hours: Reported drop-in/resource hours: Mon-Fri 12-3:30 p.m. (older listings; confirm by phone). The outreach team also works on the street, including evenings.

Services Offered

Free supportive services for young adults ages 18-24 experiencing homelessness: street outreach to youth on the streets; a walk-in resource/day center with food and water, showers, hygiene and personal care items, clothing, and a food box; case management; housing navigation and housing assessments; coordinated entry (a direct access point, not just a directory listing) for HUD housing programs including rapid rehousing and permanent supportive housing; and connections to education, employment and medical care (earlier listings offered free medical visits at the day site through Phoenix Children's Hospital's Healthmobile).

Eligibility Requirements

Young adults ages 18-24 who are experiencing homelessness or housing instability in Maricopa County. Housing programs are accessed by completing a housing assessment (TAY-VI-SPDAT) through coordinated entry; resource-center services are described as free and low-barrier.

Population Served

Young adults ages 18-24 experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including street-based youth; the day center historically also welcomed teens as young as 12 with its outreach services.

How to Best Connect

Call 480-868-7527 to reach UMOM's youth outreach/coordinated-entry line for ages 18-24. The youth access/day center has been listed at Tempe First United Methodist Church, 215 E. University Drive, Tempe, AZ 85281, with reported drop-in hours of Mon-Fri 12-3:30 p.m.; some older listings give the UMOM campus at 3333 E. Van Buren St. in Phoenix. A current community listing also gives 602-819-9361 for the youth (18-24) access point at the Tempe address. Confirm the current location, hours and intake steps by phone before sending a client - the outreach team also meets youth where they are.

Important Information to Know

This program is not currently described on UMOM's main website, so verify by phone before relying on it. Listings conflict on the day-center address and hours; 480-868-7527 is the most consistently published contact. The Maricopa Regional CoC's current Get Help page routes youth 18-24 to a county Youth Resource Guide for access instructions rather than naming this number directly, so confirm current coverage by phone. Services are free; housing program openings depend on the housing assessment and available capacity/waitlists, and nothing is guaranteed. For youth under 18 in crisis, call Safe Place at 602-841-5799.

Sources: Maricopa Association of Governments — Coordinated Entry Points (Adults without Children) · City of Tolleson - Housing Resources (UMOM Youth Outreach under youth 18-24 coordinated entry) · Arizona Housing Coalition - Homelessness (Family Housing Hub intake by phone; online option) · Directory listing describing Open Hands youth shelter under UMOM (older, conflicting attribution; six beds) · Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care - Get Help · City of Mesa Police Department — Quick Reference Guide (COPA Health Community Navigation Office) · Additional Resources / Coordinated Entry Points – Justa Center (lists NREC, M–F 8 a.m.–5 p.m.)

UMOM/Tumbleweed Youth Crisis Shelter and Independent Living Programs (ages 12-17; 16-21) — needs-resolution

A Maricopa County lead for youth shelter and independent living services that were historically operated by UMOM after it absorbed Tumbleweed's youth programs in 2017: a crisis shelter for unaccompanied minors ages 12-17 and independent living programs for youth ages 16-21. The current operator of the attributed programs is unresolved - UMOM's current program pages describe shelter for families and single women, while Child Crisis Arizona adopted the Independent Living Program effective July 1, 2021 and operates Maricopa County's Safe Place youth crisis program. For immediate help for a youth 12-17, call Safe Place at 602-841-5799.

Kinds of help: Children & youth crisis care, Transitional housing; Groups: Youth

phone: 602-275-7852 (UMOM main line); 602-841-5799 (Safe Place, 24/7 youth crisis line)

address: 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008 (last-listed campus site; current youth shelter location unconfirmed)

website: https://www.umom.org/

hours: Not established - the current operator and hours for the youth crisis shelter and independent living services are unresolved. For youth 12-17 in crisis, the Safe Place line 602-841-5799 is available 24/7.

resolutionReason: Consequential unresolved attribution: UMOM's current program materials list shelter for families and single women; child-welfare and youth-service announcements document that the Independent Living Program was adopted by Child Crisis Arizona effective July 1, 2021 (up to about 50 youth ages 16-21 at two Phoenix locations, some beds reserved for Safe Place youth) and that Child Crisis Arizona operates the county's Safe Place youth crisis response - while older UMOM profiles and directories still attribute a 12-17 youth crisis shelter (Open Hands) and the 16-21 program to UMOM, with conflicting bed counts. No current UMOM-operated youth shelter or independent living program could be confirmed, so a referral to a 'UMOM youth shelter' cannot be responsibly made; the Safe Place line (602-841-5799; text SAFE + location to 44357) is the supported live route for a youth 12-17 in immediate crisis, and the historical lead is retained for administrator verification and reconciliation of older listings.

Services Offered

Historically, this included a youth crisis shelter in Maricopa County for unaccompanied minors ages 12-17 (known as Open Hands under UMOM/Tumbleweed), providing shelter, food, counseling, education support and family reunification, with longer stays and life/workforce skills for youth who could not return home; and independent living programs for youth ages 16-21 (including young parents with minor children) providing housing, case management, counseling, life skills and transportation help. It is not confirmed that UMOM still operates these programs: the Independent Living Program was adopted by Child Crisis Arizona effective July 1, 2021 - serving up to about 50 youth ages 16-21 at two Phoenix locations, with several beds reserved for youth who reach the Safe Place program - and Child Crisis Arizona is the documented operator of Maricopa County's Safe Place youth crisis response (ages 12-17).

Eligibility Requirements

As historically described: unaccompanied minor youth ages 12-17 for the crisis shelter (a legal guardian must be located within 24 hours of entering shelter), and youth ages 16-21, including those with minor children, for independent living/transitional programs. Current eligibility must be confirmed with the operating agency before any referral - the independent living program now primarily serves youth in or aging out of foster care (with Safe Place beds included), and Safe Place serves any youth 12-17 who needs help.

Population Served

Youth experiencing homelessness, runaways and youth in family conflict or unsafe situations (ages 12-17), and transition-age youth preparing for independence, including youth aging out of foster care (ages 16-21) - based on the historical program descriptions for these services.

How to Best Connect

For immediate safety and shelter for a youth 12-17 anywhere in Maricopa County, call or text Safe Place: 602-841-5799, or text 'SAFE' plus the youth's location to 4HELP (44357). Safe Place sites include all QuikTrip and Valley Metro locations. To ask whether UMOM still operates any youth crisis shelter or independent living services, call UMOM's main line at 602-275-7852. Child Crisis Arizona (childcrisisaz.org) is the documented current operator of both the former UMOM Independent Living Program and Safe Place. Do not send a youth to the former UMOM/Open Hands youth shelter address without confirming who currently operates it.

Important Information to Know

Current operator unresolved: UMOM's current program pages describe shelter for families and single women, and no youth shelter or independent living program appears among them. In 2021 the Youth Independent Living Program moved from UMOM to Child Crisis Arizona (effective July 1, 2021), and Child Crisis Arizona announced it became the home of Safe Place in Maricopa County (the 12-17 crisis line above); several Child Crisis Arizona beds are reserved for youth who reach Safe Place overnight. Some older profiles and directories still attribute a 12-17 youth crisis shelter (Open Hands; one directory describes a six-bed shelter serving about 200 youth a year) and the 16-21 independent living program to UMOM, and reported bed counts differ (6 vs. 10). Because a misdirected referral could put a minor at risk, treat this as a lead to verify, not a confirmed placement route; the Safe Place line is the supported current entry point for youth 12-17.

Sources: UMOM New Day Centers — Find Help (Family Housing Hub single point of entry; call 2-1-1 before visiting) · UMOM New Day Centers - Emergency Shelter (family program and services) · Child Crisis Arizona — 'Child Crisis Arizona is home to Safe Place' (local program; 24-hour crisis line; Independent Living Program beds; all QuikTrip and Valley Metro locations) · Frontdoors Media - Child Crisis Arizona adopts UMOM Independent Living Program, July 2021 (ages 16-21, up to 50 youth, two Phoenix locations) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155) · Legacy UMOM nonprofit profile still listing a youth crisis shelter and independent living programs (conflicting attribution) · Directory listing describing Open Hands youth shelter under UMOM (older, conflicting attribution; six beds)

VA Community Resource & Referral Center (CRRC) – Phoenix (veteran housing and coordinated entry) — usable

VA one-stop center in Phoenix for veterans who are homeless or at risk of homelessness: housing assessments and coordinated-entry referrals for transitional and permanent housing and eviction prevention, plus health care, employment help, benefits and community referrals; walk-in, no referral required, free of charge.

Kinds of help: Coordinated entry & housing access points; Groups: Veterans, Re-entry

phone: 602-248-6040; 877-424-3838 (24/7 National Call Center for Homeless Veterans)

address: 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014

website: https://www.va.gov/phoenix-health-care/health-services/homeless-veteran-care/

email: VHAPHOCRRCStaff@va.gov

hours: Mon–Fri 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m., excluding federal holidays; walk-in clinic — arrive early for same-day referrals.

Services Offered

A VA one-stop center for veterans who are homeless or at risk of homelessness. Staff complete housing assessments and refer veterans to transitional housing, permanent housing and eviction-prevention programs, and place eligible veterans on the community-wide housing priority list — this is the veterans' coordinated-entry site for the Phoenix area. The center also helps with immediate food and shelter needs, job training and employment referrals, justice-system navigation and community reentry from jail, financial support to prevent homelessness, addiction and depression treatment, and health and dental care. The National Call Center for Homeless Veterans, 877-424-3838 (877-4AID-VET), answers 24/7.

Eligibility Requirements

Veterans who are homeless or at risk of becoming homeless — including veterans transitioning from jail. No referral is required, there is no cost, and you can walk in with or without an appointment. Bring identification and any military/VA paperwork you have; some on-site health services require VA eligibility or enrollment in a VA homeless program.

Population Served

Veterans and veteran families experiencing homelessness or housing instability in Maricopa County, including veterans leaving jail or prison.

How to Best Connect

Walk in or call: 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014; phone 602-248-6040; email VHAPHOCRRCStaff@va.gov. Open Monday–Friday, 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m., closed federal holidays. It is a walk-in clinic, so arrive as early in the day as possible — same-day referrals, including to transitional housing, may not be possible if you arrive late in the afternoon; if that happens, staff can still provide community shelter referrals. After hours, call 877-424-3838.

Important Information to Know

The center is in central Phoenix — plan your transportation from Mesa. No referral is needed to walk in and ask for help, and services are free of charge. A housing assessment is not an immediate placement: referrals depend on program capacity and the community priority list, so timelines vary. Veterans who also need health, mental-health, benefits or employment help can often start those services during the same visit.

Sources: VA Phoenix – Homeless Veteran care / Community Resource and Referral Center (hours, phone, email, services) · VA Phoenix Clinic – Community Resource and Referral Center location (address, hours, free services, walk-in) · 211 Arizona – Phoenix VA Community Resource & Referral Center (coordinated entry, after-hours line) · Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 206 S. 12th Ave., 602-229-5155)

Vista Colina Family Shelter (Central Arizona Shelter Services) — usable

Vista Colina is CASS's emergency family shelter in north Phoenix for parents and children experiencing homelessness. Families get private units and family-centered case management, a food pantry and clothing closet, school enrollment support for children, and housing navigation; access is through Maricopa County's family Coordinated Entry and waitlist (CASS directs families to call the Family Housing Hub at 211 first).

Kinds of help: Emergency shelter; Groups: Families

phone: 602-256-6945; TTY 800-367-8939

address: 1050 W. Mountain View Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85021

website: https://www.cassaz.org/services

hours: Family shelter operates year-round; placement is through Maricopa County family Coordinated Entry and a waitlist - call 211 (ask for the Family Housing Hub) or CASS at 602-256-6945 for current intake steps.

Services Offered

- Emergency shelter for families in north Phoenix: 36 private family units (CASS's current count; December 2024 reporting after the renovation described 30 units - 12 one-bedroom and 18 two-bedroom - housing up to 30 families and 130+ people).
- Individualized, family-centered case management addressing the goals of parents, children, and the household.
- Food pantry and clothing closet; on-site amenities have included a playground, laundry facilities, and common areas.
- Support with school enrollment and transportation so children stay connected to school.
- Housing navigation and landlord engagement toward safe, stable, long-term housing.
- Children's programming focused on education, health, and emotional and behavioral well-being.

Eligibility Requirements

- Families with children who are experiencing homelessness (reporting describes families with children under 18).
- Placement is not immediate: CASS directs families to call the Family Housing Hub at 211 first for assessment and referral to Vista Colina or other family options; after assessment, families are added to a countywide waitlist and shelters contact the next eligible families as units open.
- Confirm current criteria, required documents, and availability with 211 or CASS at 602-256-6945.

Population Served

- Parents and children experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including larger families (earlier reporting described capacity for up to 30 families and 130+ people, including children).

How to Best Connect

- Call 211 first and ask for the Family Housing Hub (Maricopa County's family Coordinated Entry) for assessment and referral to Vista Colina or other family shelter options.
- CASS main line for questions: 602-256-6945 (TTY 800-367-8939).
- Maricopa County's family Coordinated Entry point has historically been the Family Housing Hub at 3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix (602-595-8700; walk-in hours have been listed as Monday through Thursday, 8 a.m. to 4 p.m.); confirm current details with 211, because processes can change.
- Location: 1050 W. Mountain View Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85021 (north Phoenix, near 11th Avenue and Mountain View Road).

Important Information to Know

- Cost: CASS family shelter services are provided at no cost to residents.
- Availability is not guaranteed: family shelter space in Maricopa County is in high demand, families are placed from a countywide waitlist, and reporting has described hundreds of families waiting.
- Stay limits are reported differently: CASS's July 2026 article says emergency family shelter stays are now up to 30 days (raised from 14); a 2026 12News report described Vista Colina as sheltering 36 families at a time in private apartments for up to six months; an older directory listed up to 90 days. Confirm current stay limits with staff.
- Unit counts also differ (36 now listed by CASS vs. 30 after the December 2024 renovation), so confirm current capacity when you call.
- The shelter reopened in December 2024 after renovations, with new children's programming added.

Sources: CASS — Homeless Services (Single Adult Shelter; Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) · CASS — Get Help (single adult intake; same-day placement subject to availability) · CASS - From 14 days to Thirty (July 15, 2026: emergency family stays up to 30 days; countywide waitlist) · KTAR - CASS reopens renovated Phoenix shelter for families (Dec. 24, 2024: 12 one-bedroom + 18 two-bedroom units; up to 30 families/130+ people) · 12News - 'This is solvable': Valley pastor works to solve homelessness (36 families; up to six months; ~800 CASS beds)
